# Mola UI

> A design system cut like a mola: flat layers, hard keylines, revealed bands. React components for dense internal tools and AI-native interfaces. Published as `@demogar/mola-ui`: React 19, Base UI for behaviour, Tailwind v4 for styling, design tokens as CSS custom properties.

65 components and 14 AI components (79 documented pages).

Install and set up:

```bash
npm install @demogar/mola-ui @base-ui/react @fontsource-variable/archivo @fontsource-variable/alegreya @fontsource-variable/martian-mono
```

`@base-ui/react` is a peer dependency: install it beside the package so the app and Mola share one copy. The package is ESM only; `require()` works on Node 22.12 or later.

```tsx
import '@fontsource-variable/archivo/wdth.css'
import '@fontsource-variable/alegreya/wght.css'
import '@fontsource-variable/martian-mono/wdth.css'
import '@demogar/mola-ui/styles.css'
import { Button } from '@demogar/mola-ui'
```

Mount the providers once, at the app root (in Next.js, `app/layout.tsx`):

```tsx
import { DirectionProvider, ToastProvider, TooltipProvider } from '@demogar/mola-ui'

<DirectionProvider direction="ltr">
  <ToastProvider>
    <TooltipProvider>
      <App />
    </TooltipProvider>
  </ToastProvider>
</DirectionProvider>
```

`ToastProvider` is required: `useToast()` throws without it. `DirectionProvider` gives Base UI the keyboard direction; for right-to-left, set `dir="rtl"` on `<html>` and `direction="rtl"` on the provider. `TooltipProvider` is optional.

Theme and density are attributes, not props: `<html data-theme="dark" data-density="compact">`. Leave `data-theme` off to follow the OS; a stored choice is set by an inline `<head>` script before the first paint. `styles.css` includes Tailwind's preflight and styles `body`; in an existing app, import `@demogar/mola-ui/components.css` instead, which scopes the reset to the components. Already on Tailwind v4? Import `@demogar/mola-ui/tailwind.css` into your own build instead of `styles.css`.

## Rules

- Interactive edges use `shadow-cut` (`shadow-floating` on popups, `shadow-overlay` on dialogs and drawers), never `border`. `border-keyline` is for decorative dividers only. Every other edge is a named utility (`rule-b`, `cut-t`, `bar-start-ink`, `cut-anil`, `focus-ring`), never an arbitrary `shadow-[…]` or an `rtl:` twin. A box-shadow edge can grow a revealed band on hover without moving its neighbours; the named edges flip for right-to-left by themselves and keep an edge in forced-colours mode.
- Radius is zero. `rounded-full` only for something that is genuinely a dot. The system is cut cloth: nothing in it is rounded.
- State is never colour alone: glyph, word and colour, in that order of importance. It has to survive colour blindness, a greyscale screenshot and forced colours.
- Disabled is stated, not faded. No `opacity-*` on a disabled control; a disabled control of your own stands on `bg-disabled` with `text-ink-muted` and keeps its keyline. Opacity fades the keyline and the label to about 2:1, a failure axe does not report because disabled controls are exempt.
- Colours come from tokens, each through a utility its scope allows (surface `bg-`, text `text-`, edge `border-`/`decoration-`, mark `bg-`/`fill-`). No hex, rgb or arbitrary colour values outside `tokens.css`. Every token pairing has a measured contrast ratio in both themes; a colour outside the token file, or a token outside its scope (`text-cloth-pale` compiles), was never measured.
- Theme and density are attributes (`data-theme`, `data-density`) on `<html>` or a subtree, not props or React context. Dialogs and menus portal to `<body>` and must inherit them. No component takes a density prop.
- Lay out with logical properties: `ps-`/`pe-`, `ms-`/`me-`, `inset-s-`/`inset-e-`, `text-start`/`text-end`. `dir="rtl"` then mirrors the layout with no second set of classes.
- Typefaces have roles: Archivo (`font-ui`) for the interface, Alegreya (`font-text`) for prose from a person or a model, Martian Mono (`literal`) only for machine literals. A reader can tell from the letterforms whether the product, a person or the machine is speaking.
- Labels are sentence case; buttons start with a verb and name the object (“Cancel run”, not “OK”). A button that names its consequence can be chosen without reading the surrounding text.
- Unknown duration is the relleno working state, never a spinner; elapsed time counts up instead of a progress bar inventing a percentage. Model work has no knowable total, and a bar that stalls at 90% is a lie.
- Anything that writes to the world waits for a person, with the choices given equal weight (`ToolCall` approval, `ChangeReview`). An agent’s action is only as safe as the decision in front of it.
- Use the components for behaviour. Do not re-implement focus trapping, roving focus or typeahead; the components are built on Base UI. A maintained primitive gets keyboard and screen-reader behaviour right in more cases than a local copy.
- Check a component’s lifecycle phase before building on it. Stable is covered by semver, Experimental may change in a minor release, and Deprecated names its replacement: use that instead. A screen built on a component that is about to change or go is built twice.
- Do what each component’s accessibility section leaves to you, usually an accessible name or a label. The component handles roles, focus and announcements. Axe catches a missing name only on the screens it is run on; the component cannot know what its button is for.
- On a product screen, rojo means danger and is used only through the danger role (`bg-danger`, `bg-danger-soft`, `text-ink-danger`, `band-danger`). The accent is gold, by name (`band-accent`). A red label band or bullet beside a red FAILED mark makes the failure one red thing among several (ADR 0008).
- A screen reflows at 320px: only a region meant to scroll sideways (a table, code, a tab or chip row) may, and it carries the `scroll-edge` utility with `useScrollEdge()` as its ref. Table, Tabs and CodeBlock already do. WCAG 1.4.10 is measured at 320px, and a region that scrolls with no cue reads as complete: a table hid its Status column and its only action that way.
- Props share one vocabulary: `tone` is a status (`neutral`, `info`, `success`, `warn`, `danger`, `accent`), `variant` a presentation (`solid`, `outline`, `seam`), `kind` a category (`EmptyState kind="no-results"`), and `size` the t-shirt scale (`xs` to `xl`). A word that means one thing on every component can be guessed instead of looked up, by a person or an agent.
- Every component takes `ref`, `id`, `data-*` and the other DOM props of the element it documents. `className` and `style` style the box you see (the element with the cut); `ref` and the rest go to the control you focus. A wrapper around a label has `wrapperClassName`. A `className` that moved to another element when a `label` was added changed meaning silently.

## Lifecycle phases

Every component has one phase. Stable unless the index says otherwise.

- Experimental: Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.
- Stable: Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.
- Deprecated: It still works and warns in development. It will be removed; move to its replacement.

## Actions

### Button

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-actions-button--overview

A cut-shape button for an action on this page, with IconButton for an action shown as an icon alone.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Starting an action: run, save, delete, cancel.
- Primary for the main action of a panel, secondary for the rest, danger only for destruction, ghost for the least emphasis.
- Work in flight after a press, with loading, which keeps the label and adds the working strip instead of a spinner.
- Icon-only actions in dense rows and toolbars, with IconButton and its required label.

When not to:
- Going to another page or URL. Use LinkButton.
- A row of related actions above a table or canvas, with one tab stop. Use Toolbar.
- More actions on one object than deserve a button each. Use Menu.
- Choosing between views, where the pressed state stays. Use Segmented control.

- Do: Keep one primary button per panel, and keep danger for actions that destroy something.
- Don’t: Use danger red for emphasis, or set several primary buttons side by side.
- Do: Let a disabled button keep its stated look, and say nearby why it is unavailable.
- Don’t: Fade a button with opacity, or disable it with no explanation.

Content:
- Start with a verb and name the object: “Delete run”, “Start run”, not “OK”, “Yes” or “Submit”.
- Write labels in sentence case with no trailing period; the button sets them in capitals itself.
- Give IconButton a label that names the action and the object, “Copy run id”; it is also the tooltip.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the button, including while it is loading.
- Enter or Space: Activates it, unless it is disabled or loading.

Mola handles:
- Renders a native `<button>` through Base UI, so it is announced as a button with its label as the name.
- While `loading`, sets `aria-busy`, stops accepting presses and stays focusable, so focus is not thrown back to the top of the page.
- Hides the leading icon and the arrow from assistive tech, so the label alone is the name.
- Gives `IconButton` its `label` as both the `aria-label` and the native tooltip.

You handle:
- Write a label that names the action with a verb: Deploy, Save changes.
- Give every `IconButton` a `label`; the icon is not a name.
- Use `LinkButton` when the control goes somewhere rather than does something.

#### `Button` (component)

- `variant`: `primary`, `secondary`, `danger`, `ghost` (default `primary`)
- `size`: `sm`, `md`, `lg` (default `md`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the button, merged with `cn()` so yours win a conflict. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute (`data-disabled:`). |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading icon. Decorative: the label is the accessible name. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | Work is in flight. The label stays (so the button never changes width mid-click), a strip of working relleno runs along its foot, and the button stops accepting presses while staying focusable and announced as busy. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | The control height. Density already re-scales every size, so reach for a different one only to set a button apart from its neighbours, not to make a whole screen compact. |
| `variant` | `"primary" \| "secondary" \| "danger" \| "ghost"` |  | The weight of the action: `primary` (ink) for the panel's main action, `secondary` beside it, `danger` only for what destroys something, `ghost` for low-stakes actions in dense rows. |
| `withArrow` | `boolean` | `false` | Trailing arrow that slides 3px on hover — for actions that move you somewhere. |

Also accepts the props of `ButtonProps`, `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="button">`.

#### `ButtonArrow` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<svg data-slot="button-arrow">`.

#### `IconButton` (component)

A square button that carries only an icon. `label` is required and becomes the accessible name and the native tooltip, because an icon-only control with no name is the single most common accessibility failure in tool UIs.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | The accessible name. Required: an icon is not a label. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the button, merged with `cn()` so yours win a conflict. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute (`data-disabled:`). |
| `loading` | `boolean` |  | Work is in flight. The label stays (so the button never changes width mid-click), a strip of working relleno runs along its foot, and the button stops accepting presses while staying focusable and announced as busy. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | The control height. Density already re-scales every size, so reach for a different one only to set a button apart from its neighbours, not to make a whole screen compact. |
| `variant` | `"secondary" \| "danger" \| "primary" \| "ghost"` |  | The weight of the action: `primary` (ink) for the panel's main action, `secondary` beside it, `danger` only for what destroys something, `ghost` for low-stakes actions in dense rows. |

Also accepts the props of `ButtonProps`, `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="button">`.

#### `buttonVariants` (function)

A button is a cut shape, so its edge is a `box-shadow` keyline rather than a `border`. The signature hover reveals a band of the layer beneath the shape, which means the edge has to grow by several pixels without moving anything around it. A border would reflow the toolbar; a shadow does not.

Types: `ButtonArrowProps`, `ButtonProps`, `IconButtonProps`

Used in: Agent console, Agent hand-off, Confirmation, Empty states, Filtered table, Form validation, List and detail, Settings page

## Data display

### Avatar

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-avatar--overview

A person or an agent as a square tile, with initials or a glyph on a colour picked from the name.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Showing who did something in a log, a page header or a list of reviewers.
- Marking an agent with a glyph through icon, so a person and a bot are never confused.
- Several people in one place, with AvatarGroup, which folds the rest into a “+n” tile past max.

When not to:
- Stating a status or a role. Use Badge.
- An image or chart slot that has nothing in it yet. Use Placeholder.

- Do: Pass the full name; it becomes the accessible name and seeds the colour.
- Don’t: Pass initials or a handle as the name; a screen reader then announces “AP”.

Content:
- Name people by full name and agents as people know them: “Support triage”.
- Name an AvatarGroup by role: “Reviewers”, “On call”.

Mola handles:
- Gives the tile `role="img"` and the `name` as its accessible name and tooltip.
- Hides the initials or icon from assistive tech, and gives the image an empty `alt`, so the name is read once.
- Keeps the initials until the image has loaded, so a broken URL never shows a broken-image glyph.
- Makes `AvatarGroup` a named group, and reads the overflow tile as "3 more" rather than "+3".

You handle:
- Pass the full name, not initials, as `name`.
- Give `AvatarGroup` a `label` that says who the people are: Reviewers, On call.
- Wrap the avatar in a link or button yourself if it opens a profile; it is not interactive.

#### `Avatar` (component)

- `size`: `xs`, `sm`, `md`, `lg`, `xl` (default `md`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` (required) | `string` |  | The person's or agent's name. Becomes the accessible name and seeds the colour. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Shown instead of initials when there is no image — e.g. a bot glyph for an agent. |
| `size` | `"xs" \| "sm" \| "md" \| "lg" \| "xl"` |  | The square's side, on the spacing scale so density re-scales it: `md` for a table row or a message, `xs`/`sm` inline with text, `lg`/`xl` for a profile header. |
| `src` | `string` |  | An image URL. The initials stay until it has loaded, and stay for good if it fails, so a broken URL never shows a broken-image glyph. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="avatar">`.

#### `AvatarGroup` (component)

Overlapping tiles, each separated from the next by a ring of the page ground inside its keyline — the gap a blade leaves between two shapes cut from the same layer. Without it, overlapping squares merge into one shape and the count is unreadable.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | Names the group: "Reviewers", "On call". |
| `max` | `number` | `4` | Show at most this many; the rest collapse into a "+n" tile. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<AvatarGroupMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the "+n" tile's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `size` | `"xs" \| "sm" \| "md" \| "lg" \| "xl"` | `md` | Every tile's size, the "+n" tile's included; it overrides the size set on each child, so the row stays even. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="avatar-group">`.

#### `avatarLayer` (function)

#### `avatarVariants` (function)

#### `initials` (function)

Types: `AvatarGroupMessages`, `AvatarGroupProps`, `AvatarLayer`, `AvatarProps`

### Code

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-code--overview

Machine text: Code for an inline literal, CodeBlock for a labelled block with copy and wrap.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- An id, a field name or a flag inside a sentence, with Code.
- A tool’s arguments, a query or a log tail, with CodeBlock; JSON is tokenised by value kind.
- A payload cut off mid-stream; CodeBlock shows exactly what arrived.

When not to:
- Figures compared down a column, such as cost and duration; they are Archivo with tabular figures. Use Meta.
- A keyboard shortcut. Use Kbd.
- A proposed change to a file, shown as additions and removals. Use Change review.
- Labels, headings or emphasis; the monospace is only for text a machine reads. Use Text.

- Do: Use Code for a literal inside a sentence.
- Don’t: Break a sentence with a CodeBlock to show one id.
- Do: Turn copyable off for payloads that must not leave the page.
- Don’t: Put a CodeBlock on a black terminal panel; it sits on the same cloth as everything else.

Content:
- Show literals exactly as the system stores them; do not change their case or add spaces.
- Label a CodeBlock by its role or file name, as the reader would search for it: “arguments”, “query.sql”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the wrap toggle, the copy button, then the code itself.
- Enter or Space: Toggles line wrapping, or copies the code, on the focused button.
- ArrowUp or ArrowDown or ArrowLeft or ArrowRight: Scrolls the code while it has focus.

Mola handles:
- Makes the scrolling `<pre>` focusable and names it by the block label, so the overflow is reachable without a mouse.
- Reports the wrap toggle as pressed or not pressed with `aria-pressed`.
- Announces a copy through a polite status that is always mounted, as well as turning the icon into a check.
- Pins the code body left to right in a right-to-left page, while the header mirrors.

You handle:
- Give the block a `label` that says what the code is: arguments, result, query.sql.
- Set `copyable={false}` for content that must not leave the page.

#### `Code` (component)

An inline machine literal: an id, a field name, a flag.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<code data-slot="code">`.

#### `CodeBlock` (component)

A block of machine text — a tool call's arguments, a query, a log tail.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `code` (required) | `string` |  | The text, exactly as it should be copied. Rendered verbatim; format JSON before passing it. |
| `copyable` | `boolean` | `true` | Hide the copy button — for payloads that must not leave the page. |
| `label` | `string` |  | A filename or a role — "arguments", "result", "query.sql". |
| `language` | `CodeLanguage` | `text` | Shown as a tag in the header. Only `json` is tokenized; everything else is plain. |
| `lineNumbers` | `boolean` | `false` | A gutter of line numbers that stays put as the code scrolls sideways. Hidden from assistive tech and never copied. Off by default; turn it on when someone will cite a line. |
| `maxHeight` | `string` |  | Scroll inside after this height (any CSS length). |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<CodeBlockMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the wrap and copy buttons and the copy announcement. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `wrap` | `boolean` | `false` | Start with long lines wrapped. The reader can toggle it. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<figure data-slot="code-block">`.

#### `tokenizeJson` (function)

#### `JSON_TOKEN_CLASS` (constant)

Types: `CodeBlockMessages`, `CodeBlockProps`, `CodeLanguage`, `JsonToken`, `JsonTokenKind`

Used in: Confirmation

### Description list

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-description-list--overview

Term and value pairs: the properties of a run, a document or an evaluation.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A side panel of properties scanned top to bottom, with the inline layout.
- Values long enough to wrap, such as a system prompt or a path, with the stacked layout.
- A summary that closes a section, with the grid layout.
- Ids, hashes and model names marked literal, so they are never read as prose.

When not to:
- Many records with the same fields, compared row against row. Use Table.
- A few headline figures with their change over time. Use Stat.
- The fields of a record before and after a proposed change. Use FieldChanges.

- Do: Mark ids and model strings literal, and leave prose and figures in the system voice.
- Don’t: Set every value in the monospace.

Content:
- Terms are short nouns in sentence case, without colons: “Model”, “Duration”.
- Give values their units, “41s”, “$0.2185”, and show a status as a Badge rather than a bare word.

Mola handles:
- Renders a real `<dl>` with each `<dt>` and `<dd>` pair grouped in a `<div>`, so terms and values are read together.
- Sets values marked `literal` in the machine face, so an id is not mistaken for prose.

You handle:
- Write terms as short nouns, and keep each value to the fact it names.
- Put a heading above the list; it has no name of its own.

#### `DescriptionList` (component)

- `layout`: `inline`, `stacked`, `grid` (default `inline`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `DescriptionItem[]` |  | The term and value pairs, in reading order. Mark ids and hashes `literal`. |
| `layout` | `"inline" \| "stacked" \| "grid"` | `inline` | `inline` (default): term left, value right, for a side panel scanned top to bottom. `stacked`: term above value, for values long enough to wrap. `grid`: two columns under an ink rule, for a summary closing a section. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<dl data-slot="description-list">`.

#### `descriptionListVariants` (function)

Types: `DescriptionItem`, `DescriptionListProps`

Used in: Filtered table, Form validation, List and detail, Settings page

### Relative time

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-relative-time--overview

A moment as its distance from now, “3 minutes ago” or “in 2 days”, in a time element that keeps the exact moment.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- When something last happened, where recency is the point: the last run, the last sync, an edit.
- A countdown to something scheduled within days, such as the next evaluation, written “in 2 days”.
- Columns and meta lines that need a short form, with format narrow.

When not to:
- An exact moment people compare or act on, such as an audit entry or a deploy window.
- How long something has been running, counting up to the second. Use Run status.
- Events in order, each with its own time. Use Timeline.

- Do: Pass now when server rendering, or let it render after hydration; both keep the server HTML.
- Don’t: Compute “3 minutes ago” on the server and again in the browser; the two disagree and React discards the page.
- Do: Pass timeZone when the exact moment in the title must be in the product’s zone, such as UTC for run logs.
- Don’t: Rely on the title alone for a moment that matters; it is not reachable by keyboard or touch.

Content:
- Lead with the event and let the time follow: “Last sync 3 minutes ago”.
- Use one format down a column; mixing “3m ago” and “3 minutes ago” reads as two kinds of data.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `<time>` whose `dateTime` is the exact moment in ISO 8601, and whose `title` shows it to a pointer.
- Is not a live region, so a time that moves on every minute is never announced; the current text is read when it is reached.
- Takes the words from `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` for the `locale`, so a screen reader hears them in the page’s language.

You handle:
- Show the exact moment in text, not only in `title`, wherever someone must act on it; a title is not reachable by keyboard or touch.
- Say what happened beside it: “Last sync 3 minutes ago”, not “3 minutes ago” alone.

#### `RelativeTime` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `date` (required) | `string \| number \| Date` |  | The moment: a `Date`, epoch milliseconds, or an ISO 8601 string. |
| `format` | `"long" \| "short" \| "narrow"` | `long` | `long` "3 minutes ago", `short` "3 min. ago", `narrow` "3m ago" (in English). |
| `live` | `boolean` | `true` | Keep the text current while the tab is visible. Ignored when `now` is given. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | BCP 47 tag for the words and digits. Left out, `MessagesProvider`'s locale, then the runtime's. |
| `now` | `number \| Date` |  | The moment to measure from. Given, the text renders on the server and does not tick; omitted, it uses the browser's clock from hydration on. |
| `numeric` | `"auto" \| "always"` | `auto` | `auto` writes "yesterday" and "now" where the locale has them; `always` writes "1 day ago". |
| `timeZone` | `string` |  | The zone for the exact moment in `title`, and in the server text when `now` is omitted. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<time data-slot="relative-time">`.

#### `formatRelativeTime` (function)

"3 minutes ago" for `date` seen from `now`. Exported for tables and other places that need the words without the element.

Types: `RelativeTimeProps`

Used in: Confirmation, Empty states, List and detail

### Stat

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-stat--overview

A KPI tile: a label, a figure, a change with its direction and sentiment, and an optional sparkline.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Headline figures at the top of a page, grouped in a StatGroup cut from one panel.
- A change whose direction and meaning differ, such as latency going down being good news.
- The shape of the recent past as a sparkline beside the figure.

When not to:
- A reading against a limit or threshold, such as a quota. Use Meter.
- Many properties of one object. Use Description list.
- A trend that needs axes, values or several series. A sparkline is a glyph; use a chart.

- Do: State direction and sentiment separately, so cost going down reads as good.
- Don’t: Assume up is green; latency going up is a regression.

Content:
- The label names the measure and its window: “Runs today”, “p95 duration”.
- Put the unit in unit, “%”, and the comparison in the delta’s period, “vs yesterday”.
- Group digits and keep only the precision a reader uses: “1,204”, “97.8”.

Mola handles:
- Renders each tile as a `<dt>` and `<dd>` pair, inside one `<dl>` for a `StatGroup` or its own `<dl>` when alone.
- Reads the delta as one sentence, "Up 12%, vs last week, an improvement", and hides the arrow and the separate parts.
- Carries direction in the arrow and sentiment in the colour, so neither depends on colour alone.
- Hides the sparkline from assistive tech, because the figure and the delta already say what it shows.

You handle:
- Set `sentiment` on every delta; the tile does not guess that up is good.
- Give the comparison in `period` so the change has something to be measured against.

Anatomy:
- Tile (`[data-slot="stat"]`): A raised cloth tile with a keyline; inside a Stat group, the group draws it.
- Label (`[data-part="label"]`): What is measured, in the label register.
- Value (`[data-part="value"]`): The figure, in the display face with tabular numerals.
- Trend (`[data-part="trend"]`): A decorative sparkline; the last point is a cut square.
- Delta (`[data-part="delta"]`): Direction as a glyph, sentiment as a colour, and both in a sentence for screen readers.
- Unit (`[data-part="unit"]`): The unit, muted, kept on the figure’s line in either direction.
- Hint (`[data-part="hint"]`): One line of context: a budget, when it was last synced.

#### `Sparkline` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `values` (required) | `number[]` |  | The series, oldest first, scaled to its own minimum and maximum. The latest point is cut out in the accent. Empty draws nothing. Decorative: say the figure in text beside it. |
| `height` | `number` | `24` | Drawn height in pixels, 24 by default. |
| `width` | `number` | `72` | Drawn width in pixels, 72 by default. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<svg data-slot="sparkline">`.

#### `Stat` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | What the figure measures, set in the label register: "p95 latency", "Runs today". It is the tile's term. |
| `value` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The figure itself, already formatted: "1,284", "97.8". Put the unit in `unit` rather than in the string. |
| `delta` | `StatDelta` |  | The change since a reference period. State both which way it moved (`direction`, the glyph) and whether that is good (`sentiment`, the colour): up is not always good news. |
| `hint` | `ReactNode` |  | A line of context under the figure. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<StatMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the change, read as one sentence to a screen reader. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `trend` | `number[]` |  | The recent series, oldest first. Drawn in ink with the latest point cut out in the accent. |
| `unit` | `ReactNode` |  | Set smaller beside the figure: "ms", "runs", "%". |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="stat">`.

#### `StatGroup` (component)

Tiles cut from one panel rather than four floating cards: one ink keyline around the group, decorative keylines between tiles. Four separate cut shapes in a row would put eight ink edges where the eye needs one.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `columns` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5` | `4` | Columns when there is room for them. Tiles wrap down to two, then one. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<dl data-slot="stat-group">`.

Types: `StatMessages`, `SparklineProps`, `StatDelta`, `StatGroupProps`, `StatProps`

Used in: List and detail

### Swatch

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-swatch--overview

A small decorative colour key, square or round, in one of the badge tones, placed before the word it keys.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A legend entry that ties a word to the colour of map pins, chart series or rows.
- A marker before a group heading or a filter label, so it reads in the same colour as what it filters.
- Round only when the thing it keys is itself a dot, such as a map pin.

When not to:
- The state of a row or an object, or a mark that must be announced on its own. Use StatusDot.
- A short aside with a coloured marker before its title. Use Callout.

- Do: Put the word beside every swatch; the swatch is hidden from screen readers.
- Don’t: Let a row of coloured squares stand in for a legend.
- Do: Use tone="accent" when the key must match a gold field exactly.
- Don’t: Paint a swatch with bg-oro by hand; on light cloth it falls below 3:1.

Mola handles:
- Is always `aria-hidden`, so it can never be the only carrier of a meaning.
- Paints itself in the system text colour in forced colours mode, so the key stays visible.

You handle:
- Put the word the colour stands for beside the swatch.

#### `Swatch` (component)

Decorative by contract: a swatch is always hidden from assistive technology, so it can never be the only carrier of a meaning (SC 1.4.1). Put the word beside it.

- `tone`: `neutral`, `info`, `success`, `warn`, `danger`, `accent` (default `neutral`)
- `size`: `sm`, `md` (default `md`)
- `shape`: `square`, `round` (default `square`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `shape` | `"square" \| "round"` |  | `square` (default), or `round` only when the thing keyed is itself a dot, such as a map pin. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md"` |  | `md` (default) beside body text, `sm` beside a label or a small figure. |
| `tone` | `"neutral" \| "info" \| "success" \| "warn" \| "danger" \| "accent"` |  | The colour keyed, in the badge tones so a key and a status for the same thing agree; `neutral` by default. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="swatch">`.

#### `swatchVariants` (function)

A colour key: the small square before a legend entry, a filter chip's label or a flat callout's title, that ties the words to a colour used elsewhere — map pins, a column of urgencies, a chart series.

Types: `SwatchProps`, `SwatchTone`

Changes:
- 0.4.0 (2026-10-13), feature: add a flat variant and a swatch colour key [#20](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/20)

### Table

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-table--overview

Table primitives for rows of records, and DataTable for columns as data with sorting, selection, column visibility, pages, virtual rows, resizing, expansion and a frozen first column.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Comparing many records with the same fields, such as runs, documents or evaluations.
- Sorting, selection, bulk actions and pages on client or server data, with DataTable.
- Thousands of rows a reader scrolls through, with `virtualize`, and a filter beside it, because find-in-page reaches only the rows in view.
- An action table used on a phone, with `stackWhenNarrow`, so each row is a block instead of a row that scrolls sideways.
- A layout DataTable does not cover, such as virtual rows of varying height, with the primitives.

When not to:
- The properties of one record. Use Description list.
- Events where the order is the content. Use Timeline.
- A handful of headline figures. Use Stat.

- Do: Right-align numeric columns, header included, in tabular figures.
- Don’t: Left-align costs and durations, or set them in the monospace.
- Do: Show the no-results empty row with Clear filters when filters hide every row.
- Don’t: Tell someone to create their first run when a filter is hiding the runs they have.
- Do: Page a table by default, and virtualize only one the reader really scrolls through.
- Don’t: Virtualize every table, so find-in-page and printing miss most of every one.
- Do: Freeze a narrow identifying column, such as the run id.
- Don’t: Freeze a wide name column, leaving a phone no room for the rest.

Content:
- Headers are short nouns in sentence case: “Run”, “Started”, “Cost”.
- Show a value that does not exist yet as an em dash, “—”, not as zero.
- Set noun so counts read naturally: “3 runs selected”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the sort buttons, the row checkboxes and the other controls in turn.
- Enter or Space: Sorts by the focused column header.
- Space: Selects or clears the focused row checkbox, or every row on the page from the header box.
- ArrowUp or ArrowDown or ArrowLeft or ArrowRight: Scrolls the table frame when it overflows and has focus.
- Escape: Closes the column menu and returns focus to the Columns button.
- Enter or Space: Expands or collapses the focused row’s details, with `renderExpanded`.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowRight: On a column resize handle, moves the column’s edge by 16px. In right-to-left the edge is on the left, and the arrow pointing away from the column widens it.
- Shift+ArrowLeft or Shift+ArrowRight: On a column resize handle, moves the edge by 64px.
- Home or End: On a column resize handle, makes the column its narrowest or its widest.

Mola handles:
- Puts the scrolling frame in the tab order only when the table overflows it, and then makes it a group named as the table is.
- Sets `aria-sort` on the sorted column only, and `aria-selected` on selected rows.
- Uses native checkboxes with a 24px hit area around the drawn square, and announces the selection count once through a polite status.
- Marks a loading `DataTable` with `aria-busy` and hides its skeleton rows from assistive tech.
- With `virtualize`, sets `aria-rowcount` and `aria-rowindex` so the full count is heard, and keeps the focused row rendered while it is scrolled away.
- Makes each column resize handle a focusable separator with its width as `aria-valuenow`, so it can be found and moved without a pointer.
- Gives each row’s disclosure button `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls` pointing at its detail row while open.
- Keeps table semantics in the stacked phone layout, with explicit roles, and hides the repeated cell labels from assistive tech, which already hears the column header.

You handle:
- Name the table with `label` or `aria-label`, or a `caption`.
- Give `rowLabel` so each row checkbox says which row it selects, and `expandLabel` so each disclosure button says which row it opens.
- Mark figure columns `numeric`, on the header and the cells alike.
- Give a virtual table a filter or search: find-in-page and a screen reader’s browse mode reach only the rows in view.

Anatomy:
- Frame (`[data-slot="table-frame"]`): Scrolls the table in both directions, and joins the tab order when it overflows or has a sticky header.
- Column header (`[data-part="head"]`): The label register on an ink rule; a sort control when sortable.
- Row (`[data-part="row"]`): A row at the density’s row height, with a soft keyline under it.
- Footer (`[data-part="footer"]`): Totals, on a shaded ground under an ink rule.
- Caption (`[data-part="caption"]`): What the table shows, under it, read by screen readers as its name.
- Resize handle (`[data-part="resizer"]`): A focusable separator on a header’s trailing edge, dragged or moved with the arrow keys.
- Detail row (`[data-part="detail"]`): What an expanded row shows, on the shaded ground under it.

#### `Table` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `frameClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the scrolling frame, e.g. `max-h-96`. |
| `framed` | `boolean` | `false` | Bound the table with a keyline on a raised ground — for a table that is the panel. |
| `stickyHeader` | `boolean` | `false` | Keep the header row visible while the body scrolls. Give the frame a height via `frameClassName`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<table data-slot="table">`.

#### `TableBody` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<tbody data-slot="table-body">`.

#### `TableCaption` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<caption data-slot="table-caption">`.

#### `TableCell` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `numeric` | `boolean` | `false` | Right-aligned tabular figures. |
| `truncate` | `boolean` | `false` | Cut long content to one line with an ellipsis. The full text goes in `title` when the content is a string, so it is never lost — only folded. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<td data-slot="table-cell">`.

#### `TableCheckbox` (component)

A native checkbox, drawn as a cut square. Native, so space toggles it, forms submit it and screen readers announce it with no ARIA to keep in sync.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `indeterminate` | `boolean` | `false` | Some but not all rows are selected. A DOM property, not an attribute, so it is set by effect. |

`className` goes to `<span data-slot="table-checkbox">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input>`.

#### `TableEmpty` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the `<tr>`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `colSpan` (required) | `number` |  | How many columns the row spans: all of them, including a checkbox column, or the empty state sits in the first cell. |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` |  | One or two buttons; for a filtered table, the one that clears the filters. |
| `kind` | `"empty" \| "no-results" \| "error" \| "no-permission"` |  | Why it is empty, as on `EmptyState`: `empty` (the default), `no-results` when filters emptied it, `error`, or `no-permission`. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<TableMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default heading. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `title` | `ReactNode` |  | The empty state's heading. Defaults to "Nothing here yet"; the children become its description. |
| `variant` (deprecated) | `"empty" \| "no-results" \| "error" \| "no-permission"` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `kind`, as on `EmptyState`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<tr data-slot="table-empty">`.

#### `TableFooter` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<tfoot data-slot="table-footer">`.

#### `TableHead` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `numeric` | `boolean` | `false` | Right-align, for a column of figures. Header and cells must agree or the label floats off its numbers. |
| `onSort` | `(() => void)` |  | Called when the header's sort control is pressed. The page decides the next direction; only used with `sort`. |
| `resizer` | `ReactNode` |  | A column resize handle, drawn after the label and outside the sort control, so the two are separate tab stops. DataTable passes its own with `resizableColumns`; give the header `relative` (or keep it sticky) so the handle can sit on its trailing edge. |
| `sort` | `TableSortDirection` |  | Makes the header a sort control. `none` renders the control in its idle state; `aria-sort` is only set on the column actually sorted, because announcing "not sorted" on every column is noise. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<th data-slot="table-head">`.

#### `TableHeader` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<thead data-slot="table-header">`.

#### `TableRow` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `selected` | `boolean` | `false` | The row is part of the current selection. Gold, because gold is the layer this system marks a choice with — it is also the text-selection colour — while añil stays free to mean "information". The fill is a wash, not the layer, so every ink in the row still clears AA; and an ink bar on the leading edge carries the state for anyone who cannot see the hue. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<tr data-slot="table-row">`.

#### `TableSkeletonRows` (component)

Loading rows: real rows at the real height, so the table does not jump when the data lands. Bar widths vary by a fixed pattern rather than at random, so a re-render does not make the skeleton twitch.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `columns` (required) | `number` |  | How many cells each row draws: the table's column count, including a checkbox column. |
| `rows` | `number` | `5` | How many rows to draw. |

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `DataTable` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `columns` (required) | `DataTableColumn<Row>[]` |  | What each column shows, how it sorts and aligns, and whether it can be hidden. Order is display order. |
| `getRowId` (required) | `(row: Row) => string` |  | A stable id per row. Selection is kept by id, so it survives sorting, paging and a refetch that returns new objects. |
| `rows` (required) | `Row[]` |  | The data. Every row when the table sorts and pages itself; one page, already sorted, when `manual`. Never mutated. |
| `bulkActions` | `((context: DataTableBulkContext) => ReactNode)` |  | Actions for the selected rows, shown in the bar above the table while any are selected. |
| `canExpand` | `((row: Row) => boolean)` |  | Whether a row has anything to expand. Rows that return `false` get no button. |
| `caption` | `ReactNode` |  | A visible caption naming the table. Prefer it to `label` when the page has room: a visible name helps every reader, not only assistive tech. |
| `columnMenu` | `boolean` | `false` | Adds a "Columns" menu to the bar above the table. |
| `columnWidths` | `Readonly<Record<string, number>>` |  | Controlled column widths in pixels, by column key. Pair with `onColumnWidthsChange`. |
| `defaultColumnWidths` | `Readonly<Record<string, number>>` |  | Column widths in pixels at the start, by key, when the table owns them. |
| `defaultExpanded` | `Iterable<string>` |  | Row ids expanded at the start when the table owns expansion. |
| `defaultHiddenColumns` | `Iterable<string>` |  | Column keys hidden at the start when the table owns visibility. The reader can bring them back from the column menu. |
| `defaultPage` | `number` | `1` | The page the table starts on when it owns paging, 1-based. Defaults to 1. |
| `defaultSelection` | `Iterable<string>` |  | Row ids selected at the start when the table owns selection. |
| `defaultSort` | `DataTableSort` |  | The sort the table starts with when it owns sorting. Clicking a sorted header cycles ascending → descending → off. |
| `empty` | `ReactNode \| DataTableEmpty` |  | What shows when there are no rows: an empty state as data (title, description, kind, actions), or a node that becomes its description. Use `kind: "no- results"` when filters emptied it. |
| `expanded` | `ReadonlySet<string>` |  | Controlled expanded rows, by id. Pair with `onExpandedChange`. |
| `expandLabel` | `((row: Row) => string)` |  | How a row's disclosure button is named, e.g. `(run) => \`Details of ${run.id}\``. |
| `frameClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the scrolling frame around the table. |
| `framed` | `boolean` | `false` | Bounds the table with a keyline on a raised ground, for a table that is the panel rather than sitting in one. |
| `hiddenColumns` | `ReadonlySet<string>` |  | Controlled hidden columns, by key. |
| `label` | `string` |  | Names the table for assistive tech when there is no visible caption. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows skeleton rows at the real row height in place of the body and says "Loading…" in the bar, so the table does not jump when the data lands. |
| `manual` | `boolean` | `false` | Sorting and paging are done by the server: rows are one page, already sorted. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<DataTableMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the counts, the selection names, the empty heading and the column menu. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `noun` | `PluralMessage` |  | What a row is, for counts: `{ one: 'run', other: 'runs' }`. Any plural category a language needs may be given (`few`, `many`); `other` is required. |
| `onColumnWidthsChange` | `((widths: Record<string, number>) => void)` |  | Called with every column's width after a resize — to remember the layout between visits. The first resize fixes each column at its rendered width. |
| `onExpandedChange` | `((expanded: Set<string>) => void)` |  | Called with the new set of expanded row ids. |
| `onHiddenColumnsChange` | `((hidden: Set<string>) => void)` |  | Called with the new set of hidden column keys — to remember the choice between visits. |
| `onPageChange` | `((page: number) => void)` |  | Called with the new page, 1-based. With `manual`, fetch that page here. |
| `onSelectionChange` | `((selection: Set<string>) => void)` |  | Called with the new set of selected ids, across every page. |
| `onSortChange` | `((sort: DataTableSort \| null) => void)` |  | Called with the new sort, or `null` when it is turned off. With `manual`, fetch the re-sorted page here; the table also resets to page 1. |
| `page` | `number` |  | Controlled page, 1-based. |
| `pageSize` | `number` |  | Rows per page. Setting it turns pagination on. |
| `paginationVariant` | `"pages" \| "compact"` | `compact` | `compact` (the default) is previous/next with the range; `pages` numbers the pages, for a table where jumping matters more than stepping. |
| `renderExpanded` | `((row: Row) => ReactNode)` |  | What a row shows when it is expanded. Setting it adds a disclosure button at the start of each row, and a detail row under it while open. |
| `resizableColumns` | `boolean` | `false` | Puts a resize handle on the trailing edge of each column header but the last, which takes the room that is left. Drag it, or focus it and use the arrow keys; in right-to-left the keys follow the edge. |
| `rowCount` | `number` |  | The total across every page, when `manual`. |
| `rowLabel` | `((row: Row) => string)` |  | How a row checkbox is named, e.g. `(run) => \`Select ${run.id}\``. |
| `selectable` | `boolean` | `false` | Adds a checkbox column and a select-all in the header. Select-all covers the current page only, so paging never selects rows nobody has seen. |
| `selection` | `ReadonlySet<string>` |  | Controlled selection. Pair with `onSelectionChange`. |
| `sort` | `DataTableSort \| null` |  | Controlled sort. `null` is "no sort"; leave undefined to let the table own it. |
| `stackWhenNarrow` | `boolean` | `false` | Below a 36rem container, draws each row as a block of label and value pairs instead of a row that scrolls sideways — for action tables used on a phone. The header stays as a bar of the sort and select-all controls. The table keeps its table semantics. |
| `stickyFirstColumn` | `boolean` | `false` | Keeps the first visible column, and the checkbox and disclosure columns before it, in place while the table scrolls sideways, with an ink edge where the frozen part ends. |
| `stickyHeader` | `boolean` | `false` | Keeps the header row visible while the body scrolls. Give the frame a height with `frameClassName`. |
| `virtualize` | `boolean` | `false` | Renders only the rows in view, plus a margin, between two spacer rows, and sets `aria-rowcount` and `aria-rowindex` so assistive tech still hears the full count. Give the frame a height with `frameClassName`. The rows that are not rendered cannot be found with the browser's find-in-page, reached in a screen reader's browse mode or printed, so pair a long virtual table with a filter. Not combined with `stackWhenNarrow`, whose rows vary in height. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the outer element that holds the bar, the table and the pagination. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<table data-slot="table">`.

#### `DataTableColumnMenu` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the "Columns" button.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `columns` (required) | `DataTableColumn<Row>[]` |  | Every column, hidden or not, so the menu can list them all. A column with `hideable: false` shows checked and disabled. |
| `hidden` (required) | `ReadonlySet<string>` |  | The keys of the columns currently hidden. The last visible column cannot be hidden. |
| `onToggle` (required) | `(key: string, visible: boolean) => void` |  | Called with a column's key and whether it should now be visible. The menu stays open, because columns are chosen several at a time. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<DataTableMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the button and the menu's heading. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="button">`.

Types: `DataTableMessages`, `TableMessages`, `TableCellProps`, `TableCheckboxProps`, `TableEmptyProps`, `TableHeadProps`, `TableProps`, `TableRowProps`, `TableSkeletonRowsProps`, `TableSortDirection`, `DataTableBulkContext`, `DataTableColumn`, `DataTableColumnMenuProps`, `DataTableEmpty`, `DataTableProps`, `DataTableSort`, `SortValue`

Used in: Confirmation, Empty states, Filtered table, List and detail

Changes:
- 0.5.0 (2026-10-14), fix: centre the empty row on the visible frame, not the whole table [#39](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/39)
- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

### Timeline

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-data-display-timeline--overview

Timeline for events already written down, on a spine; StepList for ordered steps a person follows.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- An audit log, a deploy history or the stages of an evaluation, with Timeline.
- A runbook or a checklist, with StepList and its ruled bands.
- The event happening now, with the active tone and its working relleno.

When not to:
- A live agent run whose steps change as it works. Use Agent run.
- Records compared by their fields rather than read in order. Use Table.
- Sections of one document that open and close. Use Accordion.
- A task people do in the product, step by step, with checks between steps. Use Stepper.

- Do: Put when or where in meta, and what happened in the title.
- Don’t: Leave the node colour to say what happened; the words have to carry it.

Content:
- Timeline titles state the event in the past tense: “Review approved”, “Rollout paused”.
- StepList titles are instructions: “Freeze the agent version”.
- Use one time format down the list: “Oct 1 · 09:12”.

Mola handles:
- Renders an `<ol>` named by `label`, so a screen reader says where an event sits in the sequence.
- Hides the spine and the toned nodes from assistive tech; the title and meta carry what happened.
- In `StepList`, hides positional numerals, which the list already announces, and reads authored labels.

You handle:
- Give the timeline a `label`.
- Say what happened in each title, because the node colour is not read.
- Set `titleAs` to a heading level that fits the page outline.

#### `StepList` (component)

Ruled, ordered steps — a runbook, an onboarding checklist, the stages of a rollout. Where `Timeline` is a spine with a node per event, this is a stack of ruled bands with a label column, and merging the two would mean a component whose every rule is conditional.

- `inverse`: `false`, `true`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `StepListItem[]` |  | The steps, in order. An item without a `label` is numbered by position; one authored label widens the column for every row. |
| `inverse` | `false \| true` |  | Set the list on an ink panel. |
| `titleAs` | `"h3" \| "h4" \| "p"` | `h3` | The element each step title renders as, `h3` by default: a runbook's steps are its outline. Use `p` when they are not. |
| `tone` (deprecated) | `"default" \| "inverse"` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `inverse`: `tone` is a status everywhere else, and this was a ground. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<ol data-slot="step-list">`.

#### `Timeline` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the `<ol>`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `TimelineItem[]` |  | The events, in the order they happened, one node on the spine each. A `tone` on an item states its outcome. |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | Names the list, since the spine carries no heading of its own. |
| `gap` | `"spaced" \| "flush"` | `spaced` | `spaced`   separate events, a gap between rows. `flush`    one continuous journey, carried by the connector alone. |
| `size` (deprecated) | `TimelineVariant` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `variant`, which takes the same values: a type scale is a presentation, and `size` is the t-shirt scale everywhere else. |
| `titleAs` | `"h3" \| "h4" \| "p"` | `p` | The element each title renders as. Defaults to a non-heading, since most logs are not outlines. |
| `variant` | `TimelineVariant` |  | `default`   product scale: semibold titles at body size. `editorial` the travel-guide scale it was born at, for a narrative. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<ol data-slot="timeline">`.

Types: `StepListItem`, `StepListProps`, `TimelineItem`, `TimelineProps`, `TimelineTone`, `TimelineVariant`

## Feedback

### Badge

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-badge--overview

A small status mark in one of the system’s tones; StatusDot for a square dot beside a label; RecommendationBadge for an editorial scale.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- The state of a row or an object: queued, live, failed, pinned.
- Soft by default; solid for the one state on a row that must be seen first; outline for categories.
- A state that is live right now, with pulse, which stills under reduced motion.

When not to:
- The state of a run, a step or a tool call; model work has its own vocabulary. Use Run status.
- A message with a sentence and an action. Use Callout.
- A filter a person turns on and off. Use Chip.
- A count on a tab; tabs and navigation items take count themselves. Use Tabs.

- Do: Give every badge a word, and every StatusDot a label beside it or its own label.
- Don’t: Let a coloured dot alone carry a state.
- Do: Use soft badges down a column.
- Don’t: Fill a column with solid badges; fifty solid statuses read as a quilt.
- Do: Map a stopped or cancelled state to neutral; a person chose it.
- Don’t: Show a cancellation in gold; warn is for work that is degraded or waiting on someone.

Content:
- One or two words in sentence case, with no punctuation: “Succeeded”, “Needs review”.
- Use the same status words as the filters and headers around it.

Mola handles:
- Hides `StatusDot` from assistive tech when it sits beside a word, and gives it `role="img"` and a name when `label` is set.
- Cuts each tone as its own shape — hollow, striped, filled, half, crossed, diamond — so a state never rests on colour alone, even on a solid badge.
- Stills the pulse under reduced motion, leaving the dot in place.
- Keeps dots visible in forced colours mode by painting their shapes in the system colours.

You handle:
- Put the state in words inside the badge: Failed, Running; pass `dot` so the word has its shape beside it.
- Give a `StatusDot` that stands alone a `label`.

#### `Badge` (component)

- `tone`: `neutral`, `info`, `success`, `warn`, `danger`, `accent` (default `neutral`)
- `variant`: `solid`, `soft`, `outline` (default `soft`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `dot` | `boolean` | `false` | A leading StatusDot in the badge's tone. |
| `pulse` | `boolean` | `false` | Pulse the dot — for a state that is live right now (running, streaming). |
| `tone` | `"neutral" \| "info" \| "success" \| "warn" \| "danger" \| "accent"` |  | The status the badge states, as one of the four layers. Say the state in the label too: the tone colours it, the words carry it. |
| `variant` | `"solid" \| "soft" \| "outline"` |  | How loud the claim is: `soft` (default) for a column of statuses, `solid` for the one state that must be seen from across the room, `outline` for categorical metadata. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="badge">`.

#### `RecommendationBadge` (component)

- `level`: `essential`, `highly`, `detour`, `time` (default `essential`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `"essential" \| "highly" \| "detour" \| "time"` |  | How strongly the recommendation is made, from `essential` (gold) down to `time`. Each level brings its own label; pass children only to reword it. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<RecommendationBadgeMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the label of each level. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="recommendation-badge">`.

#### `StatusDot` (component)

A square, not a circle. It is a scrap of cut cloth, and a round dot is the one shape a blade cannot make. Each tone is cut as its own shape (see STATUS_GLYPH), and every shape carries an ink keyline, because gold on light cloth is 1.66:1 — under the 3:1 a graphic mark needs to be seen.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` | `string` |  | Announced text for a dot used on its own. Omit when the dot sits beside a label that already says the state — then it is decorative and hidden. |
| `pulse` | `boolean` | `false` | The state is live. Respects reduced motion: the pulse stills, the dot stays. |
| `tone` | `BadgeTone` | `neutral` | The status, in the same tones as Badge. Defaults to `neutral`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="status-dot">`.

#### `RECOMMENDATION_LABELS` (constant)

#### `badgeVariants` (function)

A status mark. Small, flat, cut — a scrap of the layer that says what state something is in.

#### `recommendationVariants` (function)

The editorial recommendation scale of the editorial system this grew out of — kept as lineage, and because "how strongly do we stand behind this" is a scale a support or knowledge tool also has. Gold is the layer a mola reveals last and least, so gold is `essential`.

Types: `RecommendationBadgeMessages`, `BadgeProps`, `BadgeTone`, `RecommendationBadgeProps`, `RecommendationLevel`, `StatusDotProps`

Used in: Agent hand-off, Confirmation, Empty states, Filtered table, List and detail

### Banner

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-banner--overview

A strip across the top of the application for a condition of the whole app, for as long as it holds.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Maintenance, an outage or a degraded service that affects every page.
- A state of the whole workspace or account: read-only, a failed payment, a trial that ends soon.
- A dropped connection, with announce, because it appeared during the session.

When not to:
- A condition of one page or panel. Use Callout.
- Something that has just happened, such as a finished deploy. Use Toast.
- An event the operator may want to find later. Use Notification centre.
- A decision the person must make before going on. Use AlertDialog.

- Do: Show one banner at a time, the most severe, in the AppShell banner slot.
- Don’t: Stack banners until the top bar sits halfway down the screen.
- Do: Pass announce only for a banner that appears during the session.
- Don’t: Announce a banner that is there when the page loads; it talks over the page’s own title.

Content:
- The title names the condition: “Scheduled maintenance”, “Read-only workspace”.
- The message says when, and what it means for the work: “Runs started then wait in the queue.”
- The action is a verb on the object: “Update card”, “View status”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves to the action and the Dismiss button, after the skip link and before the top bar.
- Enter or Space: Runs the action, or dismisses the banner.

Mola handles:
- Is a landmark named Announcement, or your `label`, so it can be found in a landmark list.
- Says its tone in hidden words before the message, Warning, Error or Success, and hides the icon.
- Says nothing on its own. With `announce`, says the message once, politely, from a status region that mounts empty and is filled a moment later.
- Names the dismiss button “Dismiss”.

You handle:
- Give each banner a `label` that names its kind when a page could show more than one.
- Pass `announce` only for a banner that appears during the session.
- Decide whether a dismissed banner comes back, and when.

Anatomy:
- Strip (`[data-slot="banner"]`): Full bleed, the tone’s wash, with a heavy foot edge in the status colour: the region a status owns.
- Icon (`[data-part="icon"]`): The tone’s glyph, so the tone is never colour alone. Hidden; the tone is also said in words.
- Message (`[data-part="message"]`): The condition and what it means for the work, in ink, never in the tone’s colour.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): A few words in bold that name the condition.
- Action (`[data-part="action"]`): One follow-up, a verb on the object.
- Dismiss (`[data-part="dismiss"]`): Present only with onDismiss; the caller decides whether the banner comes back.

#### `Banner` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the `<section>`, the strip itself.

- `tone`: `neutral`, `info`, `success`, `warn`, `danger` (default `info`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `action` | `ReactNode` |  | One follow-up, usually a `Button` at `size="sm"` or a link: “View status”. |
| `announce` | `boolean` | `false` | Say the message once, politely, when the banner appears. Only for a banner that appears during the session because something changed; one that is there when the page loads is read in order, and announcing it talks over the page's own title. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Replace the tone's icon, or `false` for none. |
| `label` | `string` |  | The landmark's name, “Announcement” (`messages.label`) by default. Name it for what it is when there is more than one kind: “Maintenance”. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<BannerMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the landmark's name, the dismiss button and the tone words read before the message. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onDismiss` | `(() => void)` |  | Renders a Dismiss button. The caller owns whether the banner comes back, and when. |
| `title` | `ReactNode` |  | A few words in bold before the message: “Scheduled maintenance”. |
| `tone` | `"neutral" \| "info" \| "success" \| "warn" \| "danger"` |  | The status of the condition, carried by the wash, the foot edge and the icon; `info` by default. The message still says it in words. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="banner">`.

#### `bannerVariants` (function)

A condition of the whole application, said once across the top of it: maintenance tonight, the workspace is read-only, the connection dropped.

Types: `BannerMessages`, `BannerProps`, `BannerTone`

### Callout

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-callout--overview

A message that belongs to a region of the page, with a tone, an optional title and actions.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A standing condition of a page or panel: a model that retires soon, a read-only workspace, a stale index.
- Something to read before working in the region, with one or two actions.
- A notice the person can dismiss, with onDismiss, where the caller decides whether it returns.
- A one-line aside beside a hero or at the head of a list, with variant="flat": the wash and a swatch, no seam, and it keeps its own height in a grid.

When not to:
- Something that has just happened, possibly elsewhere, such as a finished re-index. Use Toast.
- A condition of the whole application, such as maintenance tonight. Use Banner.
- A decision the person must make before going on. Use AlertDialog.
- A panel with nothing to show. Use Empty state.
- A short status on a row. Use Badge.

- Do: Match the tone to the consequence: warn for a limit approaching, danger for something broken.
- Don’t: Use danger for news or tips; red has to keep meaning broken.
- Do: Put a callout that appears after an action inside a live region, or use a toast.
- Don’t: Insert a static callout after an action and expect a screen reader to notice it.

Content:
- The title states the condition: “Token budget at 86%”, not “Warning” or “Heads up”.
- The body says what it means and what to do: “Switch the model in settings to keep evaluation history comparable.”
- Actions are verbs on the object: “Retry from 812”, “View log”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the dismiss button, when `onDismiss` is set.
- Enter or Space: Dismisses the callout.

Mola handles:
- Becomes a region named by its title when it has one, and stays out of the landmark list when it does not.
- Renders the title as a real heading at `headingLevel`.
- Hides the seam, the icon and the swatch from assistive tech; the title and body carry the tone in words.
- Names the dismiss button "Dismiss".

You handle:
- Choose a `headingLevel` that fits the page outline.
- Render a callout that appears in response to an action inside a live region, or use a toast.
- Move focus somewhere sensible after a dismiss, because the button that had it is gone.

Anatomy:
- Container (`[data-slot="callout"]`): The tone’s wash, bounded by the ink keyline like every cut shape.
- Seam (`[data-part="seam"]`): The relleno along the top edge: the tone as a cut-back layer, not a coloured side bar.
- Icon (`[data-part="icon"]`): The tone’s glyph, so the tone is never colour alone.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): A real heading; it also names the region.
- Body (`[data-part="body"]`): The message. Never coloured: the tone owns the region, not the reading column.
- Actions (`[data-part="actions"]`): Usually one secondary button at the small size.
- Dismiss (`[data-part="dismiss"]`): Present only with onDismiss; the caller decides whether it comes back.

#### `Callout` (component)

- `tone`: `neutral`, `info`, `success`, `warn`, `danger` (default `info`)
- `variant`: `seam`, `flat` (default `seam`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` |  | Buttons under the body. Usually one secondary, at `size="sm"`. |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5` | `3` | The heading element for `title`. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Replace the tone's default mark — the icon, or in `flat` the square swatch — or `false` for none. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<CalloutMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the dismiss button's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onDismiss` | `(() => void)` |  | Renders a dismiss button. The caller owns whether it comes back. |
| `title` | `ReactNode` |  | A short heading that states the message. With it the callout becomes a region named by the title; without it, it is plain content and the body has to stand alone. |
| `tone` | `"neutral" \| "info" \| "success" \| "warn" \| "danger"` |  | The status of the message, carried by the seam (or the swatch), the wash and the tone's icon; `info` by default. The title still has to say it in words. |
| `variant` | `"seam" \| "flat"` |  | `seam` (default) is the full message: top seam, keyline, icon. `flat` keeps only the wash and a square swatch, for a one-line aside beside a hero or at the head of a list, where a seam reads as a stripe. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="callout">`.

#### `calloutVariants` (function)

A message that belongs to a region of the page — "this agent is using a deprecated model", "billing is in read-only mode" — rather than to a moment, which is a toast's job.

Types: `CalloutMessages`, `CalloutProps`, `CalloutTone`, `CalloutVariant`

Used in: Agent console, Agent hand-off, Confirmation, Filtered table, Form validation, List and detail, Settings page

Changes:
- 0.4.0 (2026-10-13), feature: add a flat variant and a swatch colour key [#20](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/20)

### Empty state

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-empty-state--overview

What a panel shows when it has nothing to show, in four variants: empty, no results, error and no permission.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Nothing exists yet, and the action creates the first one.
- Filters hide everything, and the action clears them.
- The panel could not load, and the action retries.
- The content exists but is not the reader’s to see, and the text says who can grant it.

When not to:
- Content that is on its way. Use Skeleton.
- One slot, such as a chart or an image, waiting for its content. Use Placeholder.
- A warning about a page that does have content. Use Callout.
- A table with no rows; the table’s own empty row keeps the headers. Use Table.

- Do: Pick the variant for the conversation: no-results says filters are hiding things, and clears them.
- Don’t: Tell someone filtering their runs to “create your first run”.
- Do: Say who can grant access in no-permission.
- Don’t: Show “Access denied” with no next step.

Content:
- The title states the condition: “No runs match these filters”, “Traces did not load”.
- The description gives the cause or the count: “3 filters are hiding 1,284 runs.”
- An error says what happened and what happens next: “The trace store returned 503. This panel will try again in 30s.”

Mola handles:
- Renders the title as a real heading at `headingLevel`.
- Gives the `error` variant `role="alert"`, so a failed load is announced.
- Hides the icon and the seam from assistive tech.

You handle:
- Pick the variant that matches the situation, so the words fit it.
- Choose a `headingLevel` that fits the page outline.
- Pass the action that moves the person forward in `actions`.

Anatomy:
- Slot (`[data-slot="empty-state"]`): A shaded panel packed with relleno: a slot cut and waiting, not a blank rectangle.
- Seam (`[data-part="seam"]`): The tone’s relleno along the top, on error and no-permission only.
- Plate (`[data-part="plate"]`): A raised cloth plate, so the copy never reads across the slits.
- Icon (`[data-part="icon"]`): A small ink square with the variant’s glyph. Not an illustration.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): What state this is, as a heading.
- Description (`[data-part="description"]`): Why, and what will happen next.
- Actions (`[data-part="actions"]`): One or two buttons; the first is the way forward.

#### `EmptyState` (component)

- `kind`: `empty`, `no-results`, `error`, `no-permission` (default `empty`)
- `size`: `sm`, `md`, `lg` (default `md`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | What the state is, in a few words: "No saved replies yet", "Nothing matches 'refund'". |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` |  | One or two buttons. The first is the way forward. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | One sentence on why, or on what the action will do. |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4` | `3` | The element for `title`, `h3` by default. Set it from the page outline; the size stays the same. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Replace the kind's icon, or `false` for none. |
| `kind` | `"empty" \| "no-results" \| "error" \| "no-permission"` |  | Which of the four conversations this is: `empty` (default, nothing yet), `no-results` (a filter hides everything), `error` (could not load; announced as an alert) or `no-permission`. Each brings its icon and seam; the copy and the action must match it. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | How much of the slot the state fills vertically: `sm` inside a card or a table, `md` (default) for a panel, `lg` for a whole page. |
| `variant` (deprecated) | `EmptyStateKind` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `kind`, which takes the same values. `codemods/prop-vocabulary.ts` rewrites call sites. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="empty-state">`.

#### `emptyStateVariants` (function)

The state a panel is in when it has nothing to show — designed as a condition rather than as an apology.

Types: `EmptyStateKind`, `EmptyStateProps`

Used in: Agent hand-off, Confirmation, Empty states, Filtered table, List and detail

### Notification centre

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-notification-centre--overview

The record of what happened: a bell with an unread count, and the notifications under it, kept after their toasts have gone.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Events the operator may have missed: a failed run, an approval waiting, a view shared with them.
- The other half of a toast: send the same event to both, the toast to say it now and the centre to find it later.
- A full notifications page, with NotificationList on its own.

When not to:
- Saying an event now, where the operator is looking. Use Toast.
- A condition of the whole application that is still true. Use Banner.
- The history of one object, such as a run’s steps or a deploy log. Use Timeline.

- Do: Add the event to the centre when you raise its toast.
- Don’t: Raise a toast for an event that matters later and keep no record of it.
- Do: Let the unread count change silently; the bell’s name carries it.
- Don’t: Announce each new notification from the centre as well; the toast already said it.

Content:
- The title says what happened, in the past tense, standing on its own: “Re-index failed for Billing FAQ”.
- Times are relative while recent, “4 min ago”, and absolute after a day, “Yesterday 16:20”.
- The action is one verb: “View run”, “Review”.

Keyboard:
- Enter or Space: On the bell, opens the list and moves focus into it.
- Tab or Shift+Tab: Moves through Mark all as read and each notification’s action.
- Escape: Closes the list and returns focus to the bell.

Mola handles:
- Names the bell “Notifications”, with the unread count when there is one: “Notifications, 2 unread”.
- Changes the count silently, with no live region: the toast that raised the event already announced it.
- Renders a named list; each unread item starts with a hidden “Unread.” and its tone in words.
- Shows unread as an ink edge, a semibold title and the word New, never colour alone.
- Keeps focus in the popover when Mark all as read, its own button, goes away.

You handle:
- Raise a toast for an event that needs saying now; the centre will not say it.
- Write titles that stand on their own, away from the page they came from.
- Save the read state if it should survive a reload.

Anatomy:
- Notification (`[data-part="item"]`): One event. Unread, it carries a heavy ink edge at its start.
- Tone mark (`[data-part="tone"]`): The toast’s own cut square, so the record is recognisably the toast it came from.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): What happened, in the past tense; semibold while unread.
- New (`[data-part="unread"]`): The word that says unread, beside the edge and the weight, so unread is never colour alone.
- Time (`[data-part="time"]`): When, as a person reads it, in a time element with the machine date.
- Action (`[data-part="action"]`): One follow-up; running it marks the notification read.

#### `NotificationCentre` (component)

The bell for the top bar's `end`, and the list in a popover under it. `className`, `ref` and the rest go to the popover popup.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `notifications` (required) | `NotificationItem[]` |  | Newest first. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | Open on first render. |
