| `empty` | `ReactNode` |  | Shown when there is nothing. Defaults to “No notifications yet.” (`messages.empty`) |
| `footer` | `ReactNode` |  | Under the list: usually a link to a full notifications page, “View all”. |
| `label` | `string` |  | The popover's title and the bell's name, “Notifications” (`messages.label`) by default. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<NotificationsMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the defaults above, the unread and tone words, Mark all as read and the bell's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onMarkAllRead` | `(() => void)` |  | Called when the operator presses Mark all as read. The button shows only while something is unread. |
| `onMarkRead` | `((id: string) => void)` |  | Called with an item's id when its action runs. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the popover opens or closes. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the popover is open, when the page owns that. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="notification-centre">`.

#### `NotificationList` (component)

The list on its own, for a full notifications page or a panel the page places itself. `NotificationCentre` puts it in a popover under a bell. `className`, `ref` and the rest go to the wrapping `<div>`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `notifications` (required) | `NotificationItem[]` |  | Newest first. |
| `empty` | `ReactNode` |  | Shown when there is nothing. Defaults to “No notifications yet.” (`messages.empty`) |
| `label` | `string` |  | The list's accessible name, “Notifications” (`messages.label`) by default. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<NotificationsMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the defaults above, the unread and tone words, Mark all as read and the bell's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onMarkRead` | `((id: string) => void)` |  | Called with an item's id when its action runs. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="notification-list">`.

Types: `NotificationsMessages`, `NotificationCentreProps`, `NotificationItem`, `NotificationListProps`, `NotificationTone`

### Placeholder

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-placeholder--overview

A slot for something that does not exist yet, such as a chart with no data, announced as one image.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A chart, preview or image that cannot render yet, held at its final size.
- A layout where the slot must keep its place, with a label for the kind of thing and a name for what it would show.

When not to:
- Content that is loading and will arrive shortly. Use Skeleton.
- A whole panel with nothing in it, which needs an action. Use Empty state.
- A person or agent without a photo; initials stand in. Use Avatar.

Content:
- label is the kind, “Chart” or “Preview”; name is the thing, “Tickets by plan”.
- alt says what is missing, “No data yet: run latency by agent”, because it is announced in place of the content.

Mola handles:
- Gives the slot `role="img"` and `alt` as its accessible name.
- Hides the visible label and name from assistive tech, so the slot is read once.

You handle:
- Write an `alt` that says what is missing and why: Account logo, not uploaded yet.

#### `Placeholder` (component)

The slot where something will go and has not yet: a photograph that has not been shot, a chart with no data, a preview that cannot render.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `alt` (required) | `string` |  | Announced in place of the missing content. |
| `name` (required) | `string` |  | What it would show — "Account logo", "Tickets by plan". |
| `label` | `string` |  | The kind of thing that goes here — "Image", "Chart", "Preview". |
| `layer` | `"shade" \| "deep"` |  | The cloth it is cut from: `shade` by default, `deep` on a shaded panel, where `shade` would vanish. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<PlaceholderMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default label. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `tone` (deprecated) | `"default" \| "deep"` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `layer`: `default` is `shade`, `deep` is `deep`. A ground is a layer, as on `Panel`; `tone` is a status. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="placeholder">`.

Types: `PlaceholderMessages`, `PlaceholderProps`

### Progress

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-progress--overview

Progress for work moving toward done, which may be indeterminate; Meter for a reading inside a known range, with thresholds.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- An upload, an ingest or an evaluation over a known number of items, with Progress.
- Work that has started but has no known size yet, with a null value: the working relleno, not a spinner.
- A budget, a quota or a context window, with Meter and its warn and danger thresholds.

When not to:
- A model call or an agent run with no knowable total; show elapsed time, not a percentage. Use Run status.
- Content loading into a known layout. Use Skeleton.
- A headline figure with no range. Use Stat.
- Choosing a value on a scale. Use Slider.

- Do: Use Meter for a reading that can go down as well as up, such as tokens used this month.
- Don’t: Use Progress for a quota; a quota is not work moving toward done.
- Do: Use valueText to give the count: “212 / 300”.
- Don’t: Invent a percentage to make a wait feel shorter.

Content:
- Progress labels name the work as it happens: “Embedding documents”, “Evaluating prompts”.
- A bar that stays on screen after the work ends says how it ended: “Failed at 67%”.
- Meter labels name the measure and its period: “Tokens this month”.

Mola handles:
- Gives `Progress` `role="progressbar"` and `Meter` `role="meter"`, with their values, through Base UI.
- Names each bar by its visible `label`.
- Leaves out the value while `Progress` is indeterminate, and stills the working texture under reduced motion.
- Shows a meter's level as a figure and as ticks on the track, not by the fill colour alone.

You handle:
- Give every bar a `label`, or an `aria-label` when there is no room for one.
- Use `valueText` when a count says more than a percentage: 212 of 300 prompts.

#### `Meter` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the root, which holds the label row and the track. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | Visible label; also the accessible name. Use `aria-label` when there is no room for one. |
| `size` | `ProgressSize` | `md` | Track thickness: `sm` inside a row or a card, `md` (the default) on its own, `lg` when the reading is the point of the screen. |
| `thresholds` | `{ warn?: number; danger?: number; } \| undefined` | `{ warn: 0.75, danger: 0.9 }` | Fractions of the range at which the fill changes: gold past `warn`, rojo past `danger`. Drawn on the track as ticks. Pass `{}` for none. |
| `valueText` | `((formatted: string, value: number) => ReactNode)` |  | Replaces the value text at the end of the label row, e.g. "212 / 300 MB". Receives the formatted value and the raw number. |

Also accepts the props of `MeterRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="meter">`.

#### `Progress` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the root, which holds the label row and the track. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | Visible label; also the accessible name. Use `aria-label` when there is no room for one. |
| `showValue` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the formatted value at the end of the label row. Ignored while indeterminate. |
| `size` | `ProgressSize` | `md` | Track thickness: `sm` inside a row or a card, `md` (the default) on its own, `lg` for the one task a screen is waiting on. |
| `tone` | `"neutral" \| "danger" \| "success" \| "ink"` | `neutral` | Fill colour. `neutral` (ink) is in-flight; `success` and `danger` (verde, rojo) for a finished state that stays on screen. `ink` is deprecated since 0.7.0 and removed in 1.0.0: use `neutral`, the same fill under the status word every other `tone` uses. |
| `valueText` | `((formatted: string \| null, value: number \| null) => ReactNode)` |  | Replaces the value text, e.g. "212 / 300 prompts". |

Also accepts the props of `ProgressRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="progress">`.

#### `meterLevel` (function)

Which band a reading is in — exported for the stat tiles and tables that colour a number to match.

Types: `MeterProps`, `ProgressProps`, `ProgressSize`

### Skeleton

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-skeleton--overview

Bones in the shape of content that has not arrived, with SkeletonGroup to mark the region busy and say once what is loading.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A card, list or table whose layout is known while its data loads.
- Swapping bones for content in place, so the layout barely moves when the data lands.
- Lines of text that follow the surrounding type size, with SkeletonText.

When not to:
- Work with a known size, such as an upload. Use Progress.
- A slot whose content does not exist yet, rather than one that is loading. Use Placeholder.
- A button waiting on its own action; give it its loading state. Use Button.
- Text a model is still writing. Use Streaming text.

- Do: Wrap the bones in SkeletonGroup with a label, so the region is announced once.
- Don’t: Add a spinner or a “Loading…” line to each bone; the system has no spinner, by design.
- Do: Cut the bones to the content’s shape: an avatar bone, a short line for a name.
- Don’t: Cover the area with one generic block.

Content:
- The group label names what is loading: “Loading agent”, “Loading runs”.

Mola handles:
- Hides every bone from assistive tech.
- In `SkeletonGroup`, marks the region `aria-busy` and says the `label` once through a polite status mounted from the first render.
- Removes the sweep under reduced motion, and in forced colours draws text bones in the system grey.

You handle:
- Wrap the bones in a `SkeletonGroup` and say what is loading in its `label`: Loading runs.

#### `Skeleton` (component)

Loading is a cut shape whose content has not arrived, so a block keeps the keyline every other shape has and carries the same relleno as the empty state.

- `shape`: `block`, `text`, `line`, `avatar` (default `block`)
- `size`: `xs`, `sm`, `md`, `lg`, `xl` (default `md`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `shape` | `"block" \| "text" \| "line" \| "avatar"` |  | What the bone stands in for: a `block` (default) for a card or chart, `text` for a line, `avatar` for a person's mark. Size a block with classes. |
| `size` | `"xs" \| "sm" \| "md" \| "lg" \| "xl"` |  | For `shape="avatar"` only: the Avatar size it stands in for, so the bone is exactly the mark that replaces it. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="skeleton">`.

#### `SkeletonGroup` (component)

The region that is loading, and the one place that says so.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fallback` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The bones, shown while loading. |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | What is loading, said once: "Loading agent", "Loading runs". |
| `loading` (required) | `boolean` |  | While true the bones show and the region is busy. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` |  | The real content, shown once loaded. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="skeleton-group">`.

#### `SkeletonText` (component)

Lines of text that have not arrived. The last line is short, because a paragraph ends ragged; a block of equal bars reads as a table.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `lines` | `number` | `3` | How many lines, 3 by default. Match the text that will arrive; the last line is drawn short. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="skeleton-text">`.

#### `skeletonVariants` (function)

Types: `SkeletonGroupProps`, `SkeletonProps`, `SkeletonTextProps`

Used in: Agent hand-off, Form validation, List and detail, Settings page

Changes:
- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

### Toast

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-feedback-toast--overview

A floating notice for something that happened elsewhere or just now; a running toast turns into its result in place.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Confirming an action whose result is not visible where the operator is.
- Long work the operator may leave, with toast.running, then toast.update with the outcome.
- One follow-up action, such as Undo or View run.

When not to:
- A condition of the page that stays true, such as read-only mode. Use Callout.
- A condition of the whole application, such as a dropped connection. Use Banner.
- Keeping the event to find later; send it to the notification centre as well. Use Notification centre.
- Anything that needs a decision, or more than one action. Use Dialog.
- A validation error on a field. Use Field.
- Confirming a copy to the clipboard. Confirm it in place, as CodeBlock and Message do.

- Do: Update the running toast with the outcome, so the result lands where the work was.
- Don’t: Leave the running toast and fire a second, unrelated success toast.

Content:
- The title states what happened, in the past tense: “Index rebuilt”, “Deploy failed”.
- The description adds the cause or the figures: “1,204 documents · 3m 41s”.
- The action is one verb: “Undo”, “Retry”, “View”.

Keyboard:
- F6: Moves focus to the toast region and pauses the timers.
- Tab: Moves through the toasts and their action and dismiss buttons.
- Enter or Space: Runs the action, or dismisses the toast, on the focused button.
- Escape: Closes the toast that has focus.
- Shift+Tab: Leaves the region from its start and returns focus to where it was.

Mola handles:
- Renders the stack in a polite live region, and announces `danger` toasts assertively, once: the card opts out of the polite region so the alert is the only reading.
- Prefixes the title with hidden words for its tone: Success, Error, In progress.
- Pauses the timers while the region has keyboard focus, and keeps a running toast until it is updated or dismissed.
- Names the dismiss button "Dismiss".

You handle:
- Mount `ToastProvider` once, near the root.
- Write a title that says what happened without the page around it.
- Use a dialog for anything the person must answer; a toast can be missed.

#### `ToastProvider` (component)

Mount once near the root, inside `MessagesProvider` if the app has one. Renders the viewport, bottom-right, into a portal.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `limit` | `number` | `3` | Toasts kept visible at once; older ones fold away. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ToastMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the close button and the tone words read before a title. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. The toasts render here, so a `MessagesProvider` must sit above this one, not inside it. |
| `timeout` | `number` | `5000` | Default auto-dismiss, ms. Also the timeout a running toast starts when `update()` turns it into a result. |

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `useToast` (hook)

The toast API. Call from anywhere inside a `ToastProvider`.

Types: `ToastMessages`, `ToastOptions`, `ToastProviderProps`, `ToastTone`

Used in: Confirmation, Settings page

## Forms

### Checkbox

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-checkbox--overview

A square that turns a choice on or off, applied when the form is saved; CheckboxGroup for any number of choices from a set.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- One yes-or-no setting that takes effect on Save.
- Any number of options from a short visible list, with CheckboxGroup and its legend.
- A parent that selects all its children, and goes indeterminate when only some are on.

When not to:
- A setting that takes effect the moment it changes. Use Switch.
- Exactly one option from a set. Use Radio.
- Filters above a list that apply as they are pressed. Use Chip.
- A long list of options that people would rather search. Use Combobox.

- Do: Label the positive choice: “Stream partial output”.
- Don’t: Label a negative, “Don’t stream output”, which makes checked mean off.

Content:
- Labels state what is turned on, in sentence case, with no trailing period.
- Conditions go in description, not in the label: “Up to three times, with exponential backoff.”
- The legend names the set: “Tools”, “Notify on”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the checkbox.
- Space: Toggles it. Enter does not, as with a native checkbox.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders `role="checkbox"` with `aria-checked`, and `aria-checked="mixed"` for indeterminate.
- The `label` names the control and the whole label toggles it; the `description` sits outside the label and arrives once, through `aria-describedby`.
- Indeterminate is a bar, not a faded check, so the third state is a shape and not a tint.
- `CheckboxGroup` is a real `<fieldset>` and `<legend>`, so each option is heard in the group’s context.

You handle:
- Give every checkbox a `label`, or an `aria-label` when it stands alone in a table row.
- Give a set of checkboxes a `CheckboxGroup` with a `legend` that names the question.

#### `Checkbox` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the DOM props go to the control, with or without a `label`; the row a `label` adds takes `wrapperClassName`. `className` used to move to that row when a label was set.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the box itself, with or without a `label`. A string, not Base UI's state function: use the `data-checked` and `data-disabled` variants for state. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Second line under the label. Linked as the control's description. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | Visible label. The whole label is the press target. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the label row a `label` adds around the control. `className` always styles the control itself. |

Also accepts the props of `CheckboxRootProps`, `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="checkbox">`.

#### `CheckboxGroup` (component)

A set of checkboxes that share one value array, inside a real fieldset and legend — so a screen reader announces "Tools, group" before the first option and every option is heard in that context, which no `aria-label` on a div reproduces across readers.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `legend` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The group's name, rendered as the fieldset legend in the label register. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the `<fieldset>` that holds the legend, the description and the options. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Copy under the legend. |
| `error` | `ReactNode` |  | A rule the group breaks as a whole, such as “choose at least one”. Shown under the legend, joined to the group's description, and listed by a `FormErrorSummary` in the same `Form`. |
| `orientation` | `"horizontal" \| "vertical"` | `vertical` | Lay options out in a row rather than a column. |

Also accepts the props of `CheckboxGroupProps`.

`className` goes to `<fieldset data-slot="checkbox-group">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div>`.

#### `checkboxBoxClasses` (constant)

A square cut from the cloth. Unchecked, it is raised cloth inside an ink keyline; checked, the square is filled with ink and the mark is cut out of it in cloth — the same inversion a selected chip and a selected segment make, so "on" looks the same wherever it appears.

Types: `CheckboxGroupProps`, `CheckboxProps`

Used in: Form validation

### Chip

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-chip--overview

A filter chip with a real checkbox or radio inside, filled with ink when on, with an optional count.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Filters above a list or table that apply as they are pressed.
- Showing how many rows each filter would leave, with count.
- One of a few fixed ranges, such as 1h, 24h and 7d, with type set to radio.

When not to:
- A status shown to the reader rather than chosen by them. Use Badge.
- Switching between views of the same data. Use Segmented control.
- Options in a form that is saved later. Use Checkbox.
- Values picked from a long list into a field; Combobox shows them as chips inside it. Use Combobox.
- A set of values someone reads and removes, such as the labels on a record. Use Tag group.

- Do: Wrap chips in a ChipGroup with a legend, so “Failed” is heard as a status filter.
- Don’t: Leave a row of chips unnamed; “Failed” on its own sounds like an error.

Content:
- Use the same words as the status being filtered: “Succeeded”, “Failed”.
- Keep default chips to one line; use the comfortable size for labels that wrap.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the chip, or into a radio set at its checked chip.
- Space: Toggles a checkbox chip, or checks a radio chip.
- ArrowRight or ArrowDown: Moves to and checks the next chip in a radio set.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowUp: Moves to and checks the previous chip in a radio set.

Mola handles:
- Each chip is a real `<input type="checkbox">` or `radio`, visually hidden but focusable, so state, keys and form submission come from the platform.
- The wrapping `<label>` names the input, and `count` is joined with a real space so it is heard as “Failed 12”.
- Checked is an ink fill, not a hue change, and forced colours keep it.
- `ChipGroup` is a `<fieldset>` whose `legend` can be visually hidden with `hideLegend` and still be announced.

You handle:
- Wrap a row of chips in `ChipGroup` with a `legend` that says what they filter.
- Give radio chips one shared `name`, or the arrow keys will not move between them.

#### `Chip` (component)

`className` and `style` go to the `<label>`, which is the chip you see; `ref` and every other prop go to the `<input>`, which is the control a form, a test or a screen reader talks to. `style` used to go to the visually hidden input with the rest, where it did nothing.

- `size`: `sm`, `md`, `default`, `comfortable` (default `sm`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The visible label. Also the control's accessible name, via the wrapping `<label>`. |
| `count` | `number` |  | A count after the label: how many rows this filter would leave. Tabular, so a row of chips does not jitter as counts change. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "default" \| "comfortable"` |  | `sm` (default) is a one-line filter chip that never wraps; `md` is a control height tall and lets a longer label wrap. |
| `type` | `"checkbox" \| "radio"` | `checkbox` | `radio` for one-of-N within a `name` group, `checkbox` for multi-select. |

`className` goes to `<label data-slot="chip">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input>`.

#### `ChipGroup` (component)

A row of chips inside a real fieldset. The legend is what a screen reader says before the first chip — "Status, group" — so "Failed" is heard as a status filter rather than as an alarming word on its own.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `legend` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | What the chips filter. Rendered as the legend, so each chip is announced in its context. |
| `hideLegend` | `boolean` | `false` | Hide the legend visually while keeping it for assistive technology — for a toolbar row whose meaning is obvious from position. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<fieldset data-slot="chip-group">`.

#### `chipVariants` (function)

A filter you can see the state of. A real `<input type="checkbox|radio">` sits inside the styled `<label>`, hidden with the clip pattern (`sr-only`) rather than `display:none`, so it stays focusable and in the accessibility tree. Keyboard handling, radio-group arrow keys, the checked state and form submission all come from the platform — there is no ARIA widget to keep in sync and nothing to re-implement.

Types: `ChipGroupProps`, `ChipProps`

### Combobox

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-combobox--overview

A select you can type into: Combobox always ends on an option from the list; Autocomplete takes free text with suggestions.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A list too long to scroll, such as people, tools or runs, where typing narrows it.
- Several values from a long list, with multiple, shown as chips inside the field.
- A server search, with filter set to null and loading and status reporting the results.
- Free text where suggestions save keystrokes and keep names consistent, such as a run label, with Autocomplete.

When not to:
- A short list people can scan without typing. Use Select.
- Two to five options that should all be visible. Use Radio.
- Jumping to a page or running a command from anywhere. Use Command palette.
- Filtering the rows of a list as you type. Use SearchInput.

- Do: Use Combobox when the form must submit a value the list offered, such as a reviewer.
- Don’t: Use Autocomplete for a reviewer or an owner; a half-typed name would be submitted as typed.
- Do: Write the empty message for the moment: before typing, what can be searched; after, the query that found nothing.
- Don’t: Leave the popup blank while results load or when nothing matches.

Content:
- The placeholder says what to type: “Search people”, “run-4187 or eval-sweeper”.
- Keep a disabled option in the list, with its reason as the description: “Requires approval”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the input.
- ArrowDown or ArrowUp: Opens the list, then moves the highlight while the caret stays in the input.
- Enter: Chooses the highlighted option.
- Escape: Closes the list.
- ArrowLeft: With `multiple`, reaches the chips from the start of the input; ArrowRight in right-to-left.
- Backspace: With `multiple`, removes the focused chip, or the last one from an empty input.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders a `combobox` input wired to a `listbox` with `aria-activedescendant`, and labelled `group`s for grouped options.
- Loading and “No matches” are said in Base UI’s polite status regions, and the list carries `aria-busy` while loading.
- The clear and open buttons are named (“Clear selection”), and each chip’s remove button is named “Remove <label>”; all three are 24px targets at every density.
- With `multiple`, the input is described with the count and the key that reaches the chips.

You handle:
- Name it: put it in a `Field`, or pass `label` or `aria-label`.
- Translate `clearLabel`, `triggerLabel`, `removeLabel` and `emptyMessage` with the rest of the page.

#### `Autocomplete` (component)

Free text with suggestions: a run label, a tag, a search. The typed text is the value; picking a suggestion only fills it in.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `(string \| ComboboxOption)[]` |  | Suggestions. Plain strings, or options with a description line. |
| `defaultValue` | `string` |  | The text in the input on first render, uncontrolled. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Stated, not faded: the input goes to the shaded cloth and no suggestions open. |
| `emptyMessage` | `ReactNode` |  | What the popup says when nothing matches. Omit to close the popup instead. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | A visible label above the input. Omit it inside `Field`, which renders its own, and spread Field's control props onto this component instead. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | Suggestions are on their way: the popup shows the working texture and "Searching…" and the list is marked busy. Set it while a server search is in flight. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | Locale for the built-in matching. Omitted, the runtime's locale is used. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<AutocompleteMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the status line while loading. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Form field name. The typed text is what submits, not a suggestion's value. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: string) => void)` |  | Every change to the text, typed or chosen. |
| `placeholder` | `string` |  | Shown in the empty input. A hint at what to type, never the label: it disappears on the first keystroke. |
| `readOnly` | `boolean` |  | Shows the text without letting it change; it still submits and can be focused and read. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Marks the field required for form validation, and the `label`, when there is one, with the required mark. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Control height, shared with Input and Select so the field lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `status` | `ReactNode` |  | A line above the suggestions: "Showing 20 of 312", or an error from the search. |
| `value` | `string` |  | The text in the input, controlled. Typing and picking a suggestion both change it, so pair it with `onValueChange`. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the wrapper that holds `label` and the input. Only rendered with a `label`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input data-slot="autocomplete">`.

#### `Combobox` (component)

`className` goes to the box around the input (the element with the cut), `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props to the `<input>`, with or without a `label`; the wrapper a `label` adds takes `wrapperClassName`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `ComboboxItems` |  | Flat options, or labelled groups. With `filter={null}`, the options to show right now. |
| `clearLabel` | `string` |  | Accessible name of the clear button. |
| `defaultValue` | `string \| string[] \| null` |  | The option `value` selected on first render, uncontrolled. `null` or omitted starts empty. The option values selected on first render, uncontrolled. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Stated, not faded: the field and any chips go to the shaded cloth and the clear, open and remove buttons are taken away. |
| `emptyMessage` | `ReactNode` |  | What the popup says when nothing matches. |
| `filter` | `((option: ComboboxOption, query: string) => boolean) \| null` |  | How typed text matches an option. Pass `null` when the caller filters — a server search — and hand the results in through `items`. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | A visible label above the field. Omit it inside `Field`, which renders its own, and spread Field's control props onto this component instead. |
| `loading` | `boolean` |  | Results are on their way: the popup shows the working texture and "Searching…". |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | Locale for the built-in matching. Omitted, the runtime's locale is used. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ComboboxMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the status lines, the button names and the chip hint. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `multiple` | `boolean` |  | Omit, or `false`, for a single choice. Set `true` to pick several, shown as removable chips in the field. Pick several options, shown as removable chips in the field. Values become `string[]`. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Form field name. The selected option's `value` is submitted under it (one entry per value with `multiple`). |
| `onInputValueChange` | `((query: string) => void)` |  | What is typed, as it is typed: the hook for a server search. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: string \| null) => void) \| ((value: string[]) => void)` |  | Called with the chosen option's `value`, or `null` when the field is cleared. Called with every selected value after a pick, a chip removal or a clear. |
| `placeholder` | `string` |  | Shown in the empty input. A hint at what can be searched, never the label: it disappears on the first keystroke. |
| `readOnly` | `boolean` |  | Shows the selection without letting it change. The clear, open and remove buttons are taken away, but the value still submits and can be focused and read. |
| `removeLabel` | `((label: string) => string)` |  | Only with `multiple`: a single value has no chips to remove. Typed `never` so passing it here is an error rather than a silent no-op — an optional discriminant does not narrow the union by itself. Accessible name of each chip's remove button. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Marks the field required for form validation, and the `label`, when there is one, with the required mark. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | Control height, shared with Input and Select so a combobox lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `status` | `ReactNode` |  | A line above the results — "Showing 20 of 312", or an error from the search. |
| `triggerLabel` | `string` |  | Accessible name of the button that opens the list. |
| `value` | `string \| string[] \| null` |  | The selected option's `value`, controlled. `null` is no selection; leave it `undefined` to let the combobox hold its own state. The selected option values, controlled, in the order they were picked. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the wrapper that holds `label` and the field. Only rendered with a `label`. |

`className` goes to `<div data-slot="combobox">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input>`.

Types: `AutocompleteMessages`, `ComboboxMessages`, `AutocompleteProps`, `ComboboxCommonProps`, `ComboboxItems`, `ComboboxMultipleProps`, `ComboboxOption`, `ComboboxOptionGroup`, `ComboboxProps`, `ComboboxSingleProps`

Used in: Filtered table, Form validation

Changes:
- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

### Date picker

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-date-picker--overview

DatePicker for one date, typed or picked from a calendar; DateRangePicker for a period, with presets and two typed ends.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- One date, such as when to run an evaluation, with DatePicker.
- A period for a report or a filter, with DateRangePicker and presets such as the last 7 days.
- Dates with bounds or unavailable days, through min, max and isDateDisabled.

When not to:
- A period between two dates, where two single pickers would not check the order. Use DateRangePicker.
- One date; a range picker would ask for a second press. Use DatePicker.
- A duration, such as a timeout in seconds. Use Number input.
- A few fixed relative ranges, such as 1h, 24h and 7d. Use Chip.

- Do: Pass locale, so the typed order, digits and first day of the week follow it.
- Don’t: Guess the date order from the language; es-ES and es-PA write dates in different orders.
- Do: Keep typed entry on in DateRangePicker; typing is the accessible path into a date.
- Don’t: Turn typedEntry off unless the presets and the grid cover every case.

Content:
- Label the field by what the date is for: “Run evaluation on”, “Report period”.
- Leave the placeholder to the component; it is the locale’s own pattern.
- Rules the picker cannot know, such as required, go to Field as an instruction: “Choose a date for the evaluation.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the date input, then to the calendar button.
- Enter: In the input, reads the typed date and states why if it is refused.
- Enter or Space: On the calendar button, opens the calendar on the chosen day; in the grid, chooses the focused day.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowRight or ArrowUp or ArrowDown: Moves by day and by week; left and right swap in right-to-left.
- PageUp or PageDown or Shift+PageUp or Shift+PageDown: Moves by month, or by year with Shift.
- Home or End: Moves to the first or last day of the week.
- Escape: Closes the calendar without changing the value and returns focus to the button.

Mola handles:
- The calendar is a `grid` named by its month heading, with one tab stop; the day carries `aria-selected`, today `aria-current="date"`, and an unavailable day says so in its name.
- The month heading is a polite live region, so paging is announced.
- The input is described by its format (“Format: DD/MM/YYYY”), and a refused date is a sentence in a `role="alert"` with a mark, not colour alone.
- The calendar button is named by the value, “Change date, 6 October 2026”, and focus returns to it when the calendar closes.

You handle:
- Name it: put it in a `Field`, or pass `aria-label`.
- State rules the picker cannot know, such as “required”, through `Field`’s `error`.

#### `Calendar` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `activeCellRef` | `Ref<HTMLTableCellElement>` |  | Ref to the day cell that holds the roving tab stop. |
| `autoFocus` | `boolean` | `false` | Move focus to the active day when the calendar mounts — for a popup that has just opened. |
| `defaultFocusedDate` | `Date \| null` |  | Where focus starts. Defaults to the selected day, else today. |
| `headingId` | `string` |  | Id for the month heading, so a dialog can be named by it. |
| `isDateDisabled` | `((date: Date) => boolean)` |  | Days that exist but cannot be chosen: weekends in a freeze, public holidays. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | BCP 47 tag. `undefined` uses the runtime's locale, as Intl does. |
| `max` | `Date \| null` |  | The latest day that can be chosen, inclusive. Later days are disabled, and focus never moves past it. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<CalendarMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the month buttons' names. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `min` | `Date \| null` |  | The earliest day that can be chosen, inclusive. Earlier days are disabled, and focus never moves before it. |
| `onSelect` | `((date: Date) => void)` |  | Called with the day a person chose. Range logic belongs to the caller. |
| `renderHeading` | `((props: { id: string; className: string; children: ReactNode; }) => ReactNode)` |  | Render the heading as the popup's title instead of a plain element. |
| `selected` | `DateRange` |  | The selected day, or the range. A single date is a range whose ends match. |
| `today` | `Date` |  | Inject "now" for stable stories and tests. Pass it when server rendering too: today's date, and with it the starting month, depends on the zone it is computed in, and the grid cannot wait for hydration without arriving blank. The server's date, passed down, keeps both renders on one day. |
| `visibleDate` | `Date \| null` |  | Show this day's month and move the tab stop to it, without moving focus — for a date typed elsewhere that the grid should follow. Compared by identity, so a new `Date` for the same day shows it again after the person has paged away. |
| `weekStartsOn` | `number` |  | 0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday. Overrides the locale's first day of the week. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="calendar">`.

#### `DatePicker` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | Open the calendar on first render — for a step that exists only to pick a date. |
| `defaultValue` | `Date \| null` | `null` | The day chosen on first render, uncontrolled. `null` or omitted starts empty. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Stated, not faded: the input goes to the shaded cloth and the calendar button is disabled. |
| `isDateDisabled` | `((date: Date) => boolean)` |  | Days inside `min`–`max` that still cannot be chosen: weekends in a freeze, public holidays. Disabled in the grid, and refused when typed with a sentence naming the day. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | BCP 47 tag for names, digits, typed order and week start. `undefined` uses the runtime's locale. |
| `max` | `Date \| null` |  | The latest day that can be chosen, inclusive; only the day counts, not the time. Later days are disabled in the grid and refused when typed, with a sentence naming the limit. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<DatePickerMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the calendar button's name, the format line and the sentences a refused date is stated with. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. The calendar's own words come from the provider's `calendar` table. |
| `min` | `Date \| null` |  | The earliest day that can be chosen, inclusive; only the day counts, not the time. Earlier days are disabled in the grid and refused when typed, with a sentence naming the limit. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Submitted as `yyyy-mm-dd`, whatever the display locale. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the calendar opens or closes, by the button, Escape, a pick or a press outside. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: Date \| null) => void)` |  | Called with the day at local midnight when one is picked or a typed date commits (on Enter or blur, never per keystroke), and with `null` when the text is cleared. A typed date that cannot be read is shown as an error and does not call it. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the calendar is open, when the page owns that. |
| `placeholder` | `string` |  | Defaults to the locale's format, e.g. `DD/MM/YYYY`. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Marks the input required for form validation. Inside `Field`, the label carries the required mark. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Control height, shared with Input and Select so the picker lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `today` | `Date` |  | Inject "now" for stable stories and tests. Pass it when server rendering too: today's date, and with it the starting month, depends on the zone it is computed in, and the grid cannot wait for hydration without arriving blank. The server's date, passed down, keeps both renders on one day. |
| `value` | `Date \| null` |  | The chosen day, controlled, at local midnight. `null` is no date; leave it `undefined` to let the picker hold its own state. |
| `weekStartsOn` | `number` |  | 0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday. Overrides the locale's first day of the week. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the outer element that holds the field and its error. |

`className` goes to `<div data-slot="input-group">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input data-slot="input">`.

#### `DateRangePicker` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | Open the calendar on first render. |
| `defaultValue` | `DateRange` | `{ start: null, end: null }` | The range on first render, uncontrolled. Omitted, both ends start `null`. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Stated, not faded: the trigger goes to the shaded cloth and the popup cannot open. |
| `isDateDisabled` | `((date: Date) => boolean)` |  | Days inside `min`–`max` that cannot be an end of the range: public holidays, a freeze. Disabled in the grid and refused when typed. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | BCP 47 tag for names, digits, typed order, week start and how the period is written on the trigger. `undefined` uses the runtime's locale. |
| `max` | `Date \| null` |  | The latest day either end can be, inclusive. Later days are disabled in the grid and refused when typed. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<DateRangePickerMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the inputs' labels, the status line, the default presets' labels and the sentences a refused date is stated with. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. The calendar's own words come from the provider's `calendar` table. |
| `min` | `Date \| null` |  | The earliest day either end can be, inclusive. Earlier days are disabled in the grid and refused when typed. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Submitted as `<name>-start` and `<name>-end`, `yyyy-mm-dd`. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the popup opens or closes. A range pressed on the grid or a preset closes it; a range completed by typing leaves it open, so the person sees it drawn. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: DateRange) => void)` |  | Called with the whole range once both ends are chosen, pressed or typed, when a preset is picked, and with both ends `null` when both typed dates are cleared. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the calendar is open, when the page owns that. |
| `placeholder` | `string` |  | The trigger's text while no range is chosen. Defaults to "Choose a period" (`messages.placeholder`). |
| `presets` | `DateRangePreset[]` | `(Object.keys(PRESET_RANGES) as PresetKey[]).map((key) => {   const preset = { label: dateRangePickerMessages.presetLabels[key], range: PRESET_RANGES[key] }   defaultPresetKeys.set(preset, key)   return preset })` | Pass `[]` for none. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Marks the trigger `aria-required`. Inside `Field`, the label carries the required mark. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Trigger height, shared with Input and Select so the picker lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `today` | `Date` |  | Inject "now" for stable stories and tests. Pass it when server rendering too: today's date, and with it the starting month, depends on the zone it is computed in, and the grid cannot wait for hydration without arriving blank. The server's date, passed down, keeps both renders on one day. |
| `typedEntry` | `boolean` | `true` | Start and end inputs above the calendar. On by default: typing is the accessible path into a date, not an extra, and the single picker always has it. Turn it off only where presets and the grid cover every case. |
| `value` | `DateRange` |  | The chosen range, controlled: `{ start, end }`, each a day at local midnight or `null`. The half-chosen range between two presses lives only in the popup and is never the value. |
| `weekStartsOn` | `number` |  | 0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday. Overrides the locale's first day of the week. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the outer element that holds the trigger. `className` styles the trigger itself. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="date-range-picker-trigger">`.

#### `formatDateRange` (function)

"6 – 12 Oct 2026" in the locale's own words, or null for an incomplete range.

#### `defaultRangePresets` (constant)

#### `getDatePattern` (function)

The typed format for a locale, read from `formatToParts` rather than from a table of locales — so it is right for any locale the engine knows, and the guidance shown under the field can never disagree with what is accepted. The letters are Latin (DD, MM, YYYY) in every locale: it is the convention people recognise as a format, where translated letters are not.

Types: `CalendarMessages`, `DatePickerMessages`, `DateRangePickerMessages`, `DateEntryMessages`, `CalendarProps`, `DatePickerProps`, `DateRangePickerProps`, `DateLocale`, `DatePart`, `DatePattern`, `DateRange`, `DateRangePreset`

Used in: Filtered table, Form validation

- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

### Field

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-field--overview

A label, a control and a hint or error wired together; the page also covers Input, Textarea, SearchInput and Label.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Any form control that needs a visible label, a hint and a validation message, through the render prop.
- One line of text with Input, with a unit or an icon inside the field through leading and trailing.
- Several lines with Textarea, which grows with its content when autosize is set.
- Filtering a list with SearchInput, which shows a shortcut cap when empty and a clear button when not.

When not to:
- A number that is stepped, bounded or formatted. Use Number input.
- A choice from a known list. Use Select.
- A date or a period. Use Date picker.
- A prompt to a model, with attachments and a send button. Use Prompt input.
- Several controls answering one question, or a form that can fail in several places at once. Use Form.

- Do: Spread the control props onto the control, so its id, description and invalid state are wired.
- Don’t: Use the placeholder as the label; it disappears as soon as someone types.
- Do: Put the unit inside the field with trailing: “seconds”, “tokens”.
- Don’t: Write the unit as text after the field, where it reads as a second field.

Content:
- Labels are short nouns in sentence case: “Run name”, “Webhook URL”.
- Hints explain the consequence: “Shown in the run list and in alerts.”
- Errors say what is wrong and how to fix it: “Must be between 1 and 900 seconds.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the control.
- Enter or Space: On a search field’s clear button, empties the field and returns focus to it.

Mola handles:
- `Field` links the label to the control and joins the hint and error into `aria-describedby`, in the order they are shown.
- An error sets `aria-invalid` and carries a cut square so it is not colour alone. It is announced with `role="alert"`, except inside a `Form` with a `FormErrorSummary`, which announces every error once.
- The required asterisk is hidden from assistive technology while `required` reaches the control; “Optional” is kept as a separate word.
- `SearchInput` is a `searchbox`, and its clear button is named “Clear search”.

You handle:
- Spread the `control` props onto the control; without them it has no name and no description.
- When several fields can fail at once, put the fields in a `Form` with a `FormErrorSummary`; their errors then stop being alerts and the summary takes focus.
- Bind the key shown by `shortcut` yourself; the key cap is display only.

Anatomy:
- Label (`[data-part="label"]`): Names the control, in the label register, and is wired to it with htmlFor.
- Label aside (`[data-part="label-aside"]`): A count or a default beside the label; never a second label.
- Hint (`[data-part="hint"]`): States the rule. It stays when an error arrives, and is read first.
- Error (`[data-part="error"]`): Says how this value broke the rule. Announced on insertion, and marked with a cut square so it is not colour alone.

#### `Label` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<LabelMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the "Optional" tag. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `optional` | `boolean` | `false` | Renders the "Optional" tag. |
| `required` | `boolean` | `false` | Renders the required asterisk. Does NOT set `required` on any control. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<label data-slot="label">`.

#### `Field` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The visible label, wired to the control by `id`. Required: a placeholder is not a label, and a field with none is a question nobody hears. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Alias of `hint`, for callers who think of it as a description. `hint` wins if both are set. |
| `error` | `ReactNode` |  | Validation message under the control. Its presence is what marks the field invalid. |
| `hint` | `ReactNode` |  | Supporting copy under the control. |
| `labelAside` | `ReactNode` |  | Something to the right of the label: a character count, a "Reset" link. |
| `optional` | `boolean` | `false` | Renders the "Optional" tag. Ignored when `required`. |
| `required` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows the required mark on the label and passes `required` to the control through the render prop, so the browser validates it too. Overrides `optional`. |

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `Input` (component)

A text control: an `<input>` inside a box that wears the shared control classes. The box is always there, so the icon or unit of an adorned input sits inside the same keyline as the text rather than beside it — a unit outside the box reads as a second field.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `leading` | `ReactNode` |  | Inside the cut, before the text: an icon, a currency sign, a protocol. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Height and type from the density tokens. |
| `trailing` | `ReactNode` |  | Inside the cut, after the text: a unit ("ms", "tokens"), a button. |

`className` goes to `<div data-slot="input-group">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input data-slot="input">`.

#### `Textarea` (component)

Multi-line text: a system prompt, a note on a run, a JSON override. Same cut and states as `Input`; padding comes from the density tokens so the first line sits where a single-line input's text would.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `autosize` | `boolean` | `false` | Grow with the content instead of scrolling inside a fixed box, using `field-sizing: content` — no script, no resize observer, and it still respects `rows` as the minimum and `max-h-*` as the ceiling. Browsers without it fall back to the normal resizable textarea, which is the correct degradation rather than a broken one. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<textarea data-slot="textarea">`.

#### `SearchInput` (component)

The filter box that heads every list in an internal tool.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clearLabel` | `string` |  | Accessible name for the clear button. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<SearchInputMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the clear button's default name and the default placeholder. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onClear` | `(() => void)` |  | Called after the clear button empties the field. |
| `shortcut` | `string` |  | The keyboard shortcut that focuses this field, shown as a key cap while the field is empty — e.g. `⌘K` or `/`. Display only: binding the key is the page's job, because only the page knows what else is listening. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | Height and type from the density tokens. |

`className` goes to `<div data-slot="input-group">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input data-slot="input">`.

#### `fieldControlClasses` (constant)

The one class list every text control shares — Input, Textarea, the Select trigger, and the wrapper an adorned Input draws instead of the input itself.

#### `fieldControlSizes` (constant)

Height, padding and type per size. Every value is a density token, so the same `md` input is 28px in a compact table filter and 48px in a spacious form without a prop changing.

Types: `LabelMessages`, `SearchInputMessages`, `LabelProps`, `FieldControlProps`, `FieldProps`, `FieldControlSize`, `InputProps`, `TextareaProps`, `SearchInputProps`

Used in: Agent hand-off, Empty states, Filtered table, Form validation, Settings page

### File upload

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-file-upload--overview

A dropzone with a real Browse button, and a list of the files put there with their status and progress.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Attaching or uploading files, such as an evaluation dataset or documents for the knowledge index.
- Driving the transfer with onUpload, which queues, uploads, reports progress and retries each file.
- Stating the accepted types and the size limit before a file is chosen.

When not to:
- Attaching files to the next prompt in a conversation. Use Prompt input.
- Pasting text, such as a JSON override; a field is faster than a file. Use Textarea.

- Do: Reject a file in a sentence that names it and the rule: “budget.xlsx is 14.2 MB; the limit is 10 MB.”
- Don’t: Say “Invalid file”, or add a rejected file to the list.

Content:
- When onUpload rejects, its error message is one sentence the person can act on; it is shown under the row.
- Pass messages to translate the copy, and keep each rule stated with its figure.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the browse button, then to each file’s Retry and Remove buttons.
- Enter or Space: Opens the system file picker, or runs the focused Retry or Remove.

Mola handles:
- The keyboard path is a real `<button>`; the dropzone is not a fake button.
- The accepted types and size limit are part of the button’s description, ahead of `Field`’s hint.
- Drag state, added files, rejections and finished uploads are said in a polite status region, and each rejection is a sentence naming the file and the rule.
- Removing a row moves focus to a neighbouring Remove button, or back to the browse button, never to the page.

You handle:
- Name it: put it in a `Field`, or pass `aria-label`; alone it is named only “Browse”.
- Pass `messages` to translate every announcement and button name.

#### `FileUpload` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the DOM props go to the root, the region that holds the dropzone and the list; the Field wiring (`id`, `aria-*`) goes to the browse button, the control a label names.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `accept` | `string` |  | Native `accept`: `.jsonl,.csv`, `application/pdf`, `image/*`. Enforced on drop as well as in the picker. |
| `aria-describedby` | `string` |  | Ids of the hint and error, from `Field`. Joined with the drop zone's own constraints text on the browse button. |
| `aria-invalid` | `boolean \| "true" \| "false"` |  | Marks the browse button invalid and draws the drop zone in the danger band. `Field` sets it while it shows an error. |
| `aria-label` | `string` |  | Accessible name of the browse button when there is no `Field` label to name it. |
| `defaultFiles` | `FileUploadItem[]` |  | Rows to start with: files already on the server, or a fixed state for a demo. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Stated, not faded: the drop zone goes to the shaded cloth, drops are refused, and the browse, retry and remove buttons are disabled. |
| `id` | `string` |  | Id of the browse button, so `Field`'s label points at the control a person presses. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | Locale for sizes and lists. Omit to use the runtime's locale. |
| `maxConcurrent` | `number` | `2` | How many `onUpload` calls run at once. |
| `maxFiles` | `number` |  | Total files in the list, including ones already there. |
| `maxSize` | `number` |  | Bytes, per file. |
| `messages` | `{ list?: string; status?: MessagesOverride<{ attached: string; queued: string; uploading: string; done: string; failed: string; }> \| Record<FileUploadStatus, string>; ... 19 more ...; constraints?: ((types: string \| null, limit: string \| null, maxFiles: number \| undefined) => string \| null) \|...` |  | Words for translation: every prompt, status word, button name and announcement, as templates. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. A function in a key (the shape before 0.7.0) still works, and is deprecated. |
| `multiple` | `boolean` | `false` | Accept several files, added to the list. Off by default: a new file replaces the one there and cancels its upload. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Submitted with a form when there is no `onUpload`. |
| `onFilesChange` | `((files: FileUploadItem[]) => void)` |  | Every change to the list: added, progressed, finished, failed, removed. |
| `onReject` | `((rejections: FileRejection[]) => void)` |  | Files turned away, with the rule each broke. |
| `onUpload` | `((file: File, context: UploadContext) => Promise<void>)` |  | Upload one file. Resolve when done; reject with an Error whose message says why. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Makes the form refuse to submit while the list is empty, through the hidden file input. `Field` passes it with its label's mark. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="file-upload">`.

#### `defaultFileUploadMessages` (constant)

The English words as functions.

#### `describeAccept` (function)

The accepted types as a person would say them — ".jsonl or .csv" — joined with the locale's own "or". MIME types are kept as written; a reader who sees `application/pdf` can still act on it, and inventing friendly names for every type is a table nobody maintains.

#### `formatFileSize` (function)

A byte count in the reader's locale: "14.2 MB", "14,2 MB", "١٤٫٢ م.ب".

#### `matchesAccept` (function)

Whether a file matches an `accept` string, using the rules the native picker uses: `.ext` against the name, `type/*` against the MIME prefix, an exact MIME type against the type. The picker's filter is advice — every platform lets the person switch it to "All files", and drag and drop skips it entirely — so the component checks again rather than trusting it.

#### `validateFiles` (function)

Splits a batch into the files that pass and the ones that do not, each with the one rule it broke. Type is checked before size, because "wrong kind of file" is the more useful thing to hear about a 2 GB video dropped on a CSV field. Count is checked last and in order, so the first files chosen are the ones kept.

Types: `FileUploadMessageTemplates`, `FileUploadItem`, `FileUploadMessageFunctions`, `FileUploadMessages`, `FileUploadProps`, `FileUploadStatus`, `UploadContext`, `FileRejection`, `FileRejectionReason`, `FileValidationOptions`

Used in: Form validation

Changes:
- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

### Form

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-form--overview

A form that knows which fields are failing: FormErrorSummary lists them as links and takes focus on submit; Fieldset groups controls under one legend.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Any form saved as a whole, where Save can find more than one problem at once: put a FormErrorSummary at the top.
- Moving focus after a failed submit without writing it by hand: to the summary, or to the first invalid field when there is none.
- Several controls that answer one question, such as a pair of limits or a time window, with Fieldset and its legend.
- A rule a group breaks as a whole, such as “the last retry must wait longer than the first”, with the Fieldset’s error.

When not to:
- One control with its label, hint and error. Use Field.
- A set of checkboxes; CheckboxGroup is already a fieldset, and takes an error too. Use CheckboxGroup.
- A long task split into steps done in order. Use Stepper.
- A message about the page rather than about the fields in it. Use Callout.

- Do: Validate on submit, then re-check only the fields already showing an error as they change.
- Don’t: Show errors on every keystroke; an error about a value nobody has finished typing is noise.
- Do: Write each error so it makes sense in the summary, away from its field: “Enter an https:// address”.
- Don’t: Rely on the field around it: “Invalid” in a list of links says nothing.

Content:
- The summary title counts what to fix: “3 fields need fixing”. Pass title to word it otherwise or translate it.
- Use description for what happens next: “Nothing has been saved yet.”
- Legends are short nouns, like labels: “Retry delays”, “Run window”.

Keyboard:
- Enter: In a text field, submits the form; if it finds errors, moves focus to the error summary.
- Tab: From the summary, moves through its links, one per error.
- Enter: On a summary link, moves focus to that field’s control, or the first control of a group, and scrolls its label into view.

Mola handles:
- On the submit that finds errors, focus moves to the `FormErrorSummary`, a region named by its heading (“3 fields need fixing”). The focus move is the one announcement; the summary is not also a live region.
- While a summary is mounted, each field’s error drops `role="alert"` and stays in its control’s `aria-describedby`, so three errors are not three alerts.
- Without a summary, focus moves to the first invalid control in document order, and field errors keep `role="alert"`.
- Each summary entry is a real link to the control’s id, reading “Label: message”, listed in document order.
- `Fieldset` is a real `<fieldset>` named by its legend; its description and error are its `aria-describedby`, and `disabled` disables every Base UI control inside.

You handle:
- Validate in `onSubmit` and set each field’s `error`; the form does not decide what is wrong.
- Write errors that make sense away from their field, since the summary lists them on their own.
- For errors that arrive later, such as from a server, call `focus()` on the summary’s ref: only a submit moves focus by itself.
- Give the form a name with `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` when the page has more than one.

Anatomy:
- Seam (`[data-part="seam"]`): The rojo relleno a danger callout opens with: this block is about something that went wrong.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): Counts what to fix. It names the summary, so it is what a screen reader says when focus lands here.
- Description (`[data-part="description"]`): What happens next, or what was kept: “Nothing has been saved yet.”
- Error links (`[data-part="list"]`): One link per error, “Label: message”, in the order of the form; each moves focus to its field.

#### `Form` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `onSubmit` | `FormEventHandler<HTMLFormElement>` |  | Runs on submit, before focus is moved. Validate here and set your fields' errors; call `event.preventDefault()` unless you want the browser to post the form. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<form data-slot="form">`.

#### `FormErrorSummary` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | A sentence under the heading: what happens next, or what is kept. |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4` | `2` | The heading element. Match the form's outline; `h2` by default. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<FormErrorSummaryMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default heading, by count. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `title` | `ReactNode \| ((count: number) => ReactNode)` |  | The heading, which is also the summary's accessible name and what a screen reader says when it takes focus. Pass a function to word it for the count. Defaults to “1 field needs fixing” / “3 fields need fixing” (`messages.title`, which is where a translation goes). |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="form-error-summary">`.

#### `Fieldset` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `legend` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The question the controls answer, in the label register. It names the group. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Copy under the legend, read when focus enters the group. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables every control in the group. |
| `error` | `ReactNode` |  | A rule the group breaks as a whole. Marks the group invalid and is listed by `FormErrorSummary`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<fieldset data-slot="fieldset">`.

Types: `FormErrorSummaryMessages`, `FormProps`, `FormErrorSummaryProps`, `FieldsetProps`

Used in: Form validation, Settings page

### Listbox

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-listbox--overview

A list of options that stays on the page, for choosing one or several without opening anything.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Choosing several values from a short set that should stay in view, such as the models an evaluation compares.
- One value from a list the screen has room to show, where seeing every option is the point, such as a data region.
- Options that need a second line, such as a region’s code or a model’s context window.

When not to:
- One value from a list in a form where space is tight. Use Select.
- A list long enough that people would rather type. Use Combobox.
- Two to five options that each need a sentence. Use Radio.
- Filters over a table or a list. Use Chip.
- Values a person adds and removes, shown as tags. Use Tag group.

- Do: Name it with aria-labelledby pointing at the visible label above it.
- Don’t: Put buttons, links or inputs inside an option; an option is one thing to choose.
- Do: Keep an unavailable option in the list and say why in its description.
- Don’t: Remove options that cannot be chosen today; people look for them and conclude they do not exist.

Content:
- Options are names, in sentence case: “Panama City”, not “PANAMA CITY” or “Region: Panama City”.
- Put codes and figures in the description, not the label, so type-ahead matches what people read first.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the chosen option, or the first, and Tab again leaves the list.
- ArrowDown or ArrowUp: Moves focus to the next or previous option; in a single list, also chooses it.
- Home or End: Moves focus to the first or last option; in a single list, also chooses it.
- Space: Chooses the focused option; in a multiple list, toggles it.
- Shift+ArrowDown or Shift+ArrowUp: In a multiple list, moves focus and toggles the option it lands on.
- Shift+Space: In a multiple list, chooses every option from the last one chosen to the focused one.
- Control+Shift+Home or Control+Shift+End: In a multiple list, chooses every option from the focused one to the first or last, and moves there.
- Control+A or Meta+A: In a multiple list, chooses every option, or clears them all when all are chosen.

Mola handles:
- Renders `role="listbox"` with `aria-multiselectable` when several can be chosen, and `option`s with `aria-selected`.
- One tab stop with roving focus on the options themselves, so the focus ring and scrolling into view are the browser’s own.
- Typing a letter moves to the next option that starts with it; letters typed together match a word.
- Each option is named by its label and described by its second line, so the name stays short.
- A disabled option is focusable, `aria-disabled` and never chosen, so a keyboard user learns it exists.
- A chosen option is an ink fill with a check or a filled square, never the colour alone.

You handle:
- Name it: pass `aria-labelledby` pointing at a visible label, or `aria-label`.
- Say why an option is disabled, in its description or next to the list.
- Announce the consequence of a choice yourself if it changes the page, such as a count in a live region.

Anatomy:
- List (`[data-slot="listbox"]`): The cut box the options sit in. It scrolls when given a height, and holds one tab stop.
- Option (`[data-part="option"]`): One choice. A chosen one takes the ink fill; focus is a ring drawn inside it.
- Mark (`[data-part="mark"]`): In a multiple list, a square that fills when chosen, so the state is never the colour alone.
- Label (`[data-part="label"]`): The option’s name, and what type-ahead matches.
- Description (`[data-part="description"]`): An optional second line, read as the option’s description.

#### `Listbox` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the list, which wears the cut.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `options` (required) | `ListboxOption<V>[]` |  | The options, in the order they are shown and reported. |
| `defaultValue` | `string \| V[] \| null` |  | The option chosen at first, when the list keeps its own value. The options chosen at first, when the list keeps its own value. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Every option is shown and none can be chosen or focused, as a disabled native list. |
| `multiple` | `boolean` |  | Leave unset (or `false`) for one choice, where moving with the arrows chooses. Several choices: the arrows only move, and Space toggles the focused option. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: V \| null) => void) \| ((value: V[]) => void)` |  | Called with the newly chosen option, only when it changes. Called with every chosen option, in the order of `options`, when the set changes. |
| `value` | `string \| V[] \| null` |  | The chosen option, when you control it; `null` is "nothing chosen yet". The chosen options, in the order of `options`, when you control them. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="listbox">`.

Types: `ListboxCommonProps`, `ListboxMultipleProps`, `ListboxOption`, `ListboxProps`, `ListboxSingleProps`

### Number input

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-number-input--overview

A number you can type and step, formatted for a locale, with bounds, a unit and steppers inside the field.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- An exact value with bounds, such as a token budget, a retry count or a timeout.
- Currency, percentages and units Intl knows, through format.
- A short unit suffix such as “tokens”, which is also added to the description.

When not to:
- A value where its place in a range matters more than the exact figure, such as temperature. Use Slider.
- An identifier made of digits, such as a run number; it is text, not a quantity. Use Input.
- A date or a period. Use Date picker.

- Do: Pass locale and a format, so “12,000 tokens” reads as a number.
- Don’t: Use a native number input, which formats nothing and changes value when the page scrolls.
- Do: Leave the wheel off in forms, and set allowWheelScrub only on a field that is the point of its screen.
- Don’t: Let scrolling past a field change its value.

Content:
- Labels name the quantity and its scope: “Token budget per run”, “Retries before halting”.
- Errors give the bound: “The workspace allows at most 8 concurrent runs.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the input. The − and + buttons are not tab stops.
- ArrowUp or ArrowDown: Steps by `step`, with Shift by `largeStep` and with Alt by `smallStep`.
- PageUp or PageDown: Steps by `largeStep`, within the bounds.
- Home or End: Jumps to `min` or `max`.

Mola handles:
- Base UI’s NumberField formats and parses with `Intl.NumberFormat` for the `locale`, and clamps to `min` and `max`.
- The steppers are named “Increase” and “Decrease”, and the one at a bound is disabled.
- A `unit` is added to the input’s description, so the number is heard with it.
- The wheel changes the value only when `allowWheelScrub` is set, so scrolling a form never edits it.

You handle:
- Name it: put it in a `Field`, or pass `label` or `aria-label`.
- Translate `incrementLabel` and `decrementLabel`.

#### `NumberInput` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `allowOutOfRange` | `boolean` |  | Keep a typed value outside `min`/`max` instead of clamping it on commit, so form validation can say why it is wrong. Off by default; the steppers still stop at the bounds. |
| `allowWheelScrub` | `boolean` |  | Let the wheel change a focused, hovered field. Off by default; see above. |
| `decrementLabel` | `string` |  | Accessible name of the − stepper. Defaults to "Decrease"; translate it with the page. |
| `defaultValue` | `number` |  | The number on first render, uncontrolled. Omitted, the field starts empty. |
| `format` | `NumberFormatOptions` |  | `Intl.NumberFormat` options: grouping, decimals, a unit or a currency. |
| `incrementLabel` | `string` |  | Accessible names of the steppers. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | Visible label above the field. Omit inside `Field`, which renders its own. |
| `largeStep` | `number` | `10` | Shift+Arrow and PageUp/PageDown step. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | BCP 47 tag for parsing and formatting. `undefined` uses the runtime's locale. |
| `max` | `number` |  | The highest value. Stepping and committed values clamp to it, and the increase stepper is disabled there; End jumps to it. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<NumberInputMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the steppers' default names. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `min` | `number` |  | The lowest value. Stepping and committed values clamp to it, and the decrease stepper is disabled there; Home jumps to it. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: number \| null) => void)` |  | Called as the number changes, typed or stepped, with `null` when the field is emptied. For a save or a request, use `onValueCommitted`, which does not fire per keystroke. |
| `onValueCommitted` | `((value: number \| null, eventDetails: NumberFieldRootCommitEventDetails) => void)` |  | Called when the value is committed: on blur, Enter or a stepper press, not per keystroke. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Control height, shared with Input and Select so the field lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `smallStep` | `number` |  | Alt+Arrow step. |
| `snapOnStep` | `boolean` |  | Round to the nearest multiple of the step when stepping, so 13 stepped by 5 goes to 15 rather than 18. Off by default. |
| `step` | `number \| "any"` |  | ArrowUp/Down step. |
| `unit` | `string` |  | Short unit suffix inside the cut: "tokens", "ms", "%". Also added to the accessible description. |
| `value` | `number \| null` |  | The number, controlled. `null` is an empty field; leave it `undefined` to let the field hold its own state. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the wrapper that holds `label` and the field. Only rendered with a `label`. |

`className` goes to `<div data-slot="number-input">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input>`.

Types: `NumberInputMessages`, `NumberInputProps`

Used in: Form validation, Settings page

Changes:
- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

### Radio

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-radio--overview

One choice from a small set, all visible, in a named RadioGroup; square, and told from a checkbox by its mark.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Exactly one of two to five options that people should compare side by side.
- Options that each need a description, such as what happens when a tool call fails.

When not to:
- Any number of options. Use Checkbox.
- More options than fit comfortably, or options that do not need comparing. Use Select.
- Switching views, where the change is instant and nothing is submitted. Use Segmented control.
- A single setting that is on or off. Use Switch.

- Do: Give the group a label that asks the question: “When a tool call fails”.
