- Don’t: Leave the group unnamed; a screen reader then announces “group” and nothing else.

Content:
- Option labels are parallel and start the same way: “Retry, then continue”, “Skip the step”, “Halt the run”.
- The consequence goes in description: “The run is marked failed and the owner is paged.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus into the group at the checked radio, and Tab again leaves the group.
- ArrowDown or ArrowRight: Moves to and checks the next radio, wrapping at the end.
- ArrowUp or ArrowLeft: Moves to and checks the previous radio.
- Space: Checks the focused radio. Enter does not.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders `role="radio"` inside `role="radiogroup"`, so the square is still announced as a radio button.
- `RadioGroup` is named by its visible `label` through `aria-labelledby`, and described by its `description`.
- Each radio is named by its `label` alone, and its `description` arrives through `aria-describedby`.

You handle:
- Give `RadioGroup` a `label` that asks the question the options answer.
- Give every radio a `label`.

#### `Radio` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the DOM props go to the control, with or without a `label`; the row a `label` adds takes `wrapperClassName`. `className` used to move to that row when a label was set.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the circle itself, with or without a `label`. A string, not Base UI's state function: use the `data-checked` and `data-disabled` variants for state. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Second line under the label. Linked as the radio's description, before any hint or error `Field` adds. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | Visible label. The whole label is the press target and names the radio. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the label row a `label` adds around the control. `className` always styles the control itself. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`, `RadioRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="radio">`.

#### `RadioGroup` (component)

The group, named by a visible label in the rótulo register via `aria-labelledby` — a radiogroup with no name is announced as "group" and nothing else, which leaves a screen reader user choosing between options to a question they never heard.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The question the options answer. Names the radiogroup. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the wrapper that holds the label, the description and the group. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Copy under the label, linked as the group's description so it is heard on entering the group. |
| `orientation` | `"horizontal" \| "vertical"` | `vertical` | Lay the options out in a column (the default) or a wrapping row. |

Also accepts the props of `RadioGroupProps`.

`className` goes to `<div data-slot="radio-group-field">`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div>`.

Types: `RadioGroupProps`, `RadioProps`

Used in: Form validation, Settings page

### Segmented control

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-segmented-control--overview

One choice from a few views or settings, all visible and switched instantly, cut from one piece.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Switching between views of the same content: Table, Board, Timeline.
- A setting with two to five short options that applies at once, such as density.
- Icon-only segments in a tight space, with the labels kept as accessible names.

When not to:
- More than five options, or options that need explaining. Use Select.
- A choice that is submitted with a form. Use Radio.
- Panels of different content, each with its own heading. Use Tabs.
- Filters that can be combined. Use Chip.

- Do: Always have one segment selected; pressing the selected one does nothing.
- Don’t: Use segments as a row of separate action buttons.

Content:
- Labels are one word where possible: “Table”, “Board”.
- Name the group with aria-label: “View”, “Density”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus into the control, and Tab again leaves it.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowRight: Moves focus between segments, wrapping at the ends.
- Home or End: Moves focus to the first or last segment.
- Enter or Space: Chooses the focused segment.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders a group of `aria-pressed` buttons with roving focus.
- The chosen segment is an ink fill, and pressing it again does not empty the control.
- With `iconOnly`, each segment is named by its `label` and the icon is hidden from assistive technology.

You handle:
- Pass `aria-label` to name the group, such as “View”.
- Give `label` as a string when you use `iconOnly`, so the segment has a name.

#### `SegmentedControl` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the group.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `options` (required) | `SegmentedOption<V>[]` |  | The segments, in order: a `value`, a `label` and optionally an icon or `disabled`. Two to five short ones; past that, use a Select. |
| `defaultValue` | `string` |  | The segment pressed on first render, uncontrolled. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Disables every segment. To disable one, set `disabled` on its option instead. |
| `fill` | `boolean` | `false` | Segments share the full width equally. |
| `iconOnly` | `boolean` | `false` | Render icons only; labels become the accessible names. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: V) => void)` |  | Called with the newly pressed segment's `value`. Pressing the segment that is already pressed does not call it, so the group never empties. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md"` | `md` | Height: `md` (the default) matches the other controls in a row; `sm` for a toolbar or a dense table header. |
| `value` | `string` |  | The pressed segment's `value`, controlled. Give one: a segmented control always has a choice made. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="segmented-control">`.

Types: `SegmentedControlBaseProps`, `SegmentedControlProps`, `SegmentedOption`

### Select

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-select--overview

A choice from a list in a popup, with an optional second line per option and labelled groups.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- One value from a list short enough to scan without typing, such as an environment or a model.
- Options that need a second line, such as a model’s speed and cost.
- A field in a form, inside Field, or a compact filter in a toolbar.

When not to:
- A list long enough that people would rather type. Use Combobox.
- Two to five options that should all be visible. Use Radio.
- Switching views instantly. Use Segmented control.
- A list of actions rather than a value. Use Menu.
- Several values, or a list the page has room to keep in view. Use Listbox.

- Do: Keep a retired option in the list, disabled, with the reason as its description.
- Don’t: Remove an option someone may look for without saying where it went.

Content:
- Option labels are the names people use, “Cayuco Steady 3”; the description adds the trade-off, “Balanced · default”.
- The placeholder says what to choose: “Choose an environment”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the trigger.
- Enter or Space or ArrowDown or ArrowUp: Opens the list.
- ArrowDown or ArrowUp: Moves the highlight through the options.
- Home or End: Highlights the first or last option.
- Enter or Space: Chooses the highlighted option and closes the list.
- Escape: Closes the list without changing the value.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders the trigger as a `combobox` and the list as a `listbox` of `option`s with `aria-selected`.
- Typing a letter moves to the matching option, and on a closed trigger chooses it.
- The current value is a check and a wash, the highlight an ink fill, so the two are never the same mark.
- `label` is wired to the trigger with a real `<label>`, and `Field` passes its description and invalid state.

You handle:
- Name it: put it in a `Field`, or pass `label` or `aria-label`.
- Give a `placeholder` that says what to choose when there is no value.

#### `Select` (component)

The one-line form, for the nine selects in ten that are a label and a list. The parts above are exported for the tenth.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `SelectOption<V>[] \| SelectOptionGroup<V>[]` |  | Flat options, or labelled groups. |
| `className` | `string` |  | CSS class applied to the element, or a function that returns a class based on the component's state. |
| `defaultValue` | `string \| null` |  | The option `value` selected on first render, uncontrolled. `null` or omitted shows the `placeholder`. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Stated, not faded: the trigger goes to the shaded cloth and the list cannot open. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | A visible label rendered above the trigger. Omit it when the select sits inside `Field`, which renders its own — and spread Field's control props onto this component instead. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<SelectMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default placeholder. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Form field name. The selected option's `value` is submitted under it through a hidden input. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: V \| null) => void)` |  | Called with the chosen option's `value` when the selection changes. |
| `placeholder` | `ReactNode` |  | Shown in the trigger while nothing is selected: "Choose a model". Not a label; the field still needs one. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Marks the select required for form validation, and the `label`, when there is one, with the required mark. |
| `rootProps` | `Omit<Props<V, false>, "defaultValue" \| "children" \| "value" \| "items" \| "onValueChange">` |  | Props for Base UI's `Select.Root`, for what the one-line form does not name: `open`, `onOpenChange`, `modal`, `readOnly`. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Trigger height, shared with Input so a select lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `value` | `string \| null` |  | The selected option's `value`, controlled. `null` is no selection, which shows the `placeholder`; leave it `undefined` to let the select hold its own state. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the wrapper that holds `label` and the trigger. Only rendered with a `label`. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="select-trigger">`.

#### `SelectContent` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | CSS class applied to the element, or a function that returns a class based on the component's state. |
| `sideOffset` | `number` | `6` | Gap between trigger and popup, in px. |

Also accepts the props of `SelectPopupProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="select-content">`.

#### `SelectGroup` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="select-group">`.

#### `SelectGroupLabel` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="select-group-label">`.

#### `SelectItem` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | CSS class applied to the element, or a function that returns a class based on the component's state. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Secondary line under the label: a model's context window, a team's size. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`, `SelectItemProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="select-item">`.

#### `SelectRoot` (component)

A select is two shapes in this system, and they are different materials.

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `SelectSeparator` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="select-separator">`.

#### `SelectTrigger` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | CSS class applied to the element, or a function that returns a class based on the component's state. |
| `placeholder` | `ReactNode` |  | Shown when there is no value. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Trigger height, shared with Input so a select lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`, `SelectTriggerProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="select-trigger">`.

#### `SelectValue` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="select-value">`.

Types: `SelectMessages`, `SelectContentProps`, `SelectItemProps`, `SelectOption`, `SelectOptionGroup`, `SelectProps`, `SelectTriggerProps`

Used in: Form validation, Settings page

### Slider

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-slider--overview

A value or a range on a continuous scale, with the value always printed in tabular figures.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A value where the range matters as much as the number, such as temperature or a rollout percentage.
- A range between two values, with two thumbs.
- A scale whose ends need words, through minLabel and maxLabel.

When not to:
- An exact figure someone will type, such as a budget of 40,000 tokens. Use Number input.
- A reading the person cannot change. Use Meter.
- A few discrete options. Use Segmented control.

- Do: Format the printed value with its unit, through formatValue: “25% of runs”.
- Don’t: Turn showValue off, so the value can only be learned by dragging.

Content:
- End labels describe the effect at each end, not the number: “Precise”, “Creative”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the thumb, or to each thumb of a range in turn.
- ArrowRight or ArrowUp: Increases by `step`, with Shift by `largeStep`; ArrowLeft increases in right-to-left.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowDown: Decreases by `step`, with Shift by `largeStep`.
- PageUp or PageDown: Changes by `largeStep`.
- Home or End: Jumps to `min` or `max`.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders a real `<input type="range">` in each thumb, with `role="slider"` and the value in `aria-valuenow` and `aria-valuetext`.
- The `label` names every thumb.
- The value is printed beside the label, so it is never learned only by dragging.

You handle:
- Give it a `label`; without one the thumb has no name.
- The `minLabel` and `maxLabel` are hidden from assistive technology; if they carry meaning, say it in text near the slider.

#### `Slider` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the root, which holds the label row, the track and the end labels. |
| `formatValue` | `((formatted: string, value: number) => ReactNode)` |  | Format the printed value. Defaults to Base UI's locale formatting via `format`. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | Visible label. Names the thumb(s). |
| `maxLabel` | `ReactNode` |  | Label under the high end of the track. Decorative: the value text, not this, is what a screen reader hears. |
| `minLabel` | `ReactNode` |  | Labels under the two ends of the track: "Precise" / "Creative". |
| `showValue` | `boolean` | `true` | Print the current value beside the label. |

Also accepts the props of `SliderRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="slider">`.

Types: `SliderProps`

Used in: Form validation

### Switch

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-switch--overview

A setting that takes effect the moment it is switched; on is verde, the colour of live.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Turning a capability on or off at once: streaming, a tool, a flag.
- Settings lists, with labelPosition set to start so labels align left and switches right.

When not to:
- A choice that waits for a Save button. Use Checkbox.
- A choice between two named options rather than on and off. Use Radio.
- A pressed state in a row of controls, such as Wrap lines. Use Toolbar.

- Do: Apply the change as soon as the switch moves, and say in the description what it affects.
- Don’t: Mix switches and checkboxes in a form that is saved later; a switch says it has already happened.

Content:
- Label the capability, not the action: “Stream responses”, not “Turn on streaming”.
- The description states the effect or the cost: “Stored for 30 days; may contain user text.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the switch.
- Space or Enter: Turns it on or off.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders `role="switch"` with `aria-checked`.
- The `label` names the control and the whole label toggles it; the `description` arrives through `aria-describedby`.
- On is stated three ways: the square moves end to end, the track turns verde, and the keyline stays.

You handle:
- Give every switch a `label`, or an `aria-label` when it stands alone.
- Use a switch only for a setting that takes effect at once; a choice that waits for Save is a checkbox.

#### `Switch` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the DOM props go to the switch, with or without a `label`; see `Checkbox`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the switch itself, with or without a `label`. A string, not Base UI's state function: use the `data-checked` and `data-disabled` variants for state. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | Second line under the label: what turning it on does. Linked as the switch's description. |
| `label` | `ReactNode` |  | Visible label. The whole label is the press target and names the switch. |
| `labelPosition` | `"start" \| "end"` | `end` | Put the label before the switch — for settings lists, where labels align left and switches right. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the label row a `label` adds around the control. `className` always styles the switch itself. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`, `SwitchRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="switch">`.

Types: `SwitchProps`

Used in: Form validation, Settings page

### Tag group

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-tag-group--overview

A set of short values shown as tags, which can be removed, and selected when the set is also a choice.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- The labels on a record, the recipients of a report, the tools an agent may call: a set someone reads and trims.
- A short set that is also a choice, such as which label a report groups by, with selectionMode.
- Showing what a search or a form added, with a way to take each one back.

When not to:
- Filters that narrow a table or a list. Use Chip.
- Picking values from a long list while typing. Use Combobox.
- A status on a record that nobody edits. Use Badge.
- Choosing from options that should stay in view, with descriptions. Use Listbox.

- Do: Remove the item in your own state from onRemove, and let focus move to the next tag.
- Don’t: Ask for confirmation on every removal; offer a way to restore instead.
- Do: Keep a locked tag in the set with its name, so people see why it stays.
- Don’t: Use a tag group as a row of buttons that act; a tag is a value.

Content:
- Tags are one to three words, in sentence case: “Contains PII”, “Q3 review”.
- Name the group for what the tags are: “Dataset labels”, “Recipients”.
- Say what an empty group means in empty: “No labels. This dataset is not grouped anywhere.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the tag last focused, the first at the start, and Tab again leaves the group.
- ArrowRight or ArrowDown: Moves focus to the next tag, stopping at the last; ArrowLeft in right-to-left.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowUp: Moves focus to the previous tag, stopping at the first.
- Home or End: Moves focus to the first or last tag.
- Delete or Backspace: Removes the focused tag when the group is removable, and focuses the tag that took its place.
- Space or Enter: Selects or deselects the focused tag when the group is selectable.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `grid` of `row`s, one per tag, each named by its label, so the whole set is one tab stop.
- A removable tag says so with `aria-keyshortcuts`, and its × is a real button named “Remove <label>”, kept out of the tab order because the row answers Delete.
- After a removal, focus moves to the next tag, the previous one at the end, or the group once it is empty, never to the page body.
- A selectable group sets `aria-selected` on every tag and `aria-multiselectable` when several can be chosen; a selected tag is an ink fill with a check.
- An empty group says so in words, in a cell, rather than leaving a blank gap.

You handle:
- Name it with `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`: “Dataset labels”.
- Remove the item from your own list in `onRemove`; the group does not own it.
- Translate `removeLabel` and `empty`.

Anatomy:
- Tag (`[data-part="tag"]`): One value: a focusable row, cut like a chip. Selected, it takes the ink fill.
- Check (`[data-part="check"]`): Marks a selected tag, beside the ink fill.
- Label (`[data-part="label"]`): The tag’s text, and its accessible name.
- Remove (`[data-part="remove"]`): The ×: a button named “Remove <label>”, out of the tab order because the tag answers Delete.
- Empty text (`[data-part="empty"]`): What the group says when it has no tags.

#### `TagGroup` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the grid.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `TagGroupItem[]` |  | The tags, in order. The group shows them; your state owns them. |
| `defaultValue` | `string[]` |  | The tags selected at first, when the group keeps its own selection. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | No tag can be removed or selected; they stay readable. |
| `empty` | `ReactNode` |  | What the group says when it has no tags. Kept as text, so an empty group is never a blank gap. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<TagGroupMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the remove buttons' default name and the empty text. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onRemove` | `((value: string) => void)` |  | Makes the tags removable: an × on each, and Delete or Backspace on the focused one. The group does not own the list; remove the item in your state and focus moves to its neighbour. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: string[]) => void)` |  | Called with every selected tag, in the order of `items`, when the selection changes. |
| `removeLabel` | `((label: string) => string)` |  | Accessible name of each remove button. |
| `selectionMode` | `"none" \| "multiple" \| "single"` | `none` | `none` (the default) for a set you only read or edit, `single` or `multiple` for a choice. |
| `value` | `string[]` |  | The selected tags. With `single`, at most one. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tag-group">`.

Types: `TagGroupMessages`, `TagGroupBaseProps`, `TagGroupItem`, `TagGroupProps`

### Time picker

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-forms-time-picker--overview

TimePicker for a time of day as hour and minute spinbuttons; DateTimePicker for a moment, a DatePicker and a TimePicker read as one Date.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A time of day with no date, such as when a nightly evaluation runs, with TimePicker.
- A moment, such as when a deploy starts, with DateTimePicker.
- Times with bounds, such as staffed hours, through min and max.

When not to:
- A date with no time. Use Date picker.
- A duration, such as a timeout in minutes. Use Number input.
- A handful of fixed slots, such as 09:00, 13:00 and 17:00. Use Segmented control.

- Do: Pass locale, so the clock, the day-period words and the separator follow it.
- Don’t: Force hourCycle to 24 for people whose locale writes a 12-hour clock, unless the product always shows one.
- Do: Say the time zone in the hint when it is not the reader’s own: “Evaluation workers run in UTC.”
- Don’t: Leave people to guess whose 09:00 it is.

Content:
- Label the field by what the time is for: “Run the nightly evaluation at”, “Deploy the new model at”.
- Rules the picker cannot know, such as required, go to Field as an instruction: “Choose a time for the pause.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves through the segments in order: hour, minute and day period, then the date input and calendar button in DateTimePicker.
- Shift+Tab: Moves back through the same stops.
- ArrowUp or ArrowDown: Steps the focused segment, wrapping at the ends; the minute moves by `minuteStep`.
- Home or End: Sets the focused segment to its first or last value.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowRight: Moves to the previous or next segment.
- Backspace or Delete: Empties the focused segment; Backspace in an empty one moves back.
- Enter: Reads the time and states why if it is refused.

Mola handles:
- The field is a `group` named by its label, and each segment a `spinbutton` named by the label and its word, “Deploy at Hour”, with `aria-valuetext` (“9”, “PM”, “Empty”).
- Digits type into a segment and move on when no further digit fits; a separator moves on, and the day period takes the first letter of the locale’s word.
- An incomplete or out-of-range time is a sentence in a `role="alert"` with a mark, not colour alone, and the value is kept until the time is valid.
- In DateTimePicker, the calendar keeps every key of the Date picker page, and a moment outside `min` or `max` is stated under both halves.

You handle:
- Name it: put it in a `Field`, or pass `aria-label`.
- Translate the segment words and sentences through `messages`.
- Say whose time zone a time is in when it is not the reader’s.

Anatomy:
- Field (`[data-slot="time-picker-field"]`): The cut shape of every text control: a `group` named by the label, with the focus ring around all of its segments.
- Hour (`[data-part="hour"]`): A spinbutton, 1–12 or 0–23 as the locale counts hours.
- Minute (`[data-part="minute"]`): A spinbutton, 0–59; Up and Down move by `minuteStep`.
- Day period (`[data-part="day-period"]`): AM or PM in the locale’s own words; only where the locale keeps a 12-hour clock.
- Icon (`[data-part="icon"]`): A clock, hidden from assistive technology; the label says what the field is.

#### `DateTimePicker` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` |  | Open the calendar on first render. |
| `defaultValue` | `Date \| null` | `null` | The moment shown on first render, uncontrolled. `null` or omitted starts empty. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Stated, not faded: both halves are disabled and go to the shaded cloth. |
| `hourCycle` | `12 \| 24` |  | Overrides the locale's clock. |
| `id` | `string` |  | Given to the date input, so a `<label for>` focuses it. |
| `isDateDisabled` | `((date: Date) => boolean)` |  | Days inside `min`–`max` that still cannot be chosen, as on DatePicker. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | BCP 47 tag for names, digits, the typed date order, the clock and the week start. |
| `max` | `Date \| null` |  | The latest moment, inclusive. Later days are unavailable in the calendar, and a later time on the same day is refused with a sentence under both halves. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<DateTimePickerMessages>` |  | Words for translation: "Date" and "Time", which name each half, and the time half's segment names and sentences. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. The date half's words come from the provider's `datePicker` table. |
| `min` | `Date \| null` |  | The earliest moment, inclusive. Earlier days are unavailable in the calendar, and an earlier time on the same day is refused with a sentence under both halves. |
| `minuteStep` | `number` |  | Minutes per Up or Down press in the minute segment. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Submitted as `yyyy-mm-ddThh:mm`, local time, as `<input type="datetime-local">` writes it. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the calendar opens or closes, as on DatePicker. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: Date \| null) => void)` |  | Called with the combined moment once both halves hold an allowed value, and with `null` when either is emptied. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the calendar is open, when the page owns that. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Marks both halves required for form validation. Inside `Field`, the label carries the required mark. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Control height for both halves, shared with Input. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `today` | `Date` |  | As on DatePicker: inject "now" for stable stories, and pass the server's date when server rendering. |
| `value` | `Date \| null` |  | The moment, controlled, in local time. `null` is none; leave it `undefined` to let the picker hold its own state. |
| `weekStartsOn` | `number` |  | 0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="date-time-picker">`.

#### `TimePicker` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `defaultValue` | `string \| null` | `null` | The time shown on first render, uncontrolled. `null` or omitted starts with every segment empty. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Stated, not faded: every segment is disabled and the field goes to the shaded cloth. |
| `hourCycle` | `12 \| 24` |  | Overrides the locale's clock, for a product that always shows one. |
| `id` | `string` |  | Given to the first segment, so a `<label for>` (as `Field` writes it) focuses it. |
| `locale` | `LocalesArgument` |  | BCP 47 tag for the clock (12- or 24-hour), the day-period words and the digits. `undefined` uses the runtime's locale. |
| `max` | `string \| null` |  | The latest allowed time, `HH:mm`. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<TimePickerMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the segment names and the sentences. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `min` | `string \| null` |  | The earliest allowed time, `HH:mm`. |
| `minuteStep` | `number` | `1` | Minutes per Up or Down press in the minute segment; typing still takes any minute. |
| `name` | `string` |  | Submitted as `HH:mm`, whatever the display locale. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: string \| null) => void)` |  | Called on blur or Enter with a complete, allowed time, or `null` when emptied. |
| `required` | `boolean` |  | Marks every segment required for form validation. Inside `Field`, the label carries the required mark. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Control height, shared with Input and DatePicker so the picker lines up in a form row. Defaults to `md`; density rescales every size. |
| `value` | `string \| null` |  | The time, controlled: `HH:mm`, 24-hour, as `<input type="time">` writes it; `null` for none. Leave it `undefined` to let the picker hold its own state. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the outer element that holds the field and its error. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="time-picker-field">`.

#### `getTimeLayout` (function)

Everything locale-specific about writing a time, read from `Intl` rather than from a table: whether the clock is 12- or 24-hour (es-PA writes `9:05 p. m.`, es-ES `21:05`), where the day period goes, and what separates the hour from the minute (`21.05` in Finnish).

Types: `DateTimePickerMessages`, `TimePickerMessages`, `DateTimePickerProps`, `TimePickerProps`, `TimeLayout`, `TimeSegment`, `TimeString`

## Layout

### App shell

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-app-shell--overview

The frame a tool lives in: a top bar, a sidebar that collapses to a rail or becomes a sheet, and one scrolling main region.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- The outer frame of an application with navigation, search and a work area.
- Navigation with sections, a current page and counts, through Sidebar, NavSection and NavItem.
- The product mark, search and account controls, through Topbar.
- A condition of the whole application above the top bar, through the banner slot and a Banner.

When not to:
- The title, description and actions at the top of one page inside the shell. Use Page header.
- Details beside the work, opened from a row. Use Drawer.
- Switching between views inside a page. Use Tabs.

- Do: Mark the current destination with active, and set countTone to danger only for things that need a person.
- Don’t: Fill every count in rojo; totals are not alarms.
- Do: Render each NavItem as your router’s link with render={<Link to="/runs" />}, so the shell navigates without reloading the page.
- Don’t: Wrap a NavItem in your own link, or pass a plain href in a single-page app; the first nests links, the second reloads the document.

Content:
- Navigation labels are short nouns: “Runs”, “Agents”, “Evaluations”.
- Give a count its countLabel, “3 failed”, so the number is not read alone.
- Name the Sidebar landmark: “Main”, “Settings”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the skip link first, then the sidebar toggle, the top bar and the navigation.
- Enter: Follows the skip link to the main region, or the focused navigation link.
- Enter or Space: Collapses or expands the sidebar, or opens the navigation sheet on a narrow screen.
- Escape: Closes the navigation sheet and returns focus to the button that opened it.

Mola handles:
- Gives the page a banner, a named navigation, a main region and a skip link to it.
- Reports the sidebar toggle's state with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`, and keeps item labels as screen-reader text when collapsed.
- Opens the sidebar on a narrow screen as a modal sheet titled Navigation, which traps focus and has a visible close button.
- Marks the active item with `aria-current="page"` and reads its count in words: Runs, 3 failed.

You handle:
- Give the sidebar a `label` that names the navigation.
- Give each page one `h1`, usually through `PageHeader`.
- Put a condition of the whole application in the `banner` slot, as a `Banner`, rather than above the shell.

#### `AppShell` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sidebar` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The navigation, usually a `Sidebar`. The same tree is drawn in the layout from 920px and in a modal sheet below it, so desktop and mobile navigation cannot drift. |
| `topbar` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The bar across the top, usually a `Topbar`. It stays put while `<main>` scrolls, so the switcher and search are never lost. |
| `banner` | `ReactNode` |  | A condition of the whole application, usually a `Banner`: drawn full width above the top bar, so it pushes the frame down rather than covering it. After the skip link in reading order, so the way to the content stays first. |
| `defaultSidebarCollapsed` | `boolean` | `false` | Start with the desktop sidebar collapsed to its icon rail. Defaults to `false`; ignored below 920px, where the sidebar is a sheet. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<AppShellMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the skip link, the phone sheet's heading and close button, and SidebarToggle's names, which read them from here. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onSidebarCollapsedChange` | `((collapsed: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the desktop sidebar collapses or expands — to remember the choice between visits. |
| `sidebarCollapsed` | `boolean` |  | Controlled collapse of the desktop sidebar. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="app-shell">`.

#### `SidebarToggle` (component)

The control that collapses the sidebar on desktop and opens the sheet on mobile. Two buttons, one shown per breakpoint, rather than one button that asks `matchMedia` what it is: CSS decides which exists, so the server render is already right and nothing flickers on hydration.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="sidebar-toggle">`.

#### `Topbar` (component)

The bar across the top. Raised cloth bounded by one ink rule — the single structural edge in the frame; the sidebar's edge is a quiet keyline, so the eye reads one horizontal plane over the work rather than a box around it.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `center` | `ReactNode` |  | Search, or a command-palette trigger. Centred in the free space. |
| `end` | `ReactNode` |  | Notifications, help, the signed-in user. |
| `start` | `ReactNode` |  | The product mark or switcher. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<header data-slot="topbar">`.

#### `NavItem` (component)

One destination. Active is an ink bar on the leading edge plus a washed ground and a heavier label — three cues, so it never depends on the wash alone, which nearly disappears on some displays.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` (required) | `string` |  | The visible label. A string, so it can also be the rail's tooltip. |
| `active` | `boolean` | `false` | The current page. Sets `aria-current="page"`. |
| `count` | `number` |  | A count beside the label: open items, failures. |
| `countLabel` | `string` |  | Read with the count: "3 failed". Without it the number is read alone. |
| `countTone` | `"neutral" \| "danger" \| "attention"` | `neutral` | `danger` fills the count in the danger colour — for failures that need a person, not for totals. `attention` is deprecated since 0.7.0 and removed in 1.0.0: use `danger`, the same fill under the status word every `tone` uses. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | A leading icon. Needed for the collapsed rail, where the icon is all that shows and the label becomes its tooltip. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<a data-slot="nav-item">`.

#### `NavSection` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` |  | The group's title, in the rótulo register. Names the list. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="nav-section">`.

#### `Sidebar` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | Names the navigation landmark: "Main", "Settings". |
| `footer` | `ReactNode` |  | Pinned below the navigation behind a keyline — the signed-in account, help, settings. |
| `header` | `ReactNode` |  | Pinned above the navigation and outside its scroll — a workspace switcher or the product's name. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<nav data-slot="sidebar-nav">`.

#### `SidebarContext` (constant)

Types: `AppShellMessages`, `AppShellProps`, `SidebarContextValue`, `SidebarToggleProps`, `TopbarProps`, `NavItemProps`, `NavSectionProps`, `SidebarProps`

Used in: Settings page

### Container

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-container--overview

The page column, in prose, page, wide and full widths; Section and SectionHeader set the rhythm down the page.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Holding a page to a readable or a working width, with gutters that move with density.
- Wide tables and dashboards, with size set to wide.
- Reading pages, with size set to prose, which never widens.
- Spacing sections down a page with Section, and titling them with SectionHeader.

When not to:
- A bounded region with its own ground and edge. Use Panel.
- Columns of cards or tiles. Use Grid.
- The outer frame of an application. Use App shell.

Content:
- A SectionHeader label goes under the heading and says what kind of section it is: “Evaluation suite”.

Mola handles:
- Renders a plain `<div>` with no role, so it adds nothing to what a screen reader hears.
- Keeps a side gutter at every width, so content never touches the screen edge.

You handle:
- Add the landmark yourself, such as `<main>`, when the container holds one.

#### `Container` (component)

- `width`: `page`, `wide`, `prose`, `full` (default `page`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` (deprecated) | `ContainerWidth` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `width`, which takes the same values. `codemods/prop-vocabulary.ts` rewrites call sites. |
| `width` | `"page" \| "wide" \| "prose" \| "full"` |  | The column's measure: `page` (default), `wide` for tools whose tables want the screen, `prose` for reading, `full` for no limit. The gutters stay either way. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="container">`.

#### `Section` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `divided` | `boolean` | `false` | A keyline between this section and the one above it. |
| `rhythm` | `"page" \| "panel"` | `panel` | `page` is the editorial 56/80/112px rhythm; `panel` is the tool rhythm. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="section">`.

#### `SectionHeader` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the outer `<div>`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The section's heading, rendered at `level`. |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` |  | Actions, aligned to the heading's baseline on the right. |
| `label` | `string` |  | The section's label. It renders UNDER the heading, not above it: a small label above a heading pre-announces what the heading is about to say better. Below, the same string reads as what kind of section this is. |
| `lead` | `ReactNode` |  | A sentence or two under the heading, set as a Lead, that says what the section is for. |
| `level` | `1 \| 2 \| 3` | `2` | The heading level, 2 by default. Set it from the outline of the page, not from the size you want. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="section-header">`.

#### `containerVariants` (function)

The page column. `page` is the editorial width the system was born at; `wide` is for tools, where a table with twelve columns deserves the screen; `prose` is the reading measure and never widens.

Types: `ContainerProps`, `ContainerWidth`, `SectionHeaderProps`, `SectionProps`

### Divider

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-divider--overview

Three dividers in rising weight and rarity: a keyline, a relleno seam and the sawtooth diente.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A keyline between rows or fields.
- A relleno seam between two major parts of a page.
- A diente only where the page gives way to a filled panel.

When not to:
- Sections that already have headings and space between them. Use Section.
- Groups of controls in a row. Use Toolbar.

- Do: Use the keyline by default, and relleno only where a hairline is too weak.
- Don’t: Put relleno between every card; the texture turns into wallpaper.

Content:
- A divider carries no words; when a division needs a name, give it a heading instead.

Mola handles:
- Is `role="none"` by default, so a screen reader does not announce a separator between every pair of rows.
- Sets `aria-orientation` when you pass `role="separator"`.

You handle:
- Pass `role="separator"` only when the division itself means something.

#### `Divider` (component)

The system's three dividers, in rising order of weight and rarity.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `band` | `DividerBand` | `oro` | The colour of a `relleno` divider's band, gold by default. Ignored by the other variants. |
| `orientation` | `"horizontal" \| "vertical"` | `horizontal` | `vertical` stretches to the height of a flex row, for a seam between columns or toolbar groups. `diente` is horizontal only. Announced only when you also pass `role="separator"`. |
| `up` | `boolean` | `false` | Teeth pointing up — for the top edge of a filled panel. |
| `variant` | `"keyline" \| "relleno" \| "diente"` | `keyline` | `keyline` — the decorative 1px rule. Between rows, between fields. `relleno` — the filler slit: a band of colour cut by fine ink lines. For the seam between two parts of a page, not between list items. `diente`  — the sawtooth left where one ground is cut away from another. For the major seam only, where the page gives way to a filled panel. Set `color` on the element to choose the ground. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="divider">`.

Types: `DividerBand`, `DividerProps`

### Grid

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-grid--overview

Column patterns that reflow; auto fits as many columns of at least minItem as there is room for.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Cards or tiles that reflow with no breakpoints, with cols set to auto and a minItem.
- Fixed patterns of halves, thirds, quarters or fifths.
- Cells cut from one panel that share keylines, with gap set to seamless.

When not to:
- Rows of records with the same fields. Use Table.
- Headline figures, which have their own group. Use Stat.
- One column or one row of items with a single gap. Use Stack.

Content:
- Keep tiles in a grid parallel: the same fields, in the same order, with the same units.

Mola handles:
- Renders a plain `<div>` and never reorders its children, so reading order is source order.
- Collapses to fewer columns on narrow screens, so a page does not scroll sideways.

You handle:
- Put children in the order they should be read.
- Pass `role="list"`, and `role="listitem"` on each cell, when the cells are a list of like things.

#### `Grid` (component)

- `cols`: `halves`, `thirds`, `quarters`, `fifths`, `auto` (default `thirds`)
- `gap`: `tight`, `default`, `wide`, `seamless` (default `default`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `cols` | `"halves" \| "thirds" \| "quarters" \| "fifths" \| "auto"` |  | The column pattern, one or two columns on a phone and more at each breakpoint; `thirds` by default. `auto` fits as many `minItem`-wide columns as the space allows and needs no breakpoint. |
| `gap` | `"tight" \| "default" \| "wide" \| "seamless"` |  | Space between cells, on the spacing scale. `seamless` closes the gap to a shared keyline, so the cells read as one panel cut into pieces. |
| `minItem` | `string` |  | For `cols="auto"`: the narrowest a cell may get, as a CSS length. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="grid">`.

#### `gridVariants` (function)

The responsive column patterns that recur. `auto` is the one a tool reaches for most: as many columns of at least `minItem` as fit, which is what a grid of stat tiles or agent cards actually wants — it never needs a breakpoint.

Types: `GridProps`

### Page header

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-page-header--overview

The top of a page inside the shell: a breadcrumb, the title, a description, actions, a row of facts and optional tabs.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Saying where the reader is, what the page is and what they can do on it.
- Short facts about the object, such as status, owner and last update, through meta.
- Tabs that switch views of the page, on the header’s bottom edge.

When not to:
- A title for a section inside a page. Use SectionHeader.
- A title that is the whole point of the screen, such as an onboarding panel. Use Display.

- Do: Keep the page’s main action in actions, where it wraps under the title on a narrow screen.
- Don’t: Hide the main action in an overflow menu.

Content:
- The title is the object’s name or the collection’s: “Runs”, “Support triage”.
- The description says what the page holds in one sentence.
- Meta items are short facts, not sentences: “Updated 12s ago”, “Retention 30 days”.

Mola handles:
- Renders the title as the page's `h1`.
- Renders `meta` as a list, so a screen reader says how many facts there are.

You handle:
- Use one page header per page, so the page has one `h1`.
- Pass a breadcrumb that is its own named navigation.

Anatomy:
- Header (`[data-slot="page-header"]`): Closes on a keyline, unless tabs sit at its foot.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): The page’s one h1.
- Description (`[data-part="description"]`): One sentence on what the page is for.
- Actions (`[data-part="actions"]`): The page’s actions. They wrap below the title on a narrow screen rather than hide in a menu.
- Meta (`[data-part="meta"]`): Status, owner and dates, as a list divided by keylines.

#### `PageHeader` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The page's name, rendered as its one `h1`. |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` |  | The page's buttons, right of the title. They wrap below it on a narrow screen rather than hiding in an overflow menu, because the primary action should never cost an extra click. |
| `breadcrumb` | `ReactNode` |  | A `Breadcrumb`, above the title: it is navigation back up the trail, not a label for the heading. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | A sentence under the title saying what the page is for. |
| `meta` | `ReactNode[]` |  | Short facts — owner, last updated, version — in a row divided by keylines. Rendered as a list, so a screen reader hears how many there are. |
| `tabs` | `ReactNode` |  | A Tabs list. Sits on the header's bottom edge so the active tab cuts into it. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<header data-slot="page-header">`.

Types: `PageHeaderProps`

Used in: Agent hand-off, Confirmation, Empty states, Filtered table, List and detail, Settings page

### Panel

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-panel--overview

A cut surface: a region of one ground bounded by an edge, where the tone decides the ink.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Grouping content into a region of a page, bounded by the ink keyline.
- A filled region on a layer colour; the panel resets the ink so the text stays legible.
- The one panel on a screen that is its point, with edge set to band.

When not to:
- A message about a region, with a tone and an action. Use Callout.
- Content that floats over the page. Use Popover.
- A region with nothing in it yet. Use Empty state.

- Do: Set the ground with tone, so every ink inside follows it.
- Don’t: Add a background class to a div; secondary text inside it renders dark on dark.
- Do: Keep edge band to one panel per screen.
- Don’t: Band every card on the page.

Content:
- Title a panel by what it holds, “Weekly digest agent”, not “Details” or “Info”.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `<div>` by default, or a `section`, `article` or `aside` through `as`.
- On a filled tone, redefines the whole ink ramp, so secondary text inside still clears contrast.
- Draws its edge as an outline in forced colours mode, so the panel keeps its shape.

You handle:
- Name a `section` or `aside` with `aria-labelledby` pointing at its heading.

#### `Panel` (component)

- `layer`: `pale`, `cloth`, `shade`, `ink`, `rojo`, `anil`, `verde`, `oro` (default `pale`)
- `edge`: `cut`, `band`, `none` (default `cut`)
- `padding`: `none`, `sm`, `md`, `lg` (default `md`)
- `band`: `oro`, `rojo`, `anil`, `verde`, `cloth` (default `oro`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `"article" \| "aside" \| "div" \| "section"` | `div` | Render as a landmark-free `section` (named by its heading) instead of a `div`. |
| `band` | `"oro" \| "rojo" \| "anil" \| "verde" \| "cloth"` |  | The colour revealed by `edge="band"`, gold by default. Has no effect on the other edges. |
| `edge` | `"cut" \| "band" \| "none"` |  | `cut` (default) is the ink keyline; `band` reveals a band of `band`'s colour between two keylines, for the one panel that is the point of the screen; `none` for a ground inside another. |
| `layer` | `"pale" \| "cloth" \| "shade" \| "ink" \| "rojo" \| "anil" \| "verde" \| "oro"` |  | The ground the panel is cut from, `pale` by default. A filled layer (`rojo`, `anil`, `verde`, `ink`) re-inks everything inside it, so pick it here rather than with a background class. |
| `padding` | `"none" \| "sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | Inner padding on the spacing scale, so it moves with density; `md` by default, `none` for a table or a list that runs edge to edge. |
| `tone` (deprecated) | `PanelLayer` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `layer`, which takes the same values. `codemods/prop-vocabulary.ts` rewrites call sites. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="panel">`.

#### `panelVariants` (function)

The cut surface: a region of one ground, bounded by a hard edge.

Types: `PanelLayer`, `PanelProps`, `PanelTone`

### Stack

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-stack--overview

Stack for a column with one gap, Inline for a row that wraps, and RowBetween for one thing at each end.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Vertical rhythm between blocks, with a gap named by role.
- A row of buttons or badges that wraps on a phone, with Inline.
- A panel header with a title at one end and an action at the other, with RowBetween.

When not to:
- A row of controls that should be one tab stop. Use Toolbar.
- Columns that reflow with the available width. Use Grid.
- Groups that need more separation than a larger gap. Use Divider.

- Do: Pick a gap from the seven named steps.
- Don’t: Add an arbitrary margin as an eighth step; a layout that needs one needs a divider.

Content:
- In a RowBetween header, pair a noun title with a verb action: “Run history” and “Export CSV”.

Mola handles:
- Renders a plain `<div>` with no role, so reading order is source order.
- Lets `Inline` wrap by default, so a row of controls does not force the page wider than a phone.

You handle:
- Put children in the order they should be read.

#### `Stack` (component)

Vertical rhythm: children down a column, one gap between them.

- `direction`: `column`, `row` (default `column`)
- `align`: `start`, `center`, `end`, `stretch`, `baseline`
- `justify`: `start`, `center`, `end`, `between`
- `wrap`: `true`, `false` (default `false`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `align` | `"start" \| "center" \| "end" \| "stretch" \| "baseline"` |  | Cross-axis alignment. Inline defaults to `center`, so an icon and a label line up; use `baseline` for mixed type sizes. |
| `direction` | `"column" \| "row"` |  | `column` (default) stacks children down; `row` lays them across without wrapping, unless `wrap` is set. |
| `gap` | `Gap` |  | The space between children, on the role-named scale that moves with density. Stack defaults to `md`, Inline to `sm`. |
| `justify` | `"start" \| "center" \| "end" \| "between"` |  | Main-axis distribution. `between` pushes the first and last child to the ends. |
| `wrap` | `true \| false` |  | Let children wrap onto a new line. On by default for Inline, off for Stack; turn it off on a row only when its children can shrink, or it overflows a phone. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="stack">`.

#### `Inline` (component)

A row that wraps — toolbars, badge rows, button groups. Wraps by default, because a row of controls that cannot wrap is the thing that forces a 508px-wide document on a 390px phone (a real defect this system shipped).

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `align` | `"start" \| "center" \| "end" \| "stretch" \| "baseline"` | `center` | Cross-axis alignment. Inline defaults to `center`, so an icon and a label line up; use `baseline` for mixed type sizes. |
| `gap` | `Gap` | `sm` | The space between children, on the role-named scale that moves with density. Stack defaults to `md`, Inline to `sm`. |
| `justify` | `"start" \| "center" \| "end" \| "between"` |  | Main-axis distribution. `between` pushes the first and last child to the ends. |
| `wrap` | `boolean` | `true` | Let children wrap onto a new line. On by default for Inline, off for Stack; turn it off on a row only when its children can shrink, or it overflows a phone. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="inline">`.

#### `RowBetween` (component)

One thing on the left, one on the right: a panel header, a table toolbar.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="row-between">`.

#### `stackVariants` (function)

Types: `Gap`, `InlineProps`, `StackProps`

### Toolbar

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-layout-toolbar--overview

A row of related controls with one tab stop and arrow-key movement, with toggles, a field and an overflow menu.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Actions and filters above a table or canvas, such as Re-run, Stop and Share trace.
- Pressed states, with ToolbarToggle and ToolbarToggleGroup.
- A filter field in the row, with ToolbarInput and an aria-label.
- More actions than fit, folded into a More menu with ToolbarOverflow.

When not to:
- One or two actions; plain buttons are enough. Use Button.
- Switching between views. Use Segmented control.
- Actions on one row of a table. Use Menu.

- Do: Separate groups with ToolbarSeparator.
- Don’t: Group with extra space; between identical ghost buttons it reads as uneven spacing.
- Do: Order overflow items by priority, with the destructive action last.
- Don’t: Put Delete first, where it stays in the row the longest.

Content:
- Name the toolbar with aria-label: “Run actions”, “Filter runs”.
- Buttons are verbs; toggles name what they show: “Wrap lines”, “Tool calls”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus into the toolbar, onto one control.
- ArrowRight or ArrowLeft: Moves between controls, wrapping at either end; mirrored in right-to-left.
- Enter or Space: Activates the focused button, or presses the focused toggle.
- Shift+Tab: Leaves the toolbar; it is one tab stop.

Mola handles:
- Renders `role="toolbar"` with roving focus from Base UI, so ten controls are one tab stop.
- Reports toggles as pressed with `aria-pressed`.
- In a text field, lets the arrow keys move the caret and leave only at its edges.
- Removes overflowed actions from the row and puts them in a More menu, so the arrows never stall on a hidden control.

You handle:
- Name the toolbar with `aria-label`, and each toggle group too.
- Give a `ToolbarInput` an `aria-label`; the placeholder is not a name.
- Give icon-only buttons an `aria-label`.

#### `Toolbar` (component)

- `variant`: `panel`, `bare` (default `bare`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `aria-label` (required) | `string` |  | Names the toolbar: "Run actions", "Text formatting". |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the toolbar, merged with `cn()`. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute. |
| `variant` | `"panel" \| "bare"` |  | `bare` (default) inside a header or a table frame; `panel` gives the toolbar its own cut ground over a canvas. |

Also accepts the props of `ToolbarRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="toolbar">`.

#### `ToolbarButton` (component)

A Button that takes part in the toolbar's arrow-key focus. Ghost and small by default.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the button, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `Toolbar`. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading icon; decorative. An icon-only button still needs an `aria-label`. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `sm` | The control height. Density already re-scales every size, so reach for a different one only to set a button apart from its neighbours, not to make a whole screen compact. |
| `variant` | `"secondary" \| "danger" \| "primary" \| "ghost"` | `ghost` | The weight of the action: `primary` (ink) for the panel's main action, `secondary` beside it, `danger` only for what destroys something, `ghost` for low-stakes actions in dense rows. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`, `ToolbarButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="toolbar-button">`.

#### `ToolbarGroup` (component)

Wraps, like `ToolbarToggleGroup` and for the same reason: the toolbar wraps its children, and a group is one child. Unwrapped, the app shell's three filter buttons ran 86px past a 320px phone.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="toolbar-group">`.

#### `ToolbarInput` (component)

A text field that is one of the toolbar's stops. The arrow keys move the caret while there is text to move through and only leave the field at its edges — Base UI's rule, and the one that lets a person edit a filter without being thrown onto the next button.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the `<input>` itself. Set a width with `wrapperClassName`. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | A leading glyph. Defaults to the search glyph; `false` for none. |
| `wrapperClassName` | `string` |  | Classes for the wrapper, e.g. a width. |

Also accepts the props of `ToolbarInputProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<input data-slot="toolbar-input">`.

#### `ToolbarOverflow` (component)

Actions that fold into a "More" menu when the row runs out of room.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `ToolbarOverflowItem[]` |  | In priority order: the first items stay visible longest. |
| `align` | `"start" \| "end"` | `end` | Which end of the free space the buttons hug. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ToolbarOverflowMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the menu button's label and name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `moreLabel` | `string` |  | The name of the menu button that collects what does not fit. "More actions" (`messages.moreActions`) by default. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="toolbar-overflow">`.

#### `ToolbarSeparator` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the keyline between groups. A string, for the reason given on `Toolbar`. |

Also accepts the props of `ToolbarSeparatorProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="toolbar-separator">`.

#### `ToolbarToggle` (component)

A two-state button: "Show archived", "Wrap lines". Inside a `ToolbarToggleGroup` it is one of a set.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the toggle, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `Toolbar`. |
| `defaultPressed` | `boolean` |  | Whether it starts on, without controlling it afterwards. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading icon; decorative. An icon-only toggle still needs an `aria-label`. |
| `onPressedChange` | `((pressed: boolean) => void)` |  | Called with the new state each time the toggle is pressed. |
| `pressed` | `boolean` |  | Whether the toggle is on, when you control it. Inside a `ToolbarToggleGroup`, control the group's value instead. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `sm` | The control height. Density already re-scales every size, so reach for a different one only to set a button apart from its neighbours, not to make a whole screen compact. |
| `value` | `string` |  | Identifies the toggle inside a `ToolbarToggleGroup`. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`, `ToolbarButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="toolbar-toggle">`.

#### `ToolbarToggleGroup` (component)

A set of toggles: one-of (the default) or `multiple`. It sits inside the toolbar's roving focus rather than adding a second arrow-key scope, so the arrows walk straight through it and out the other side.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `aria-label` (required) | `string` |  | Names the set: "Density", "Status". |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the group, which wraps like the toolbar. A string, for the reason given on `Toolbar`. |

Also accepts the props of `ToggleGroupProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="toolbar-toggle-group">`.

#### `toolbarVariants` (function)

A row of related controls with `role="toolbar"` and one tab stop: Tab enters the toolbar, the arrow keys move inside it, Tab leaves. In a dense tool a table header can carry ten controls, and ten tab stops between the page title and the first row is a keyboard user's afternoon.

Types: `ToolbarOverflowMessages`, `ToolbarButtonProps`, `ToolbarInputProps`, `ToolbarOverflowItem`, `ToolbarOverflowProps`, `ToolbarProps`, `ToolbarSeparatorProps`, `ToolbarToggleGroupProps`, `ToolbarToggleProps`

Used in: Filtered table

Changes:
- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

## Navigation

### Accordion

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-navigation-accordion--overview

Sections of one document that open one or several at a time; Collapsible for a single disclosure.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Answers in an FAQ, or the advanced settings of an agent.
- Settings where comparing two sections matters, with multiple.
- One disclosure inside something else, such as a raw response, with Collapsible.

When not to:
- Peer views of one object where only one shows at a time. Use Tabs.
- Content most readers need on every visit. Leave it open on the page.
- The model’s thinking behind an answer. Use Reasoning.
- Ordered steps to follow. Use StepList.
- A hierarchy people move through and pick from, such as files or pages. Use Tree.

- Do: Say in the trigger what is inside, with a count in meta.
- Don’t: Hide a required field inside a closed section.

Content:
- Triggers are questions in an FAQ, “What counts as a run?”, and nouns in settings, “Limits”.
- Meta is a short fact: “6 tools”, “Defaults”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus from one trigger to the next, through any open panel’s content.
- Enter or Space: Opens or closes the focused section.

Mola handles:
- Each trigger is a `<button>` inside an `<h3>`, with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`.
- Each open panel is a `region` named by its trigger.
- Closed panels are hidden until found, so find-in-page opens the section that holds the match.
- The plus and cross marker is hidden from assistive technology; the state is in `aria-expanded`.

You handle:
- Place it under an `<h2>`, so the `<h3>` triggers sit in the page outline.
- Use `Collapsible` for a single disclosure inside something else; it has no heading.

#### `Accordion` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the root, merged with `cn()` so yours win a conflict. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute instead (`data-open:`). |

Also accepts the props of `AccordionRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="accordion">`.

#### `AccordionItem` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the item, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `Accordion`. |

Also accepts the props of `AccordionItemProps`, `UseCollapsibleRootParameters`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="accordion-item">`.

#### `AccordionPanel` (component)

`ref` and the DOM props go to the panel; `className` goes to the content inside it. The panel animates its own height, and padding on it would be clipped while it does, so the padding a consumer adds belongs to the content.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the content inside the panel, not the panel itself: the panel animates its height, and padding on it would be clipped while it does. |

Also accepts the props of `AccordionRootProps`.

`className` goes to `<div>`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="accordion-panel">`.

#### `AccordionTrigger` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the trigger button (not the heading around it), merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `Accordion`. |
| `meta` | `ReactNode` |  | Secondary text on the same line, right of the title — a count, a status. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="accordion-trigger">`.

#### `Collapsible` (component)

One disclosure on its own — "Show 12 more fields", "Raw response". No rules, no heading semantics: it is a control inside something else, not a section.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="collapsible">`.

#### `CollapsiblePanel` (component)

`className` goes to the content inside the panel, for the reason given on `AccordionPanel`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the content inside the panel, for the reason given on `AccordionPanel`. |

Also accepts the props of `CollapsiblePanelProps`.

`className` goes to `<div>`; `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="collapsible-panel">`.

#### `CollapsibleTrigger` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the trigger button, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `Accordion`. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="collapsible-trigger">`.

Types: `AccordionItemProps`, `AccordionPanelProps`, `AccordionProps`, `AccordionTriggerProps`, `CollapsiblePanelProps`, `CollapsibleTriggerProps`

### Breadcrumb

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-navigation-breadcrumb--overview

Where a page sits, as links back up the path, with the current page as plain text.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Pages nested two or more levels deep: Workspace / Agents / knowledge-agent / Run #4182.
- Long ids in the path, shortened in the middle with middleTruncate.

When not to:
- Moving between pages of the same list. Use Pagination.
- Switching between views of one page. Use Tabs.
- Top-level navigation between parts of the product. Use App shell.

- Do: Pass renderLink your router’s link, as an element (<Link />) or a function that returns one.
- Don’t: Link the current page; a link to where you already are does nothing.

Content:
- Use each page’s own title as its crumb, so the trail matches the headings.
- Shorten ids in the middle, keeping both ends: “run_8f3a…c21e”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus from one link to the next.
- Enter: Follows the focused link.

Mola handles:
- It is a `<nav>` named “Breadcrumb” holding an ordered list.
- The last item is plain text with `aria-current="page"`, not a link to where you already are.
- The separators are empty CSS rules, not slash characters, so they are not read out.

You handle:
- Leave `href` off the last item.
- When you shorten a label with `middleTruncate`, make sure the full id is on the page it leads to.

#### `Breadcrumb` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `BreadcrumbItem[]` |  | The trail, root first. The last item is the current page and renders as text whether or not it has an `href`; pass a long id through `middleTruncate` so both ends survive. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<BreadcrumbMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the landmark's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. `aria-label` still wins. |
| `renderLink` | `BreadcrumbLinkRender` |  | The element each link crumb renders as: your router's `<Link />`, or a function returning one. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<nav data-slot="breadcrumb">`.

#### `middleTruncate` (function)

`run_8f3a2b91c21e` → `run_8f3a…c21e`. Both ends survive; the middle is what nobody reads.

Types: `BreadcrumbMessages`, `BreadcrumbItem`, `BreadcrumbLinkRender`, `BreadcrumbProps`

Used in: Agent hand-off, Confirmation, Empty states

### Link

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-navigation-link--overview

Going somewhere: Link for text, in a sentence or on its own, and LinkButton for a navigation cut like a button.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A place in a sentence, with the inline variant: “Open the run history to restore one.”
- A link set on its own under a list or in a card’s foot, with standalone: “View all runs”.
- The main way forward when it is a navigation, with LinkButton: “Open run”, “Read the guide”.
- A page outside the app, with external, which opens a new tab and says so.

When not to:
- Doing something on this page: saving, deleting, opening a dialog. Use Button.
- A destination in the app’s sidebar. Use NavItem.
- The path back up from the current page. Use Breadcrumb.
- A numbered source an answer cites. Use Citation.

- Do: Pass render your router’s link, as an element (<NextLink href="/runs" />) or a function that returns one.
- Don’t: Wrap a Button in a link, or put onClick navigation on a Button; a link opens in a new tab, shows its address and is announced as a link.
- Do: Use external for a page that leaves the app, so the arrow and the hidden “(opens in a new tab)” go with it.
- Don’t: Open a link in the app in a new tab; it breaks Back for everyone and strands a screen reader user.

Content:
- Name the destination, not the gesture: “run history”, never “click here” or “this link”.
- In a sentence, link the words that name the place, not the whole sentence.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the link.
- Enter: Follows the link. Space does not, even on a LinkButton: it is a link, and Space scrolls the page.

Mola handles:
- Renders an `<a>` (or your router’s link, through `render`), so it is announced as a link, opens in a new tab on a middle click and shows its address.
- Underlines `inline` links at all times, so a link in a sentence is not told apart by colour alone.
- With `external` or `target="_blank"`, adds `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, the up-right arrow and a hidden “(opens in a new tab)” after the label, from `messages.link.newTab`.
- Hides the glyphs from assistive technology, so the label is the name.

You handle:
- Write link text that names the destination on its own, since screen readers list links out of context.
- Make sure the element you pass to `render` renders an `<a>` with an `href`, and forwards the props and ref it receives.
- Use a `Button` that says why, not a link, when the destination is not available yet; a link cannot be disabled.

#### `Link` (component)

Text that goes somewhere: in a sentence (`inline`, underlined) or on its own (`standalone`). `className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the `<a>`, or to the element `render` returns.

- `variant`: `inline`, `standalone` (default `inline`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the link, merged with `cn()` so yours win a conflict. |
| `external` | `boolean` |  | The link leaves the app: it opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, marked by the up-right arrow and a hidden "(opens in a new tab)". `target="_blank"` turns the same marks on. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<LinkMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the hidden new-tab cue. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `variant` | `"inline" \| "standalone"` |  | `inline` sits in a sentence and is always underlined, because colour alone cannot pick a link out of the words around it (WCAG 1.4.1). It takes the font of the text it sits in. `standalone` stands on its own ("View all runs" under a list) in the UI face, and underlines on hover: its place says it is a link. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<a data-slot="link">`.

#### `LinkButton` (component)

A link that looks like a button, for the main way forward that is a navigation: "Open run", "Read the guide". It is still an `<a>`, so it opens in a new tab on a middle click, shows its address on hover, and Space does not press it. It has no `disabled`: a link cannot be disabled, so render a `Button` that says why instead.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the link, merged with `cn()` so yours win a conflict. |
| `external` | `boolean` |  | The link leaves the app: it opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, marked by the up-right arrow and a hidden "(opens in a new tab)". `target="_blank"` turns the same marks on. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading icon. Decorative: the label is the accessible name. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<LinkMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the hidden new-tab cue. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | The control height. Density already re-scales every size, so reach for a different one only to set a button apart from its neighbours, not to make a whole screen compact. |
| `variant` | `"secondary" \| "primary" \| "ghost"` |  | The weight of the way forward, as on Button. There is no `danger`: going somewhere destroys nothing, and the rojo button keeps meaning that it does. |
| `withArrow` | `boolean` | `false` | Trailing arrow that slides 3px on hover, as Button's does: for a link that moves you on in a flow. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<a data-slot="link-button">`.

#### `linkVariants` (function)

A link: it goes somewhere. A button does something. The audit found "use a link styled with `buttonVariants`" on three pages and no component to do it with, so each caller rebuilt the classes, the arrow and the new-tab cue, and most left the cue out.

Types: `LinkMessages`, `LinkButtonProps`, `LinkExternalProps`, `LinkProps`

### Pagination

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-navigation-pagination--overview

Moving through a long ordered result, as numbered pages or as a compact range with two arrows.
