Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Results where a page is a place people come back to, with the pages variant.
- Dense tables where the question is how much is left, with the compact variant: “51–100 of 1,284”.

When not to:
- A table that also sorts and selects; DataTable includes its pagination. Use DataTable.
- Showing where a page sits in the hierarchy. Use Breadcrumb.
- Steps of a flow that must be done in order. Use Stepper.

- Do: Wire onPageChange to the router when a page should have its own URL.
- Don’t: Expect the page numbers to be links; the component does not own your URLs.

Content:
- Give the compact variant pageSize and total, so it states the range rather than “Page 2 of 26”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus through Previous, the page buttons and Next.
- Enter or Space: Goes to the focused page.

Mola handles:
- It is a `<nav>` named “Pagination”, with page buttons named “Page 5” and the current one marked `aria-current="page"`.
- Previous and Next are named buttons, disabled at the ends.
- The ellipses are hidden from assistive technology, and the compact range text is a polite live region.

You handle:
- Pass a distinct `aria-label` when a page has more than one pagination.
- After a page change, move focus to the results or say what loaded; the page buttons do not announce it.

#### `Pagination` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `onPageChange` (required) | `(page: number) => void` |  | Called with the 1-based page a person asked for. The page is controlled: set `page` from it, or from the router if pages are addressable. |
| `page` (required) | `number` |  | 1-based. |
| `pageCount` (required) | `number` |  | How many pages there are. The next button is disabled on the last. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<PaginationMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the landmark's name, the buttons and the range text. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `pageSize` | `number` |  | For the compact range text: rows per page and the total row count. |
| `siblings` | `number` | `1` | Pages either side of the current one in the `pages` variant. |
| `total` | `number` |  | Total row count for the `compact` range text. Without it, or without `pageSize`, the text falls back to "Page 3 of 26". |
| `variant` | `"pages" \| "compact"` | `pages` | `pages` (the default) for results where a page is a place people return to; `compact` ("51–100 of 1,284" and two arrows) for dense tables where how much is left matters more. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<nav data-slot="pagination">`.

#### `getPageRange` (function)

The visible slots for `page` of `pageCount` (both 1-based). Always `5 + 2 × siblings` long once it needs ellipses.

Types: `PaginationMessages`, `PageRangeItem`, `PaginationProps`

### Stepper

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-navigation-stepper--overview

A task done in order, one step at a time: the steps with their state, and the current step as a form that validates before it moves on.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Creating something that needs several decisions in a set order, such as an agent, a data source or an evaluation.
- A flow where each step depends on the one before, so later steps cannot be judged until earlier ones are done.
- Letting people go back to a step they finished and change an answer without losing the rest.

When not to:
- Settings that can be changed in any order and saved together. Use Form.
- Views of one thing people switch between freely. Use Tabs.
- Instructions a person reads and follows outside the product, such as a runbook. Use StepList.
- The progress of work the system is doing, such as an agent run. Use Agent run.

- Do: Keep to three to six steps, each a decision with a short noun for a title: “Model”, “Tools”.
- Don’t: Split a short form into steps to make it look smaller; every Continue is a page someone has to wait for.
- Do: End on a Review step that shows every answer before Finish.
- Don’t: Let Finish be the first time someone sees what they chose across steps.

Content:
- Name Finish for what it does: “Create agent”, “Connect source”.
- Step descriptions say what the step decides, in one sentence.
- Translate the buttons, the “Step 2 of 4” line and the state words with messages.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves through the steps that can be returned to, then into the current step’s fields, Back and Continue.
- Enter or Space: On a step in the list, opens that step and moves focus to its heading.
- Enter: In a text field, or on Continue, submits the step; a step that passes moves on and focuses the next heading.

Mola handles:
- The steps are an ordered list in a `nav` named by `label`; the current one has `aria-current="step"`.
- Each step states its state in words (“Done”, “Current”, “To do”, “Needs fixing”) as well as by its marker’s shape and colour, and a step button is named by those words, “Basics Done”.
- When the step changes, focus moves to the new step’s heading, which follows a “Step 2 of 4” line; on first render focus is left where it is.
- The panel is a `Form`, so a step with errors keeps focus in the step and sends it to the `FormErrorSummary` there.
- Steps beyond the furthest one reached are plain text, not disabled buttons, so they are not tab stops.

You handle:
- Name the list with `label`, such as “Steps to create an agent”.
- Validate in `onStepSubmit` and return `false` to keep a step; put a `FormErrorSummary` in any step with more than one field.
- Translate every word it writes with `messages`.
- Keep it out of another `<form>`: the panel is already one.

Anatomy:
- Steps (`[data-part="steps"]`): The ordered list of steps, in a nav named by label; reached steps are buttons.
- Bar (`[data-part="bar"]`): A band along the top of each step that carries its state’s colour.
- Marker (`[data-part="marker"]`): The square: a number to do, a check when done, a “!” when it needs fixing. Its shape states the state without colour.
- Step title (`[data-part="title"]`): The step’s short name; the same words head its panel.
- State (`[data-part="state"]`): The state in a word: To do, Current, Done or Needs fixing.
- Panel (`[data-part="panel"]`): The current step, a Form: Continue submits it, and it only moves on when the step passes.
- Position (`[data-part="position"]`): “Step 3 of 4”, above the heading that takes focus when the step changes.
- Actions (`[data-part="actions"]`): Back, and Continue or Finish, which is the form’s submit button.

#### `Stepper` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` (required) | `(step: StepperStep, index: number) => ReactNode` |  | Renders the current step's content: its fields, and a `FormErrorSummary` if it has more than one. |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | Names the list of steps, which is a `nav`: “Steps to create an agent”. |
| `steps` (required) | `StepperStep[]` |  | The steps, in the order they are done. |
| `defaultStep` | `string` |  | The step to open on, uncontrolled. The first step by default. |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3` | `2` | The level of the panel heading; match the page outline. `h2` by default. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<StepperMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the buttons, the "Step 2 of 4" line and the state words. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onComplete` | `(() => void)` |  | Called after the last step's `onStepSubmit` passes. |
| `onStepChange` | `((step: string) => void)` |  | Called with the new step's id when Continue, Back or a step in the list changes it. |
| `onStepSubmit` | `((step: string, event: FormEvent<HTMLFormElement>) => boolean \| void \| Promise<boolean \| void>)` |  | Runs when Continue or Finish is pressed, with the step's id and the submit event. Validate here; return `false` (or a promise of `false`) to stay on the step. While a promise is pending, Continue shows it is working. |
| `step` | `string` |  | The current step's id, controlled. Pair with `onStepChange`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="stepper">`.

Types: `StepperMessages`, `StepperProps`, `StepperStep`, `StepperStepState`

### Tabs

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-navigation-tabs--overview

Switching between views of one thing: underline for views, panel for tabs that are containers.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Views of one object, such as a run’s Trace, Output, Cost and Logs, with the underline variant.
- Containers, such as a code sample in three languages, with the panel variant.
- A count on a tab, such as the number of tool calls, through count.

When not to:
- Changing how the same data is displayed, such as Table or Board. Use Segmented control.
- Sections someone may want open together. Use Accordion.
- Moving between parts of the product. Use App shell.
- Steps that must be done in order, each checked before the next. Use Stepper.

- Do: Keep labels short, in sentence case.
- Don’t: Set tab labels in capitals; a row of capitalised labels reads as buttons.

Content:
- Labels are one or two nouns: “Trace”, “Output”, “Cost”.
- Let a long list scroll rather than shortening labels until they stop meaning anything.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the selected tab, and from there to its panel.
- ArrowLeft or ArrowRight: Moves focus between tabs, wrapping at the ends; swapped in right-to-left.
- Home or End: Moves focus to the first or last tab.
- Enter or Space: Selects the focused tab.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders `tablist`, `tab` with `aria-selected`, and a `tabpanel` named by its tab and reachable with Tab.
- Focus does not select a tab unless `activateOnFocus` is set, so passing over a tab never starts loading it.
- A tab’s `icon` is hidden from assistive technology, and its `count` is part of its name.

You handle:
- Give every tab a text label, even when it has an icon.
- Set `activateOnFocus` only when every panel is cheap to show.

Anatomy:
- List (`[data-part="list"]`): The row of tabs, on a keyline. Scrolls sideways rather than wrapping.
- Tab (`[data-part="tab"]`): One tab. Roving focus and arrow keys come from Base UI.
- Count (`[data-part="count"]`): An optional figure; it turns to ink on the active tab.
- Indicator (`[data-part="indicator"]`): The ink bar under the active tab. It slides; the tab text never moves.
- Panel (`[data-part="panel"]`): The content of the active tab.

#### `Tabs` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the root, merged with `cn()`. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute. |
| `variant` | `TabsVariant` | `underline` | `underline` (default) switches views of one thing, a run's Trace / Output / Logs. `panel` is for tabs that are containers, a settings sheet or a code sample in three languages: the active tab joins the panel below. Every part inside reads it, so set it here only. |

Also accepts the props of `TabsRootProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tabs">`.

#### `TabsList` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the row of tabs, which scrolls sideways when the tabs outgrow it. A string, for the reason given on `Tabs`. |
| `ref` | `Ref<HTMLDivElement>` |  | Allows getting a ref to the component instance. Once the component unmounts, React will set `ref.current` to `null` (or call the ref with `null` if you passed a callback ref). |

Also accepts the props of `TabsListProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tabs-list">`.

#### `TabsPanel` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the panel, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `Tabs`. |

Also accepts the props of `TabsPanelProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tabs-panel">`.

#### `TabsTab` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the tab, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `Tabs`. |
| `count` | `number` |  | Trailing count, e.g. the number of tool calls. Tabular. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading icon; decorative, so the label still has to name the tab. |

Also accepts the props of `NativeButtonProps`, `TabsTabProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="tabs-tab">`.

Types: `TabsListProps`, `TabsPanelProps`, `TabsProps`, `TabsTabProps`, `TabsVariant`

### Tree

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-navigation-tree--overview

A hierarchy you move through and pick from, with branches that open and close and one selected item.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- The files of a change an agent proposes, beside the diff of the selected file.
- The sections and pages of a knowledge base or documentation, as navigation.
- Folders of a dataset or a workspace, where the depth is real and people move up and down it.

When not to:
- Two levels of navigation for a whole product. Use App shell.
- Sections of one page that open and close. Use Accordion.
- A flat list of options to choose from. Use Listbox.
- Where the current page sits in a hierarchy, shown in one line. Use Breadcrumb.

- Do: Open the branches down to the selected item, so it can be seen when the page loads.
- Don’t: Nest deeper than four or five levels; the indent eats the label.
- Do: Load the selected item’s content on onValueChange, which only Enter, Space and a click send.
- Don’t: Load content as focus moves; the arrows are for moving.

Content:
- Labels are names, as the person knows them: file names as written, page titles in sentence case.
- Name the tree for what it holds: “Files changed”, “Knowledge base”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the item last focused, else the selected one, else the first, and Tab again leaves the tree.
- ArrowDown or ArrowUp: Moves focus to the next or previous visible item, without selecting it.
- ArrowRight: Opens a closed branch; on an open branch, moves to its first child. ArrowLeft in right-to-left.
- ArrowLeft: Closes an open branch; otherwise moves to the parent. ArrowRight in right-to-left.
- Home or End: Moves focus to the first or last visible item.
- Enter or Space: Selects the focused item, and opens or closes it if it is a branch.
- *: Opens every branch at the level of the focused item.

Mola handles:
- Renders `role="tree"` and one `treeitem` per visible item with `aria-level`, `aria-setsize` and `aria-posinset`, and `aria-expanded` on branches.
- Items are a flat run rather than nested groups, so each is named by its own label, not by everything inside it.
- One tab stop with roving focus; a hidden item hands the stop to the nearest open branch above it.
- Typing a letter moves to the next visible item that starts with it.
- The selected item has `aria-selected` and is a shade with an ink bar, the mark of the current location, never the colour alone.
- A disabled item is focusable, `aria-disabled` and never selected.

You handle:
- Name it: `aria-labelledby` pointing at the heading above it, or `aria-label`.
- Open the branches down to the selected item, so it is visible on load.
- Load content on `onValueChange`, which only Enter, Space and a click send, and move focus to it yourself only when the person asks.

Anatomy:
- Item (`[data-part="item"]`): One treeitem, indented by its level. The selected one is a shade with an ink bar.
- Marker (`[data-part="marker"]`): The plus on a branch, which turns into a cross when it is open.
- Icon (`[data-part="icon"]`): An optional leading icon, hidden from assistive technology.
- Label (`[data-part="label"]`): The item’s name, and what type-ahead matches.

#### `Tree` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the tree.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` (required) | `TreeNode[]` |  | The top level of the tree; branches carry their own `children`. |
| `defaultExpanded` | `string[]` |  | The branches open at first: open the ones down to the selected item. |
| `defaultValue` | `string \| null` |  | The item selected at first, when the tree keeps its own selection. |
| `expanded` | `string[]` |  | The open branches, by `value`, when you control them. |
| `onExpandedChange` | `((expanded: string[]) => void)` |  | Called with every open branch when one opens or closes. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: string \| null) => void)` |  | Called when Enter, Space or a click selects a different item. Never called as focus moves. |
| `value` | `string \| null` |  | The selected item, when you control it; `null` is none. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tree">`.

Types: `TreeBaseProps`, `TreeNode`, `TreeProps`

## Overlays

### Command palette

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-overlays-command-palette--overview

The keyboard’s front door: a searchable list of commands and destinations, opened with ⌘K or Ctrl+K.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Jumping to a run, an agent or a page by name from anywhere in the tool.
- Running a command without the pointer, with its shortcut shown beside it.
- Matching synonyms and ids through keywords.

When not to:
- Choosing a value for a form field from a long list. Use Combobox.
- Actions on one object, opened from that object. Use Menu.
- Filtering the list on the current page. Use SearchInput.
- Listing every shortcut the page binds. Use Shortcuts dialog.

- Do: Group results under headings, such as Actions, Agents and Go to.
- Don’t: Expect fuzzy matches; every typed word must appear in the label, description or keywords.
- Do: Keep unavailable commands visible and disabled, with the reason as the description.
- Don’t: Hide commands the person cannot run without saying why.

Content:
- Commands start with a verb, “Start a new run”; destinations are nouns, “Settings”.
- Descriptions add the reason or the context: “Owners only”, “Answers from the help centre”.

Keyboard:
- Control+K or Meta+K: Opens the palette when the page calls `useCommandShortcut`: ⌘K on a Mac, Ctrl-K elsewhere.
- ArrowDown or ArrowUp: Moves the highlight, skipping disabled commands and wrapping at the ends.
- Enter: Runs the highlighted command and closes the palette.
- Escape: Closes the palette.

Mola handles:
- It is a modal Base UI Dialog, titled by `label`, with focus held in the search input.
- The input is a `combobox` controlling a `listbox`, and reports itself collapsed, with no empty listbox, when nothing matches; the highlight is conveyed with `aria-activedescendant`, so the caret never leaves the input.
- Commands sit in `group`s named by their headings, and disabled ones carry `aria-disabled`.
- Once typing pauses, a polite status says the result count, or “No matches for …” when nothing matches; nothing is announced for the empty query.
- Icons and the key hints in the footer are hidden from assistive technology; the status region speaks the count instead.

You handle:
- Call `useCommandShortcut` to bind ⌘K or Ctrl-K. Give a command a `hotkey` to bind it, print it and list it in the shortcuts dialog; `shortcut` is display only.
- Write command labels as actions, “Start a new run”, and add `keywords` for the other words people use.

#### `CommandPalette` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the dialog popup, the element that is the palette. Controlled or not: every other overlay offered `defaultOpen`, and a palette that was controlled-only made the simplest case — a button that opens it — need a state hook in the page.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `groups` (required) | `CommandGroup[]` |  | The commands, in headed groups. Filtered as the person types; label-prefix matches come first within a group, and empty groups are dropped. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | Open on first render, for a palette that owns its own state. |
| `label` | `string` |  | Accessible name of the dialog. Defaults to "Command palette" (`messages.label`). |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<CommandPaletteMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the defaults above, the key hints, the empty sentence and the result count. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the palette opens or closes: the trigger, Escape, a press outside, or choosing an item, which closes it before the item runs. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the palette is open, when the page owns that (it usually does, to bind ⌘K). |
| `placeholder` | `string` |  | Shown in the empty search input. Defaults to "Type a command or search…" (`messages.placeholder`). |
| `renderEmpty` | `((query: string) => ReactNode)` |  | Shown when nothing matches. Receives the query. |
| `trigger` | `ReactElement<unknown, string \| JSXElementConstructor<any>>` |  | Rendered as the button that opens the palette, e.g. a `SearchInput`-shaped `Button`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="command-palette">`.

#### `filterCommands` (function)

The groups and items that match `query`, empty groups dropped, label-prefix matches first within each group.

#### `useCommandShortcut` (hook)

Opens the palette on ⌘K on a Mac and Ctrl-K everywhere else, from anywhere on the page, a search field included. Ctrl as well as ⌘, because half of every internal team is on Windows or Linux and a shortcut that silently does nothing there is worse than none.

Types: `CommandPaletteMessages`, `CommandGroup`, `CommandItem`, `CommandPaletteProps`

### Dialog

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-overlays-dialog--overview

Dialog for a short modal task; AlertDialog to confirm an action that destroys something.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A short task the person finishes or cancels before going back, such as renaming an agent.
- Read-only detail that needs the screen for a moment, with only the body scrolling.
- Confirming a destructive action with AlertDialog, which opens on Cancel and ignores clicks outside.
- An irreversible action at scale, with confirmationText set to the resource’s exact name.

When not to:
- Details beside the work that the person compares with the page. Use Drawer.
- A small panel anchored to a control, without trapping focus. Use Popover.
- A notice that needs no decision. Use Toast.
- A change an agent proposes for a person to review. Use Change review.

- Do: Name the object and the consequence in a destructive confirm: “Its trace, tool calls and outputs are removed for everyone.”
- Don’t: Ask “Are you sure?” over OK and Cancel.
- Do: Return a promise from onConfirm, so the dialog stays open if the work fails.
- Don’t: Close the dialog before the work has succeeded.
- Do: Say why a confirm failed in AlertDialog’s `error`, starting with what did not happen: “Not deleted. The trace store didn’t answer.”
- Don’t: Put the failure in `description`, or close the dialog and report it in a toast.
- Do: Pass `finalFocus` when the confirm removes its own trigger, pointing at the next item or the list’s heading.
- Don’t: Keep a disabled button in place only so focus has somewhere to return.

Content:
- Titles name the task or ask the question: “Rename agent”, “Delete this run?”.
- The confirm button repeats the verb and object: “Delete run”, never “OK” or “Yes”.
- An error starts with what did not happen, then why, then what is still true: “Not revoked. … The key still works.”
- The footer holds a ghost Cancel and one primary action.

Keyboard:
- Enter or Space: On the trigger, opens the dialog and moves focus into it.
- Tab or Shift+Tab: Moves through the dialog’s controls; focus stays inside.
- Escape: Closes the dialog and returns focus to the trigger, or to `finalFocus` when it is set.
- Enter: In an alert dialog’s confirmation field, confirms once the text matches.

Mola handles:
- Base UI traps focus, locks scroll, makes the page inert and returns focus on close; the popup says `aria-modal="true"`.
- The dialog is named by `title` and described by `description`, and a named Close button is always rendered unless `hideClose` is set.
- A body long enough to scroll is a tab stop named by the title, in every browser, and only while it scrolls.
- `AlertDialog` is a `role="alertdialog"` that an outside click does not dismiss, and it focuses Cancel first.
- While an async confirm is pending, both buttons stop taking presses and the dialog cannot be closed.
- An AlertDialog `error` is a `role="alert"`, announced once when it appears and again after each failed retry, and it is the confirm button’s description.

You handle:
- Write a `title` that names the task; it is the dialog’s name.
- Set `hideClose` only when the footer has its own way out.
- Give the confirm button the verb, “Delete run”, never “OK”.
- Set `error` when a confirm fails, and clear it when the dialog opens for something else.
- Set `finalFocus` when the confirm removes the trigger, so focus lands on the next item rather than the top of the page.

#### `AlertDialog` (component)

The confirmation for an action that destroys something. Built on Base UI's AlertDialog, which differs from Dialog in exactly the ways a destructive confirm needs: `role="alertdialog"`, and an outside click does not dismiss it — a stray click on the page behind must not count as an answer.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `confirmLabel` (required) | `string` |  | The verb on the confirm button: "Delete run", never "OK". |
| `description` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | What happens if the operator confirms, and whether it can be undone. Required: a destructive confirm with no consequence stated is a guess. |
| `onConfirm` (required) | `() => void \| Promise<void>` |  | May return a promise. While it is pending the confirm button shows its working strip, both buttons stop accepting presses, and the dialog stays open; it closes when the promise resolves and stays open if it rejects, so the operator can see the failure where they caused it. |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The question, naming the thing: "Delete run #4182?". It is the dialog's accessible name. |
| `cancelLabel` | `string` |  | The safe answer's label, "Cancel" (`messages.cancel`) by default. It takes focus when the dialog opens, so a reflexive Enter cancels. |
| `confirmationText` | `string` |  | When set, the confirm button stays disabled until this exact string is typed — the run id, the project slug. For actions that cannot be undone and that a muscle-memory Enter could otherwise fire. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` |  | Opens the dialog on first render without controlling it afterwards. |
| `error` | `ReactNode` |  | Why the last confirm failed, starting with what did not happen: "Not revoked. The server didn't answer." Shown between the body and the buttons, announced once when it appears, and linked to the confirm button so it is read again when focus is there. Hidden while a retry is pending. Clear it when the dialog opens for something else. |
| `finalFocus` | `boolean \| RefObject<HTMLElement \| null> \| ((closeType: InteractionType) => boolean \| void \| HTMLElement \| null)` |  | Where focus goes when the dialog closes: Base UI's `finalFocus`. By default it returns to the trigger, which is right after Cancel. After a confirm that removed the trigger (a deleted row's button), return the element a reader would carry on from: the next row's action, or the list's heading. A function receives how the dialog was closed. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<AlertDialogMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default cancel label and the confirmation field's label. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called with the next open state on every open and close, Cancel and Escape included. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the dialog is open, when you control it: to open it from somewhere other than `trigger`, or to close it after an async confirm of your own. |
| `tone` | `"neutral" \| "danger"` | `danger` | `danger` cuts the seam in rojo and arms the confirm button in red. |
| `trigger` | `ReactElement<unknown, string \| JSXElementConstructor<any>>` |  | Rendered as the trigger, e.g. a danger `Button`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="alert-dialog">`.

#### `Dialog` (component)

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `DialogClose` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="dialog-close">`.

#### `DialogContent` (component)

- `size`: `sm`, `md`, `lg` (default `md`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The dialog's accessible name. Required: an unnamed modal is announced as "dialog" and nothing else. |
| `band` | `DialogBand` | `oro` | The layer revealed in the top seam. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the popup, merged with `cn()`. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | A line under the title, read as the dialog's accessible description when it opens. Say what the dialog is for; leave instructions to the body. |
| `footer` | `ReactNode` |  | Action row under the body: right-aligned, or stacked full width with the last action on top when the row is under 24rem. Usually a `ghost` cancel and one primary. |
| `hideClose` | `boolean` | `false` | Hide the corner close button. Only when the footer carries an explicit way out. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<DialogMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the close button's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` |  | The popup's maximum width: `sm` for a short question, `md` (default) for a form, `lg` for a table or a long review. |

Also accepts the props of `DialogPopupProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="dialog">`.

#### `DialogTrigger` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="dialog-trigger">`.

#### `dialogContentVariants` (function)

A modal is a panel cut out of the page and laid on top of it, so it is the one place a seam earns the full relleno: an 11px strip of the layer colour slit with ink along its top edge. In the source mola that strip is how a panel announces where one layer ends and the next begins; here it is how a dialog says "you are now inside something" without a title bar or a tint. Gold by default, rojo on a destructive confirm — the band is the only place the dialog's intent shows before a word is read.

Types: `AlertDialogMessages`, `DialogMessages`, `AlertDialogProps`, `DialogBand`, `DialogContentProps`

Used in: Confirmation, Settings page

Changes:
- 0.5.0 (2026-10-14), feature: add an error slot and finalFocus to alert dialog [#34](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/34)

### Drawer

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-overlays-drawer--overview

A panel that slides in from an edge: details beside a list, filters, or quick actions in a bottom sheet.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- The details of a row, opened from a table, with an end drawer.
- Filters for a list, applied from the footer of a start drawer.
- Quick actions on a phone, with a bottom sheet.

When not to:
- A short task or a decision that should hold all the attention. Use Dialog.
- A few options attached to one control. Use Popover.
- Navigation for the whole product. Use App shell.

- Do: Put the apply action in the footer, which stays visible while the body scrolls.
- Don’t: Hide the close button; a touch screen-reader user has no Escape key.

Content:
- Titles name what is inside: “Run details”, “Filter runs”.
- An apply button states the result: “Show 42 runs”.

Keyboard:
- Enter or Space: On the trigger, opens the drawer and moves focus into it.
- Tab or Shift+Tab: Moves through the drawer’s controls; focus stays inside.
- Escape: Closes the drawer and returns focus to the trigger.

Mola handles:
- It is a Base UI Dialog underneath: focus trap, scroll lock, the inert page and focus return.
- The drawer is named by `title` and described by `description`, with a named Close button unless `hideClose` is set.
- The swipe to dismiss is an extra; Escape and the Close button do the same without a gesture.
- The side is logical, so an end drawer opens from the left in right-to-left, and the swipe follows.

You handle:
- Write a `title` that names what the drawer holds.
- Set `hideClose` only when the footer has its own way out.

#### `Drawer` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `side` | `DrawerSide` | `end` | The edge the drawer slides in from. `start` and `end` are logical and swap in right-to-left; the swipe that dismisses it follows. Defaults to `end`, where a details panel beside a list belongs. |

Also accepts the props of `DrawerRootProps`.

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `DrawerClose` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="drawer-close">`.

#### `DrawerContent` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The drawer's accessible name. Required: an unnamed modal is announced as "dialog" and nothing else. |
| `band` | `DrawerBand` | `oro` | The layer revealed in the top seam. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the popup, merged with `cn()`. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | A line under the title, read as the drawer's accessible description when it opens. |
| `footer` | `ReactNode` |  | Action row pinned under the scrolling body, behind a keyline. Usually a `ghost` cancel and one primary. |
| `hideClose` | `boolean` | `false` | Hide the close button. Only when the footer carries an explicit way out. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<DrawerMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the close button's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `md` | Width of a side drawer, or height of a bottom sheet. Always capped by the viewport. |

Also accepts the props of `DrawerPopupProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="drawer">`.

#### `DrawerTrigger` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="drawer-trigger">`.

Types: `DrawerMessages`, `DrawerBand`, `DrawerContentProps`, `DrawerProps`, `DrawerSide`

Used in: Filtered table

Changes:
- 0.2.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add combobox, date pickers, number input, file upload, skeleton, drawer, toolbar and data table features [#11](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/11)

### Menu

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-overlays-menu--overview

Actions on a thing that do not each deserve a button, with checkbox and radio items, submenus and shortcuts.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Row actions behind an icon button such as “Run actions”.
- View options, such as columns to show and a sort order, with checkbox and radio items.
- Related actions grouped in a submenu, such as export formats.

When not to:
- One or two actions that are always needed. Use Button.
- Choosing a value for a form field. Use Select.
- Finding any command in the product by name. Use Command palette.
- A panel with fields or an explanation. Use Popover.

- Do: Mark a destructive item with tone set to danger, and put it last, after a separator.
- Don’t: Place Delete between harmless actions.

Content:
- Items are verbs on the object: “Rename”, “Duplicate”, “Delete run”.
- Submenu items name the format or the target: “Trace as JSON”.
- Group labels are short nouns: “Columns”, “Sort by”.

Keyboard:
- Enter or Space or ArrowDown: On the trigger, opens the menu and focuses an item.
- ArrowDown or ArrowUp: Moves between items, wrapping at the ends.
- Home or End: Moves to the first or last item.
- Enter or Space: Runs the focused item, or toggles a checkbox or radio item.
- ArrowRight or ArrowLeft: Opens or closes a submenu; swapped in right-to-left.
- Escape: Closes the menu and returns focus to the trigger.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders `menu`, `menuitem`, `menuitemcheckbox` and `menuitemradio`, with typeahead and focus return.
- The highlight is an ink fill, rojo on a destructive item, so the current row is a shape and not a tint.
- Icons, check marks and the submenu chevron are hidden from assistive technology.
- Checkbox and radio items keep the menu open, so several can be set in one visit.

You handle:
- Give the trigger an accessible name; an icon-only trigger needs an `IconButton` with a `label`.
- Bind the keys shown by `shortcut` yourself; they are display only.

#### `Menu` (component)

Actions on a thing — a run, a row, a document — that do not each deserve a button.

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `MenuCheckboxItem` (component)

The check sits in a fixed leading column, so checked and unchecked labels stay aligned down the menu. Toggling does not close the menu: a set of column toggles is chosen several at a time.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the item, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `MenuContent`. |
| `shortcut` | `string` |  | A key hint, as on `MenuItem`: shown, not bound. |

Also accepts the props of `MenuCheckboxItemProps`, `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-checkbox-item">`.

#### `MenuContent` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `align` | `Align` | `start` | How the menu lines up along that side, `start` by default, so it hangs from the trigger's leading edge. |
| `alignOffset` | `number` | `0` | A shift along the aligned edge in pixels, 0 by default. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the popup, merged with `cn()`. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute. |
| `side` | `Side` | `bottom` | Which side of the trigger the menu opens on, `bottom` by default. Base UI flips it when there is no room. |
| `sideOffset` | `number` | `6` | The gap between trigger and menu in pixels, 6 by default: enough that the menu's keyline does not merge with the trigger's. |

Also accepts the props of `MenuPopupProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu">`.

#### `MenuGroup` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-group">`.

#### `MenuGroupLabel` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-group-label">`.

#### `MenuItem` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the item, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `MenuContent`. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading icon; decorative. |
| `shortcut` | `string` |  | A key hint, right-aligned in muted figures: "⌘D". It only shows the key; binding it is yours, and it is read as part of the item's name. |
| `tone` | `"default" \| "neutral" \| "danger"` | `neutral` | `danger` destroys something: it highlights in rojo instead of ink. `default` is deprecated since 0.7.0 and removed in 1.0.0: use `neutral`, the status word every other `tone` uses. |

Also accepts the props of `MenuItemProps`, `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-item">`.

#### `MenuRadioGroup` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-radio-group">`.

#### `MenuRadioItem` (component)

One-of-N. The mark is a filled square — a radio dot would be the only round thing in the system.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the item, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `MenuContent`. |

Also accepts the props of `MenuRadioItemProps`, `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-radio-item">`.

#### `MenuSeparator` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-separator">`.

#### `MenuShortcut` (component)

Right-aligned key hint. The keys are left to right in every direction (see Kbd): in a right-to-left menu `⌘S` would otherwise render as S⌘. `dir` is on an inner span, not the root, because a logical margin resolves against the element's own direction: on the root, `ms-auto ps-4` became a left margin in a right-to-left row and pushed the hint against the label.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="menu-shortcut">`.

#### `MenuSubmenu` (component)

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `MenuSubmenuTrigger` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the item, merged with `cn()`. A string, for the reason given on `MenuContent`. |
| `icon` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading icon; decorative. The trailing chevron is always drawn. |

Also accepts the props of `MenuSubmenuTriggerProps`, `NativeButtonProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="menu-submenu-trigger">`.

#### `MenuTrigger` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="menu-trigger">`.

Types: `MenuCheckboxItemProps`, `MenuContentProps`, `MenuItemProps`, `MenuRadioItemProps`, `MenuSubmenuTriggerProps`

### Popover

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-overlays-popover--overview

A non-modal panel anchored to the control that opened it.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Filters for one table column, with Apply and Reset.
- A short explanation someone asks for, such as what a step budget is.
- The details of one item, in a few lines or controls.

When not to:
- A label for an icon-only control. Use Tooltip.
- A list of actions. Use Menu.
- A task that should hold focus until it is done. Use Dialog.
- A preview of a cited source. Use Citation.

- Do: Give the popover a title, or an aria-label when it has none.
- Don’t: Put information people need only inside a popover they may never open.

Content:
- The title says what the popover does: “Filter by status”.
- The description says how it applies: “Runs matching any checked status are shown.”

Keyboard:
- Enter or Space: On the trigger, opens the popover and moves focus into it.
- Tab: Moves through the popover’s controls, then on to the page after the trigger.
- Escape: Closes the popover and returns focus to the trigger.

Mola handles:
- Base UI renders the popup as a non-modal `dialog`, with `aria-expanded` on the trigger.
- A `title` names the popover and a `description` describes it.
- An outside press closes it, and the page behind stays usable.

You handle:
- Pass a `title`, or label the popup yourself; without one it is announced only as “dialog”.
- Use a Dialog instead when the content must be answered before going on.

#### `Popover` (component)

A non-modal panel anchored to the control that opened it — filters on a table column, the details of a run step, a share sheet.

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `PopoverClose` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="popover-close">`.

#### `PopoverContent` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `align` | `Align` | `start` | How the popover lines up along that side, `start` by default. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the popup (`w-72` and `p-4` by default), merged with `cn()`. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | A line under the title, wired as the popover's accessible description. |
| `side` | `Side` | `bottom` | Which side of the trigger the popover opens on, `bottom` by default. Base UI flips it when there is no room. |
| `sideOffset` | `number` | `8` | The gap between trigger and popover in pixels, 8 by default. |
| `title` | `ReactNode` |  | Heading, wired as the popover's accessible name. |

Also accepts the props of `PopoverPopupProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="popover">`.

#### `PopoverTrigger` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<button data-slot="popover-trigger">`.

#### `floatingSurface` (constant)

The one class list every floating surface in the system shares: popovers, menus, tooltips' larger cousins, the command palette, toasts.

Types: `PopoverContentProps`

### Shortcuts dialog

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-overlays-shortcuts-dialog--overview

Every keyboard shortcut the page binds, listed from one registry and opened with ?; useHotkeys binds and registers each one.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Listing the shortcuts of a tool people use all day, from a help button and the ? key.
- Binding a page-wide shortcut with useHotkeys, so it is listed in its own words and printed for the platform.
- Giving a command palette entry a hotkey, so it runs without opening the palette.

When not to:
- Finding and running a command by name. Use Command palette.
- Showing one key beside the control it triggers. Use Tooltip.
- Writing a key in running text. Use Kbd.

- Do: Use mod for a cross-platform chord: ⌘ on a Mac, Ctrl elsewhere.
- Don’t: Bind Ctrl-K on a Mac, where it deletes to the end of the line in every text field.
- Do: Use a sequence such as g r for navigation, and leave useHotkeys to keep plain keys out of text fields.
- Don’t: Pass inFields to a plain-key shortcut; it will eat the letter someone was typing.

Content:
- Labels start with a verb and name the object: “Open the command palette”, “Go to runs”.
- Groups are short nouns: General, Navigation, Runs.

Keyboard:
- ?: Opens the dialog, or closes it, from anywhere but a text field.
- Tab or Shift+Tab: Moves between the dialog’s controls; focus stays inside.
- Escape: Closes the dialog and returns focus to where it was.

Mola handles:
- Lists only the shortcuts bound under the `HotkeysProvider`, so the list cannot drift from the keys.
- Prints each key for the platform, ⌘ on a Mac and Ctrl elsewhere, and hides the keycaps behind the same keys in words: “Control K”, “G, then E”.
- Keeps every key sequence left to right in a right-to-left page, as macOS and Windows do: G then E is typed G first, so it never reads E then G.
- `useHotkeys` ignores plain keys typed in a text field, every key during IME composition, key repeat and keys a component has already handled.
- It is a modal Base UI Dialog, titled “Keyboard shortcuts”, with a Close button.

You handle:
- Mount `HotkeysProvider` once near the root, around `ToastProvider`.
- Give every `useHotkeys` call a `label` that starts with a verb.
- Use `mod` for cross-platform chords, and sequences rather than single letters for navigation.

#### `ShortcutsDialog` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest go to the dialog popup, as on `DialogContent`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `band` | `DialogBand` |  | The layer revealed in the top seam. |
| `className` | `string` |  | Classes for the popup, merged with `cn()`. A string, not Base UI's function of state: style a state with its `data-*` attribute. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | Open on first render. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` |  | A line under the title, read as the dialog's accessible description when it opens. Say what the dialog is for; leave instructions to the body. |
| `footer` | `ReactNode` |  | Action row under the body: right-aligned, or stacked full width with the last action on top when the row is under 24rem. Usually a `ghost` cancel and one primary. |
| `hideClose` | `boolean` |  | Hide the corner close button. Only when the footer carries an explicit way out. |
| `hotkey` | `string \| false` | `?` | The key that opens it, listed with the rest; `?` by default. `false` binds none. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ShortcutsDialogMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the title, its own binding's label and the empty sentence. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. The key names come from the provider's `hotkeys` table. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the dialog opens or closes: the hotkey, the trigger, Escape, Close. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the dialog is open, when the page owns that. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "lg"` | `sm` | The popup's maximum width: `sm` for a short question, `md` (default) for a form, `lg` for a table or a long review. |
| `title` | `ReactNode` |  | The dialog's title and accessible name. Defaults to “Keyboard shortcuts” (`messages.title`). |
| `trigger` | `ReactElement<unknown, string \| JSXElementConstructor<any>>` |  | Rendered as the button that opens the dialog, e.g. a help `IconButton` in the top bar. |

Also accepts the props of `DialogPopupProps`.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="shortcuts-dialog">`.

#### `HotkeysProvider` (component)

Mount once near the root, around `ToastProvider`, so the toast region's F6 is listed too. Renders no element.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `platform` | `HotkeyPlatform` |  | Which modifier `mod` means: `apple` for ⌘, `other` for Ctrl. Detected from the browser by default; set it to pin a story, a test, or a server render. |

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

#### `describeHotkey` (function)

The same keys in words, for a screen reader: "Command K", "G, then D". A glyph like ⌘ is read as "place of interest sign", or not at all.

#### `formatHotkey` (function)

The keys as a keyboard prints them, one array per step: `mod+k` is `[['⌘', 'K']]` on a Mac and `[['Ctrl', 'K']]` elsewhere. Modifiers in each platform's own order (⌃⌥⇧⌘ on a Mac, Ctrl+Alt+Shift elsewhere). Shift is left off a symbol that already needs it: `?` is printed `?`, not `⇧ ?`.

#### `useHotkeyKeys` (hook)

The keys of a binding as this platform prints them, flattened: `['⌘', 'K']` on a Mac, `['Ctrl', 'K']` elsewhere. For the display-only `shortcut` props on `Tooltip`, `MenuItem` and `SearchInput`, so the hint and the binding are one string.

#### `useHotkeyPlatform` (hook)

The platform the nearest provider pins, or the browser's. Until hydration it is `other`, the answer a server can give, so the first client render matches the server's HTML and a Mac swaps Ctrl for ⌘ a frame later rather than failing hydration.

#### `useHotkeys` (hook)

Bind `keys` (see hotkeys.ts for the grammar) to `handler` on the window, and list it under the nearest `HotkeysProvider`. Pass `null` to list a key something else already binds.

#### `useRegisteredHotkeys` (hook)

Every shortcut bound under the nearest provider, in the order they were bound. Empty without one.

Types: `HotkeysMessages`, `ShortcutsDialogMessages`, `HotkeyPlatform`, `ShortcutsDialogProps`, `HotkeysProviderProps`, `RegisteredHotkey`, `UseHotkeysOptions`

### Tooltip

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-overlays-tooltip--overview

A short label on hover or focus, with an optional keyboard shortcut.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Naming an icon-only button, and its shortcut.
- A `Toolbar` of icon buttons inside TooltipProvider, so each next label appears without a delay and the arrow keys move between them.

When not to:
- Information people need to finish the task; a tooltip does not exist on touch. Use Popover.
- Anything with a link or a button in it. Use Popover.
- Explaining a form field; give it a hint. Use Field.

- Do: Pair a tooltip with IconButton’s label.
- Don’t: Use a tooltip instead of an accessible name.

Content:
- A verb phrase in sentence case, with no trailing period: “Copy run id”.
- Write keys as they appear on the keyboard: ⌘, ⇧, R.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Focusing the trigger shows the tooltip, not only hovering it.
- Escape: Hides the tooltip.

Mola handles:
- It opens on keyboard focus as well as on hover.
- The popup is `role="tooltip"`, and while it is open the trigger is `aria-describedby` it, joined with any description the trigger already has.
- `shortcut` keys are drawn as `<kbd>` elements and read as words: “Command Shift R”.
- Inside a `TooltipProvider`, moving along a `Toolbar` shows the next tooltip at once, after the first delay.

You handle:
- Give the trigger its own accessible name, such as an `IconButton` `label`; the tooltip is its description, not its name.
- Never put information only in a tooltip; it does not exist on touch.
- Pass a focusable element as the child.

#### `Tooltip` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the tooltip popup; the trigger is `children`, which keeps its own.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` (required) | `ReactElement<unknown, string \| JSXElementConstructor<any>>` |  | The element that triggers it. Must be focusable — usually a Button or IconButton. |
| `content` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | The label: a few words, no interactive content. It reaches a screen reader only as the trigger's description, and not at all on touch, so it must repeat something said elsewhere rather than be the only place it lives. |
| `align` | `Align` | `center` | How it lines up along that side, `center` by default. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` |  | Shows it on first render without controlling it afterwards. |
| `delay` | `number` |  | Overrides the provider's delay for this tooltip. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` |  | Never open: for a trigger whose label is already visible in this state. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<TooltipMessages>` |  | Overrides the words the tooltip says on its own: the spoken names of shortcut keys. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean, eventDetails: TooltipRootChangeEventDetails) => void)` |  | Called when hover, focus or Escape would open or close it, with the next state. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether it is showing, when you control it. Rarely needed: hover and focus open it already. |
| `shortcut` | `string[]` |  | Keys, in order: `['⌘', 'K']`. |
| `side` | `Side` | `top` | Which side of the trigger it appears on, `top` by default. Base UI flips it when there is no room. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tooltip">`.

#### `TooltipProvider` (component)

A tooltip is the inverse panel at its smallest: ink ground, cloth type. It is the one floating surface that is NOT raised cloth, on purpose — a tooltip is a label, not a place, and it should never be mistaken for something you can move the pointer into and click. The ink ground also makes it the only thing on screen at that value, so a 12px label is found at a glance against a dense table.

Renders no element of its own, so it takes no `className` or `ref`.

Types: `TooltipMessages`, `TooltipProps`

## Typography

### Display

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-display--overview

Archivo Bold set wide, for the one title that is the point of a screen.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- An empty state, an onboarding panel or the page that introduces a tool.
- The signature display-cut class, on a filled panel, once per page.

When not to:
- Page titles in a working view. Use Heading.
- A label that says what kind of block this is. Use Rotulo.

- Do: Use one per screen at most.
- Don’t: Set display-cut on light cloth; it reads as a sticker, not appliqué.

Content:
- Keep it to a few words; it is set at up to 76px.

Mola handles:
- Renders an `h1` by default, or an `h2` or `p` through `as`, without changing the look.

You handle:
- Use `as` so the element matches the outline: at most one `h1` per page.

#### `Display` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `"h1" \| "h2" \| "p"` | `h1` | The heading element. Defaults to h1; the visual register does not change. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<h1 data-slot="display">`.

Types: `DisplayProps`

### Heading

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-heading--overview

Four heading levels for wayfinding in a tool, with size to separate the look from the outline.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A page title at level 1, panel titles at 2, groups at 3, and a label over a cluster of fields at 4.
- A heading whose look should differ from its outline level, through size.

When not to:
- The one statement that is the point of a screen. Use Display.
- A label that says what kind of block this is. Use Rotulo.
- Bold text that is not a heading. Use Text.

- Do: Change size to get a smaller heading.
- Don’t: Skip a level in the outline to get a smaller heading.

Content:
- Sentence case, with no trailing period: “Run history”, “Tool calls in this step”.
- Name the content, not the action: “Arguments”, not “View the arguments”.

Mola handles:
- Renders the element its `level` names, `h1` to `h4`.
- Lets `size` change the look without changing the outline.

You handle:
- Pick `level` from the page outline and never skip one; change `size` for a smaller look.

#### `Heading` (component)

- `visualLevel`: `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` (required) | `HeadingLevel` |  | The outline level, `h1` to `h4`. Required, and set from the document outline: never skip one to get a smaller look; use `visualLevel`. |
| `size` (deprecated) | `HeadingLevel` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `visualLevel`, which takes the same values. `codemods/prop-vocabulary.ts` rewrites call sites. |
| `visualLevel` | `1 \| 2 \| 3 \| 4` |  | The level it should look like, when that must differ from the outline level. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<h2 data-slot="heading">`.

#### `headingVariants` (function)

Four levels, and the visual size follows the level unless told otherwise.

Types: `HeadingLevel`, `HeadingProps`

Used in: List and detail, Settings page

### Kbd

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-kbd--overview

A key as a cut shape, with KbdChord for keys pressed together.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A keyboard shortcut in text, a hint or a footer.
- Keys pressed together, such as ⌘ and K, with KbdChord.

When not to:
- A literal the machine reads, such as an id or a flag. Use Code.
- The shortcut of an icon button, shown on hover. Use Tooltip.

Content:
- Use the key’s symbol or its printed name: ⌘, ⇧, ↵, Esc.
- When a shortcut differs between platforms, show both, or bind both as the command palette does with ⌘K and Ctrl+K.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `<kbd>`, and nests a chord's keys inside one outer `<kbd>`, as the HTML spec describes.
- Keeps keys left to right in a right-to-left page, isolated from the text around them: a shortcut is typed in a fixed order, so ⌘K never reads K⌘ and Ctrl K never reads K Ctrl.

You handle:
- Write key names a screen reader can say, or add the spoken name nearby: Command K for ⌘K.

#### `Kbd` (component)

A key, as a cut shape: raised cloth, ink keyline, and a heavier foot where the keycap sits on the board — the one place a solid offset is honest, because a key really is a block that moves down when pressed.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<kbd data-slot="kbd">`.

#### `KbdChord` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `keys` (required) | `string[]` |  | The keys, in order: `['⌘', 'K']`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<kbd data-slot="kbd-chord">`.

Types: `KbdChordProps`

### Lead

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-lead--overview

The paragraph under a page title that says what the screen is for, in the reading voice.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- One or two sentences under a page or section title, read once on a first visit.
- The explanation under a Display title on an onboarding or first-run screen.

When not to:
- Interface text, such as descriptions and hints. Use Text.
- Several paragraphs of long-form content. Use Prose.

Content:
- Say what the screen is for and what the reader can do there, in plain sentences.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `<p>` held to the reading measure.

You handle:
- Use it once, under the page title; it is not a heading.

#### `Lead` (component)

The first thing someone says, so it is set in the reading voice rather than the system one. Alegreya from here down; Archivo above and around.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<p data-slot="lead">`.

### Meta

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-meta--overview

Small facts compared down a column, such as duration, cost, count and time, in tabular figures.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A line of figures under a title or in a row: “2.4 s · 3,812 tokens · $0.014”.
- Timestamps and counts that are reference rather than content, such as when a run started.

When not to:
- Ids and other machine literals. Use Code.
- Labelled properties of one object. Use Description list.
- Descriptions or hints written as sentences. Use Text.

Content:
- Separate facts with a middle dot, “ · ”, and always give the unit.
- Keep each fact to a figure and its unit; no sentences.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `<p>` in tabular figures, so facts line up down a column.

You handle:
- Write the unit in words or a standard symbol, so the figure means something when read aloud.

#### `Meta` (component)

Duration, cost, count, timestamp — facts that are compared down a column, so they carry tabular figures. This is the role a monospace usually plays in tool UIs, minus the costume: Archivo's tabular figures line up exactly as well and keep the row in one voice.

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<p data-slot="meta">`.

### Prose

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-prose--overview

The reading voice for long-form markup, such as a document, a run summary or a model’s answer.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Rendered markdown or rich text that cannot be annotated element by element.
- A model’s finished answer, so it reads as speech rather than as interface.
- A chat column or a side panel, with size set to sm.

When not to:
- An answer that is still streaming. Use Streaming text.
- Interface text: labels, hints and descriptions. Use Text.
- A single introductory paragraph under a title. Use Lead.

- Do: Keep the reading measure, and let margins absorb the width.
- Don’t: Set fluid on long text in a wide panel; the lines grow too long to read.

Content:
- Headings inside prose use the same sentence case as the rest of the interface.

Mola handles:
- Styles the markup inside it without changing its elements, so headings, lists and quotes keep their meaning.
- Underlines links, so they are not marked by colour alone.
- Holds text to the reading measure unless `fluid` is set.

You handle:
- Supply markup with real headings in order, lists and link text that makes sense on its own.

#### `Prose` (component)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fluid` | `boolean` | `false` | Let the text run the full width of its container instead of the reading measure. |
| `size` | `"sm" \| "md" \| "base"` | `md` | `sm` for a chat column or a side panel; `md` for a document. `base` is deprecated since 0.7.0 and removed in 1.0.0: use `md`, the same size. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="prose">`.

Types: `ProseProps`

### Pullquote

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-pullquote--overview

A voice interrupting the prose: a quotation in the reading face, under a relleno band, with its source.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A quoted customer, a key finding in a research summary, or a sentence from an answer an operator pinned.
- Breaking a long write-up, such as an evaluation report, with the sentence the reader should keep.

When not to:
- A passage quoted as evidence for a claim. Use Sources.
- A message about the page. Use Callout.

Content:
- Quote exactly, and give cite as who said it and where: “Evaluation lead, research interview 7”.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `figure` with the quote in a `blockquote` and the attribution in a `figcaption` outside it.
- Hides the relleno band from assistive tech.

You handle:
- Pass `cite` so the quote says who said it.

#### `Pullquote` (component)

A voice interrupting the prose, so it keeps the reading face and takes a relleno band above it rather than a hairline rule — the filler slit is this system's divider, and a pull quote is exactly the seam it was made for.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `cite` | `string` |  | Who said it. Rendered in the label register, under the quote. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<figure data-slot="pullquote">`.

Types: `PullquoteProps`

### Rotulo

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-rotulo--overview

The label register, cut short by a band, set under the block it belongs to.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Saying what kind of thing a block is, or who it is from, under its heading.
- A label inside a dense header, with plain, which drops the band.

When not to:
- An eyebrow above a heading; the heading says it better. Use Heading.
- A status. Use Badge.
- The label of a form field. Use Label.

- Do: Put the rótulo under the heading.
- Don’t: Put it above the heading as an eyebrow.
- Do: Keep the oro band for ink grounds.
- Don’t: Use oro on light cloth, where it measures 1.66:1 and disappears.

Content:
- Two or three words in sentence case; the register sets them in capitals.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `<p>`; the band before it is drawn in CSS and is not read.

You handle:
- Place it under the block it labels, not above a heading as an eyebrow.

#### `Rotulo` (component)

The label register — Archivo pushed out along its width axis, cut short by a band of the layer beneath.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `band` | `RotuloBand` | `ink` | The revealed band before the label. Defaults to ink: on light cloth gold measures 1.66:1 and the mark disappears, and rojo, the default until 2026-10, is the danger colour on a product screen (docs/adr/0008-the-rojo-register.md). Rojo stays available for editorial pages; gold is for ink grounds. |
| `plain` | `boolean` | `false` | Drop the band and keep only the lettering — for a label inside a dense header. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<p data-slot="rotulo">`.

Types: `RotuloBand`, `RotuloProps`

### Text

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/components-typography-text--overview

Running interface text in the system voice, in three inks: ink, ink-2 and muted.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Descriptions, hints and secondary lines in the interface.
- Figures compared down a column, with tabular.
- A result stated in words, with tone set to danger or success.

When not to:
- Long-form text someone reads, such as an answer or an article. Use Prose.
- Headings. Use Heading.
- Machine literals. Use Code.

- Do: Use ink for what the reader came for, ink-2 for what explains it, and muted for what they can skip.
- Don’t: Add a fourth grey, or let colour alone carry a result.

Content:
- Sentence case throughout; full sentences end with a full stop, labels do not.

Mola handles:
- Renders a `p`, `span` or `div` only, so body text cannot sit on a heading element.
- Inherits the ink of the ground it sits on by default, so it stays legible on a filled panel.

You handle:
- Use `Heading` for headings; `Text` never carries the outline.
- Say a danger or success state in words; the tone alone is colour.

#### `Text` (component)

- `size`: `xs`, `sm`, `md`, `lg`, `base` (default `md`)
- `variant`: `default`, `secondary`, `muted` (default `default`)
- `tone`: `neutral`, `danger`, `success`, `default`, `2`, `muted` (default `neutral`)
- `weight`: `regular`, `medium`, `semibold` (default `regular`)
- `tabular`: `true`, `false` (default `false`)
- `truncate`: `true`, `false` (default `false`)

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `"div" \| "p" \| "span"` | `p` | The element, `p` by default: `span` inside a line, `div` around block content. Headings are `Heading`, so the outline never lies. |
| `ref` | `Ref<HTMLElement>` |  | Forwarded to the rendered element, whichever `as` picks. |
| `size` | `"xs" \| "sm" \| "md" \| "lg" \| "base"` |  | A step on the type scale, which density re-scales; `md` (body) by default. |
| `tabular` | `true \| false` |  | Tabular figures, so numbers in a column or a ticking counter keep their width. |
| `tone` | `"neutral" \| "danger" \| "success" \| "default" \| 2 \| "muted"` |  | A status ink, over the variant's: `danger` or `success` for a line that reports an outcome. Pair it with words or a glyph that say the state. |
| `truncate` | `true \| false` |  | Cut to one line with an ellipsis. Only for text whose full value is available elsewhere, such as a tooltip or a detail view. |
| `variant` | `"default" \| "secondary" \| "muted"` |  | Which of the three inks: `default` for what the reader came for, `secondary` for what explains it, `muted` for what they can skip. |
| `weight` | `"regular" \| "medium" \| "semibold"` |  | `regular` by default; `medium` or `semibold` to lift a name or a figure out of a line without changing its size. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<p data-slot="text">`.

#### `textVariants` (function)

Running UI text in the system voice. Three inks and no more: ink for what the reader came for, ink-2 for what explains it, muted for what they can skip. A fourth grey would be a grey nobody can tell from its neighbours — and muted is already the floor, at 4.83:1 on the shaded ground.

Types: `TextProps`

## AI interface

### Agent run

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-agent-run--overview

A long-running agent run as a timeline: its status and live clock, each step on one spine, and the reason it stopped.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Showing a run an operator watches or comes back to, step by step, while it works and after it ends.
- Answering “is it still working, what is it doing, how long has it taken, does it need me” on one panel.
- Putting the way back next to a failure: pass failure and a Run error sits between the header and the steps.
- Offering Cancel run while the run is open, and Retry run once it has failed or been stopped.

When not to:
- Events that have already been written down, such as a deploy history or an audit log. Use Timeline.
- A fixed sequence of instructions a person follows, such as a runbook. Use StepList.
- One tool invocation, shown on its own or inside a message. Use Tool call.
- Work with a known total, such as embedding 1,204 documents. Use Progress.

- Do: Show elapsed time that keeps counting on the active step, with the working relleno under it.
- Don’t: Add a percentage or a spinner to a run; it has no knowable total, and the system has no spinner by design.
- Do: Title each step in words, “Break the result down by plan”, and put the tool’s literal name in its detail.
- Don’t: Title a step with a raw tool name or “Step 4”; the spine already numbers the steps.

Content:
- Name the agent as people know it, in sentence case: “Cayuco research agent”.
- Step titles start with a verb and name the object: “Query the run history”, not “Querying…”.
- Write the summary as a result: “Answered with 3 sources. One guardrail flagged for review.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves through Cancel run or Retry run, Copy run id, and each step that has detail, including a step held open because it failed or waits for approval.
- Enter or Space: Activates the focused button, or opens and closes the focused step.
- Shift+Tab: Moves back through the same stops.

Mola handles:
- The run is a section named by its heading, and the steps are an ordered list named “Steps of” the run.
- Each change of run status is announced once through a polite status region; single steps are not announced, a failure the panel already alerts is not said twice, and a streamed answer inside the run leaves “Response complete.” to the run’s own “Run succeeded.”.
- When a retry removes the failure panel, focus moves to the run’s heading instead of falling to the page.
- Each step’s status is a glyph, a word and a colour, and a step that needs approval or has failed opens itself.

You handle:
- Give the run a `name` and each step a `title` in words; the tool’s literal name belongs in `detail`.
- Set `headingLevel` so the run’s heading fits the page outline.

#### `AgentRun` (component)

A long-running agent run, as a timeline.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` (required) | `string` |  | The agent's name — "Cayuco research agent". |
| `runId` (required) | `string` |  | The run's identifier, set as a literal with a copy button beside it, because it is what an operator pastes into a ticket or a log search. |
| `status` (required) | `RunStatusValue` |  | The state of the run as a whole. It drives the header's glyph, word and clock, which actions are offered (Cancel while active, Retry once failed, timed out or cancelled), and the one polite announcement made per change. |
| `steps` (required) | `readonly AgentStep[]` |  | The timeline, in order: the order is the content, so the steps render as an ordered list. A step waiting for approval or failed opens itself. |
| `endedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When the run ended. Freezes the header clock; omit while the run is still going. |
| `failure` | `AgentRunFailure` |  | Shown while the run is failed, timed out or cancelled: what stopped it, in words and verbatim, with the ways back. It takes over the header's *Retry run*, so the retry sits next to the reason for it. |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3` | `3` | Heading level for the run's name. Defaults to 3; use 2 when the run is the page's main subject. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<AgentRunMessages>` |  | Words for translation: step kinds, status announcements, the header buttons and the copy button. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `model` | `string` |  | The model identifier, set as a literal. |
| `onCancel` | `(() => void)` |  | Stops the run. Shows a Cancel run button while the run is active; omit to offer no way to stop it from here. |
| `onRetry` | `(() => void)` |  | Runs it again from the start. Shows Retry run once the run has failed, timed out or been cancelled; when `failure` is set the retry moves into the failure panel, next to the reason. |
| `onRetryFromStep` | `((stepId: string) => void)` |  | Re-run from the failed step, keeping the work before it. Offered when `failure.stepId` is set. |
| `startedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When the run started. With it, the header shows an elapsed clock that keeps counting while the run is active. |
| `summary` | `ReactNode` |  | Shown under the timeline once the run has finished. |
| `usage` | `Pick<TokenUsageProps, "input" \| "output" \| "cached" \| "costUsd" \| "contextWindow">` |  | Token counts and cost for the run, shown inline in the header. Omit when the provider does not report them rather than passing zeros. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="agent-run">`.

Types: `AgentRunMessages`, `AgentRunFailure`, `AgentRunProps`, `AgentStep`, `AgentStepKind`

Used in: Agent console, Agent hand-off

Changes:
- 0.3.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add change approval, citation cards and run recovery [#13](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/13)

### Change review

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-change-review--overview

A change an agent proposes, shown as the reviewer reads it, with Approve, Request changes and Reject under it.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- An agent proposes edits to files or records that a person must approve before they are applied.
- The reviewer needs the diff, the agent’s reasons in its own words and the decision in one place.
- Showing a record change field by field, before and after, with FieldChanges rather than a JSON diff.

When not to:
- One tool call that needs a yes or no before it runs; the approval belongs on the call. Use Tool call.
- Confirming a destructive action the person started themselves. Use AlertDialog.
- Code or a payload that is not a change. Use CodeBlock.

- Do: Keep Approve, Request changes and Reject the same size and variant, as the component draws them.
- Don’t: Restyle Approve as the ink primary or Reject as a quiet link; a nudged approval is a defect.
- Do: Pass the agent’s explanation as summary, so the reviewer can judge its reasoning.
- Don’t: Show the diff alone and leave the reviewer to guess why the agent made the change.

Content:
- Title the change by what it does, “Roll the help panel out to Starter”, not by the files it touches.
- Name a record by its type and key, so each field change has an owner: “Feature flag help_panel_v2”.
- A reason for a no says what is wrong and what would make it acceptable, because the agent acts on it.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves to the Reason field, then to Approve, Request changes and Reject.
- Enter or Space: Decides with the focused button.
