- Enter or Space: On a hunk header in a diff, folds or unfolds that hunk.

Mola handles:
- Reject or Request changes with an empty reason states the error in words, marks the field invalid, moves focus to it and does not call `onDecide`.
- The outcome replaces the form and takes focus once, so it is heard once; a decision that arrives already made does not take focus.
- The three decisions sit in a group named “Decision”, each a glyph and a word of the same weight.
- Diff lines are list items that say “Added:” or “Removed:”, line numbers and +/− glyphs are hidden from screen readers, and counts are read as “12 lines added, 3 lines removed”.

You handle:
- Give the change a `title` that says what it does, and pass `reviewer` so the outcome says who decided.
- Set `headingLevel` on the review and its diffs so the headings fit the page outline.

#### `ChangeCount` (component)

"+12 −3": the size of a change, at a glance.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `added` (required) | `number` |  | Lines added. Shown as "+n" and said as "n lines added". |
| `removed` (required) | `number` |  | Lines removed. Shown as "−n" and said as "n lines removed". |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ChangeCountMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the sentence a screen reader hears. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="change-count">`.

#### `ChangeReview` (component)

An agent proposes a change; a person reviews it and decides.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `agent` (required) | `string` |  | Who proposed it — "Cayuco research agent". |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | What the change does, in words — "Roll the help panel out to Starter". |
| `children` | `ReactNode` |  | The change itself: `FileDiff`s, `FieldChanges`, or both. |
| `decision` | `ChangeDecision \| null` |  | Controlled decision. `null` means undecided. |
| `defaultDecision` | `ChangeDecision \| null` | `null` | Uncontrolled starting decision. |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3` | `3` | Heading level for the proposal's title. Defaults to 3; use 2 when the review is the page's main subject. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ChangeReviewMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the decisions, the reason field and the header labels. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `model` | `string` |  | The model identifier, set as a literal. |
| `onDecide` | `((status: ChangeDecisionStatus, reason: string) => void)` |  | Called with a valid decision only: never for a rejection without a reason. |
| `reviewer` | `string` |  | Who decided, shown with the outcome — "M. Herrera". |
| `summary` | `ReactNode` |  | The agent's explanation of the change, in its own words. Set as prose. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="change-review">`.

#### `FieldChanges` (component)

A change to a record rather than a file: a feature flag, a customer's plan, a schedule. Each field reads before → after.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `changes` (required) | `readonly FieldChange[]` |  | The fields that changed, each as before → after. Leave out the fields that did not change: the list is the change, not the record. |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | What the record is — "Feature flag help_panel_v2". |
| `headingLevel` | `3 \| 4 \| 5` | `4` | Heading level for the record's title. Defaults to 4, under a `ChangeReview` heading. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<FieldChangesMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the count and the before/after words. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="field-changes">`.

#### `FileDiff` (component)

One file's change, as a reviewer reads it.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `path` (required) | `string` |  | The file's path after the change. |
| `defaultCollapsed` | `boolean` | `false` | Start with every hunk collapsed. |
| `diff` | `string` |  | One file's unified diff, as `git diff` prints it. |
| `headingLevel` | `3 \| 4 \| 5` | `4` | Heading level for the path. Defaults to 4, under a `ChangeReview` heading. |
| `hunks` | `readonly (DiffHunk \| DiffHunkInput)[]` |  | Or the hunks, structured. Wins over `diff`. |
| `kind` | `FileChangeKind` | `modified` | What happened to the file, shown as a mark and a word in the header. Defaults to `modified`; a `renamed` file with no hunks says so instead of showing an empty diff. |
| `layout` | `"unified" \| "split"` | `unified` | `unified` is one column of lines. `split` sets old against new side by side once the diff's container is 48rem wide, and stays unified below that — a phone has no room for two columns of code. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<FileDiffMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the kind of change, the rename and the words said before each changed line. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `previousPath` | `string` |  | The path before a rename. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="file-diff">`.

#### `countChanges` (function)

Lines added and removed across hunks — the figures `ChangeCount` shows.

#### `diffLines` (function)

Two versions of a text → the hunks between them, numbered, with `context` unchanged lines around each change (3 by default, as `git diff`).

#### `parseUnifiedDiff` (function)

Parse the hunks of one file's unified diff. File headers (`diff --git`, `index`, `---`, `+++`) are skipped — the path is `FileDiff`'s own prop — and so is `\ No newline at end of file`, which is a fact about bytes, not a line anyone reviews.

Types: `ChangeCountMessages`, `ChangeReviewMessages`, `FieldChangesMessages`, `FileDiffMessages`, `ChangeCountProps`, `ChangeDecision`, `ChangeDecisionStatus`, `ChangeReviewProps`, `FieldChange`, `FieldChangesProps`, `FileChangeKind`, `FileDiffProps`, `DiffHunk`, `DiffHunkInput`, `DiffLine`, `DiffLineKind`

Changes:
- 0.3.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add change approval, citation cards and run recovery [#13](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/13)

### Confidence

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-confidence--overview

How sure the system is, in three levels and in words, with UncertaintyNote for a specific claim to check.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Stating how well an answer is supported, High, Medium or Low, with the basis it rests on.
- Flagging one claim the reader should verify, next to that claim, with UncertaintyNote.
- Offering the action that verifies a doubtful claim, such as opening the dashboard it came from.

When not to:
- Showing confidence as a percentage or score. No model is calibrated enough for “87.3%”; state a level and its basis.
- Saying that something failed or needs attention; uncertainty is information, not an alarm. Use Callout.
- A reading inside a known range, such as a token budget. Use Meter.

- Do: Give a basis an operator can check: “3 sources agree”, “single source, 9 months old”.
- Don’t: Show a bare level, or a decimal that will be read as a measurement.
- Do: Say in the note exactly what to verify and why it is in doubt.
- Don’t: Put a generic disclaimer under every answer; people stop reading it by the second day.

Content:
- Write the basis as a short fact in lower case, since it follows the level: “sources disagree on the segment size”.
- Title an UncertaintyNote with the claim, “Unverified figure”, not with “Warning” or “Note”.

Mola handles:
- The level is said in words, “High confidence”, and the three segments are hidden from screen readers.
- The segments differ in how many are filled, not only in colour, so the level reads in greyscale.
- `UncertaintyNote` is an `<aside>` named by its `title`, with its icon hidden from screen readers.

You handle:
- Pass `basis` so the reader hears what the confidence rests on.
- Give each `UncertaintyNote` a specific `title`; two notes with the default share one landmark name.

#### `Confidence` (component)

How sure the system is, in three steps and in words.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` (required) | `ConfidenceLevel` |  | How strong the support is: three coarse steps, shown as filled segments and a word, never a percentage the model cannot honestly claim. |
| `basis` | `string` |  | What the confidence rests on — "3 sources agree", "single source". |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ConfidenceMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the word for each level. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="confidence">`.

#### `UncertaintyNote` (component)

Where the model flags its own doubt, in the answer, next to the claim.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `action` | `ReactNode` |  | An action that resolves it — "Open the dashboard". |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<UncertaintyNoteMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default title. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `title` | `string` |  | The short claim — "Unverified figure". Defaults to "The model is unsure" (`messages.title`). |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<aside data-slot="uncertainty-note">`.

Types: `ConfidenceMessages`, `UncertaintyNoteMessages`, `ConfidenceLevel`, `ConfidenceProps`, `UncertaintyNoteProps`

Used in: Agent console, Agent hand-off

### Markdown

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-markdown--overview

A model’s Markdown answer, streamed and rendered in Mola’s typography, with its citation markers kept and no HTML ever injected.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- The model answers in Markdown: headings, tables, numbered and nested lists, quotes, links and fenced code.
- The answer cites sources with [n] markers that must stay Citations, with their preview cards.
- Rendering untrusted model output, which must never reach the page as markup.

When not to:
- Plain paragraphs, bullets and code with no headings, tables or links; the smaller grammar is enough. Use Streaming text.
- Long-form content you write and trust, such as documentation, rendered by your own pipeline. Use Prose.
- A tool’s raw result or a payload, which is machine text, not an answer. Use CodeBlock.

- Do: Pass renderCitation built once with citationRenderer(sources), and render the SourceList on the same page.
- Don’t: Put a general Markdown library in front of the text; it drops the [n] markers or reads them as links.
- Do: Append to text as tokens arrive and pass status, so open markers close and the end is announced once.
- Don’t: Strip or buffer the Markdown yourself until a block is complete; the component already holds back what cannot be read yet.

Content:
- Set headingLevel so the answer’s # sits one level under the heading above it.
- Ask the model for the subset it renders: no raw HTML, footnotes or reference-style links.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves through the answer’s links and citation markers in reading order, then a code block’s wrap and copy buttons and a table that scrolls.

Mola handles:
- Headings are real headings, from `headingLevel` down, so the answer joins the page outline; lists, quotes and tables are their own elements.
- The text is `aria-busy` while it streams, and a polite status says “Response complete.” once, when a running stream ends.
- Nothing from the model is rendered as HTML, and a link keeps its href only for http(s), mailto and relative paths.
- An absolute link opens in a new tab and says “(opens in a new tab)” to screen readers.
- Citation markers go through `renderCitation`, so with `citationRenderer` each is a link named “Source 2:” and the source’s title.

You handle:
- Set `headingLevel` so the answer’s headings sit under the heading above it.
- Render the `SourceList` on the same page, so each marker’s `#source-n` link has somewhere to land.

#### `Markdown` (component)

A model's answer written in Markdown, streamed and rendered in Mola's typography, with its citation markers kept.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `text` (required) | `string` |  | The Markdown received so far. Grows; never rewritten from the top. |
| `doneAnnouncement` | `string` |  | Spoken once when a stream that was running completes. |
| `errorAnnouncement` | `string` |  | Spoken once when a stream that was running fails. |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4` | `3` | The heading level a `#` becomes; `##` is one deeper, and so on, never past 6. Defaults to 3, for an answer under a page and a section heading. The look follows the depth in the Markdown, not the level, so the same answer looks the same wherever it is placed. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<MarkdownMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default done and error announcements. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. The new-tab cue on an absolute link is `link.newTab`, shared with Link. |
| `renderCitation` | `((n: number) => ReactNode)` |  | How an inline `[n]` marker renders, usually `citationRenderer(sources)`. Without it the marker stays as text. Pass a stable function (module scope or `useMemo`): finished blocks skip re-rendering only while it stays the same. |
| `status` | `StreamStatus` | `done` | Where the stream is. `streaming` shows the caret, sets `aria-busy` and closes markers still open at the end; the move from `streaming` to `done` or `error` is announced once. Defaults to `done`. |
| `variant` | `"default" \| "muted"` | `default` | `muted` for text the reader may skim — reasoning, not the answer. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="markdown">`.

Types: `MarkdownMessages`, `MarkdownProps`

### Message

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-message--overview

One turn of a conversation, laid out as a ledger: who spoke, when, with which model, and what they said or did.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Transcripts between an operator, an assistant, the system and its tools, where each turn is a record.
- Assistant answers with copy, regenerate and rating actions, and a stated failure with Retry.
- A long conversation in Thread, which follows new turns only while the reader is at the bottom.

When not to:
- The model’s thinking before it answers; it is evidence, not part of the answer. Use Reasoning.
- A run that stopped at a step and can resume from it; a message error covers one turn only. Use Run error.
- Rendering the answer’s text; a message is the frame and the answer goes inside it. Use Streaming text.
- A standing notice outside a conversation, such as a read-only workspace. Use Callout.

- Do: Pass the system’s error verbatim in error, so an operator can paste it into a ticket.
- Don’t: Replace it with “Something went wrong”; the component already adds the plain sentence above it.
- Do: Let every turn take the full measure, one after another.
- Don’t: Wrap turns in chat bubbles; long answers, tables and code need the whole column.

Content:
- Set author to the name people know, “Cayuco” or “M. Herrera”; the role is only the fallback.
- Pass the model as its identifier, “cayuco-deep-3”; it is shown as a literal.
- System turns state what changed in a sentence or two: “Context reset. Earlier turns are summarised.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Reaches a message’s Copy, Regenerate and rating buttons, which stay in the tab order while hidden.
- Enter or Space: Activates the focused action, Retry, or Jump to latest.
- Tab: In a `Thread`, also stops on the transcript itself, so it can be scrolled.
- ArrowUp or ArrowDown or PageUp or PageDown: Scrolls the focused transcript.

Mola handles:
- Each message is an article named by its speaker and time, and a failed message states the failure in an alert with the error verbatim.
- The rating buttons are toggle buttons with `aria-pressed`, and a copy is confirmed in a status region.
- A `Thread` is a region named by `label`, not a live region, so a streaming answer is not read token by token.
- The thread follows new content only while the reader is at the bottom, and offers Jump to latest when they are not.

You handle:
- Give `Thread` a `label` that names the conversation.
- Pass `author` when the speaker is more specific than the role, so the article’s name says who.

Anatomy:
- Turn (`[data-slot="message"]`): One turn of the ledger, separated from the last by a keyline.
- Author (`[data-part="author"]`): Who spoke, cut short by the role’s band.
- Model (`[data-part="model"]`): Which model answered, as a machine literal.
- Body (`[data-part="body"]`): The content. The model’s words are set in Alegreya; the operator’s in Archivo.
- Error (`[data-part="error"]`): The failure in a sentence, the error verbatim, and Retry beside it.
- Time (`[data-part="time"]`): When, as a machine-readable time element.
- Actions (`[data-part="actions"]`): Copy, regenerate and rating. Shown on hover or focus, always on touch, and never while streaming.

#### `Message` (component)

One turn of a conversation.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `role` (required) | `MessageRole` |  | Who the turn belongs to. Sets the gutter label and its band; the content's typeface comes from what you pass in (`StreamingText` for a model's reply). |
| `author` | `string` |  | Who is speaking, if more specific than the role — "Cayuco", "M. Herrera". |
| `copyText` | `string` |  | Copies this text. Omit to hide the copy action. |
| `error` | `string` |  | The error, verbatim from the system. Shown as a literal — operators paste it into tickets. |
| `feedback` | `"up" \| "down"` |  | The rating already given, if any. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<MessageMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the role labels, the error block and the actions. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `model` | `string` |  | The model that produced it. A machine identifier, so it is set as a literal. |
| `onFeedback` | `((value: "up" \| "down") => void)` |  | Called with the reader's rating. Omit to hide the rating actions. |
| `onRetry` | `(() => void)` |  | Regenerates the turn. Shows Retry among the actions, or in the error block when `status` is `error`; omit to hide it. |
| `status` | `"error" \| "streaming" \| "done"` | `done` | `error`: the message failed partway. Content so far stays; the error is stated under it. |
| `timestamp` | `string \| Date` |  | When the turn was sent. Shown in the gutter and in the turn's accessible name; see `timeZone` for when it renders. |
| `timeZone` | `string` |  | IANA zone the time is shown in, such as `"UTC"` or `"America/Panama"`. Without it the time is the reader's own and appears only after hydration, because the server cannot know the reader's zone; pass one to have the time in the server HTML. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<article data-slot="message">`.

#### `Thread` (component)

The scrolling transcript.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | Names the region. Required: a transcript is a landmark worth finding. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ThreadMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the jump button and the scrolling area's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `stickThreshold` | `number` | `48` | Distance from the bottom, in px, that still counts as "at the bottom". |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="thread">`.

Types: `MessageMessages`, `ThreadMessages`, `MessageProps`, `MessageRole`, `ThreadProps`

Used in: Agent console, Agent hand-off

### Prompt input

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-prompt-input--overview

The composer: a field that grows, attachments, a toolbar, and a send button that becomes Stop while a response generates.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Taking the next prompt in a conversation or an agent console.
- Letting the operator stop a response from the place the send button was.
- Keeping a draft while offline, with the reason it cannot be sent stated beside it.
- Long briefs where Enter should add a line, with submitOn set to mod-enter.

When not to:
- A multi-line field in a form that is submitted with other fields. Use Textarea.
- Searching or filtering a list. Use SearchInput.
- Jumping to a page or running a command by name. Use Command palette.

- Do: Set status to offline and keep the field editable, so the draft survives a dropped connection.
- Don’t: Disable the field while offline or while a response generates; the operator loses the draft or the next prompt.

Content:
- The label names who is being addressed: “Message Cayuco”.
- Write the placeholder as a hint of scope, “Ask about a run, an evaluation, a document…”, never as the only label.
- Name attachments by file name, with type and size as the detail: “CSV · 4.1 MB”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves to each attachment’s remove button, then to the field, the toolbar, and Send or Stop.
- Enter: Sends the prompt; with `submitOn="mod-enter"` it adds a new line instead.
- Shift+Enter: Adds a new line when Enter sends.
- Control+Enter or Meta+Enter: Sends the prompt when `submitOn` is `mod-enter`.
- Enter or Space: Activates Send, Stop or Remove.

Mola handles:
- The field is labelled by `label` in a visually hidden `<label>`, and described by the hint line, which says why it cannot send while offline.
- Offline keeps the field editable and the draft kept; while generating, Send becomes Stop in the same place.
- Enter does not send while an input method is still composing.
- The token estimate is heard as “About 120 tokens”, and each attachment’s remove button is named “Remove” and the file name.

You handle:
- Pass a `label` that says who the message goes to, such as “Message Cayuco”.
- Give each control you put in `toolbar` its own accessible name.

Anatomy:
- Container (`[data-slot="prompt-input"]`): The form, keylined. The focus ring goes on the whole box, not the textarea.
- Attachments (`[data-part="attachments"]`): Attachment chips, above the text, each with its own remove button.
- Input (`[data-part="input"]`): A textarea that grows to maxRows, labelled by a visually hidden label.
- Toolbar (`[data-part="toolbar"]`): The caller’s controls: attach, model picker.
- Hint (`[data-part="hint"]`): The send shortcut and the token estimate. It describes the textarea.
- Send (`[data-part="send"]`): Disabled until there is something to send. Becomes Stop while generating.
- Stop (`[data-part="stop"]`): Replaces Send while the model is generating.

#### `AttachmentChip` (component)

A file or context item attached to the next prompt. The filename is a literal.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` (required) | `string` |  | The file or item's name, set as a literal and used in the remove button's name ("Remove report.csv"). |
| `detail` | `string` |  | e.g. "CSV · 48 KB". |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<AttachmentChipMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the remove button's name. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onRemove` | `(() => void)` |  | Takes the attachment off the next prompt. Shows a remove button; omit for an attachment that cannot be removed. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="attachment-chip">`.

#### `PromptInput` (component)

The composer.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` (required) | `string` |  | The visible-to-assistive-tech name of the field — "Message Cayuco". |
| `onSubmit` (required) | `(value: string) => void` |  | Called with the trimmed prompt. The field clears itself when uncontrolled. |
| `attachments` | `ReactNode` |  | Attachment chips, above the field. |
| `defaultValue` | `string` |  | The starting draft when the composer owns it. Defaults to empty; the field clears itself after a send. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Locks the composer, for a conversation the reader may not write to. To stop sending for a while, use `status="offline"` instead, which keeps the draft editable and says why. |
| `maxRows` | `number` | `10` | Rows before the field scrolls instead of growing. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<PromptInputMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the placeholder, the hints, the estimate and the buttons. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onStop` | `(() => void)` |  | Called by the stop button while `status` is `generating`. |
| `onValueChange` | `((value: string) => void)` |  | Called on every keystroke with the whole draft, untrimmed. |
| `placeholder` | `string` |  | Defaults to "Ask anything…" (`messages.placeholder`). It is a hint, never the name: `label` is what assistive tech announces. |
| `showEstimate` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the rough token estimate. |
| `status` | `"idle" \| "generating" \| "offline"` | `idle` | `generating`: a response is streaming. The field stays editable — the operator can draft the next prompt — and the send button becomes stop. `offline`: sending is impossible. The draft is kept, and the reason is said. |
| `submitOn` | `"enter" \| "mod-enter"` | `enter` | `enter`: Enter sends, Shift+Enter is a newline — chat convention. `mod-enter`: ⌘/Ctrl+Enter sends, Enter is a newline — for long prompts, where an accidental send costs a model run. |
| `toolbar` | `ReactNode` |  | Leading controls in the footer — a model picker, a tool toggle. |
| `value` | `string` |  | The draft, when the page owns it — to restore a draft or clear it from outside. Pair with `onValueChange`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<form data-slot="prompt-input">`.

Types: `AttachmentChipMessages`, `PromptInputMessages`, `AttachmentChipProps`, `PromptInputProps`

Used in: Agent console

### Reasoning

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-reasoning--overview

The model’s thinking behind a disclosure, summarised as “Thought for 12.4s” and set a step quieter than the answer.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Keeping reasoning available as evidence, for the moment the answer looks wrong.
- Showing that the model is still thinking, with the working texture and a live clock.

When not to:
- The answer itself. Use Message.
- The steps an agent took, such as tool calls and handoffs. Use Agent run.
- Doubt about one claim, which belongs next to the claim. Use UncertaintyNote.

- Do: Leave reasoning closed, so the answer is what is read first.
- Don’t: Open reasoning by default above every answer; when the answer is right, it is noise.

Content:
- Pass startedAt and endedAt, or durationMs, so the summary states a time rather than the bare word “Reasoning”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the summary line.
- Enter or Space: Opens or closes the reasoning.

Mola handles:
- The summary is a button with `aria-expanded`, named by its own words: “Thinking” or “Thought for 12.4s”.
- Opened while the model thinks, the text is `aria-busy` and says “Reasoning complete.” once when it ends.
- The working texture is hidden from screen readers; the word “Thinking” says the same thing.

You handle:
- Pass `startedAt` and `endedAt`, or `durationMs`, so the summary says how long the model thought.

#### `Reasoning` (component)

The model's thinking, behind a disclosure.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `text` (required) | `string` |  | The reasoning text so far. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | Start with the reasoning shown. Closed by default, because reasoning is evidence for when the answer looks wrong, not the answer. |
| `durationMs` | `number` |  | A known duration, when there are no timestamps. |
| `endedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When the thinking ended. Freezes the clock; omit while the model is still thinking. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ReasoningMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the summary line and the announcement when it finishes. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `startedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When the thinking started. Gives the summary line its clock: live while streaming, "Thought for 12.4s" once done. |
| `status` | `"streaming" \| "done"` | `done` | `streaming` while the model is still thinking. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="reasoning">`.

Types: `ReasoningMessages`, `ReasoningProps`

Used in: Agent console

### Run error

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-run-error--overview

Why a run stopped: the step, a plain sentence, the error verbatim, and the ways back in order of cost.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A run failed, was rate limited, timed out or was cancelled, and the operator has to decide what to do next.
- Offering Retry from step N, Retry run and Copy error details next to the reason.
- Keeping output that arrived before the stop, marked as partial, with PartialOutput.

When not to:
- A failed run shown with its steps; pass failure to the run and it places this panel itself. Use Agent run.
- One tool call that failed; the call shows its own error and Retry call. Use Tool call.
- A condition of a page or region that is not a run failure, such as a stale index. Use Callout.
- A panel that could not load its data. Use Empty state.

- Do: Choose the kind that matches the next move: failed, rate_limited, timed_out or cancelled.
- Don’t: Report a rate limit or a person’s cancellation as a plain failure; what the operator does next differs.
- Do: Pass the step’s number and title, so the headline matches the timeline.
- Don’t: Show “Something went wrong” with no step, no sentence and no literal.

Content:
- A custom message says what happened and what was kept: “The query was denied, so the run stopped. Steps 1–3 are kept.”
- Pass the error exactly as the tool returned it; do not reword, shorten or translate it.
- A cancellation is stated without alarm, in the past tense: “You stopped the run.”

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves to Retry from step, Retry run and Copy error details, in that order.
- Enter or Space: Activates the focused button.

Mola handles:
- The panel is a group named by its headline, and a failure, rate limit or timeout is an alert; a cancellation is not.
- A countdown is announced only with the error, at ten seconds and at zero, never every second.
- While a retry must wait, the buttons are disabled and a sentence says until when; Retry from step is described by the headline.
- Copy error details confirms the copy once in a status region.

You handle:
- Pass `step` and `stepNumber` so the headline and the retry button say where the run stopped.
- Used on its own rather than inside `AgentRun`, move focus somewhere sensible when a retry removes the panel.

#### `PartialOutput` (component)

Output that stopped arriving before it was finished — kept, and marked.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<PartialOutputMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the label and the default note. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `note` | `ReactNode` |  | What cut it short, in words. Defaults to a plain statement. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="partial-output">`.

#### `RunError` (component)

What stopped a run, said plainly, with the way back next to it.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `at` | `number \| Date` |  | When it stopped, for the copied details. |
| `autoRetry` | `boolean` | `false` | At `retryAt`, retry by itself instead of re-enabling the buttons. |
| `copyable` | `boolean` | `kind !== 'cancelled'` | Offer *Copy error details*. On by default except for a cancellation, which is not an error. |
| `error` | `string` |  | The error, verbatim. |
| `kind` | `RunErrorKind` | `failed` | Why the run stopped, which sets the headline, the plain sentence and the glyph. Defaults to `failed`; `cancelled` is not an error, so it offers no copy action. |
| `message` | `ReactNode` |  | Replaces the plain sentence under the headline. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<RunErrorMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the status words, the sentences, the countdown, the buttons and the copied details. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onRetry` | `(() => void)` |  | Runs the whole run again from the start. Shows *Retry run*, disabled until `retryAt` passes. |
| `onRetryFromStep` | `(() => void)` |  | Re-runs from the step that stopped, keeping the work before it, so it takes the stronger button when both retries are offered. Disabled until `retryAt` passes. |
| `retryAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When a retry is allowed (or will happen). Shows a countdown. |
| `runId` | `string` |  | The run id, for the copied details. |
| `step` | `string` |  | The step that stopped, in words — "Break the result down by plan". |
| `stepCount` | `number` |  | How many steps the run has, so the position reads "step 4 of 5". Only used with `stepNumber`. |
| `stepNumber` | `number` |  | The step's position, counted from 1. Shown as "step 4 of 5" and used to name *Retry from step 4*. |
| `tool` | `string` |  | The tool that failed, exactly as the model called it. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="run-error">`.

#### `formatRunErrorDetails` (function)

The plain-text block *Copy error details* puts on the clipboard: everything a ticket needs, one fact per line. In English unless `messages` (a full RunError table, `defaultMessages.runError` with your keys) says otherwise: this is a plain function, so it cannot read `MessagesProvider`.

#### `formatCountdown` (function)

`24s`, `1m 05s`. Whole seconds, rounded up, because a countdown that says "0s" while there is still half a second to wait is a promise broken in front of the reader. Zero-padded past a minute so it ticks in place.

#### `useCountdown` (hook)

Milliseconds left until `until`, ticking while there is time left and stopping at zero. `undefined` without a target.

Types: `PartialOutputMessages`, `RunErrorMessages`, `PartialOutputProps`, `RunErrorKind`, `RunErrorProps`

Changes:
- 0.3.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add change approval, citation cards and run recovery [#13](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/13)

### Run status

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-run-status--overview

The one status vocabulary for a run, a step and a tool call: a glyph, a word and a colour, with an elapsed clock.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- The state of any unit of model work: queued, running, streaming, needs approval, succeeded, failed, cancelled or timed out.
- Elapsed time that keeps counting for open work, or a fixed duration for finished work.
- Status in a header, in the label register, or in a row or sentence with register set to inline.

When not to:
- A status that is not model work, such as a document tag or a deploy state. Use Badge.
- Work with a known total that can honestly show how much is done. Use Progress.
- A whole run with its steps. Use Agent run.

- Do: Use label to make a state more specific, such as “Thinking” for a reasoning step; the glyph stays.
- Don’t: Invent a ninth status such as “Rate limited”; it is a reason on a failed run, said in words.

Content:
- Keep the status words as they are: “Succeeded”, “Failed”, “Needs approval”, “Timed out”.
- Use the same words everywhere on the page; a run is not “Errored” in a table and “Failed” in its header.

Mola handles:
- Every status is a glyph, a word and a colour; the glyphs differ in shape, and the glyph is hidden from screen readers.
- The elapsed clock is plain text, so it does not announce every tick.

You handle:
- Announce a change of status yourself where it matters; `RunStatus` is not a live region.
- Use `label` to change the word, such as “Thinking”, and keep it a word a person would say.

Anatomy:
- Glyph (`[data-part="glyph"]`): A cut square whose fill and mark differ per status, so status survives greyscale.
- Label (`[data-part="label"]`): The status in words. The colour repeats it, never replaces it.
- Duration (`[data-part="duration"]`): Elapsed time, ticking while the run is open, fixed once it ends.

#### `RunStatus` (component)

Glyph + word + colour, always all three. State is never carried by colour alone: a red dot means nothing to one reader in twelve, and "Failed" in running text means the same thing to everyone.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status` (required) | `RunStatusValue` |  | The state shown: it picks the glyph, the word (unless `label` overrides it) and its colour, and whether the clock keeps running. |
| `durationMs` | `number` |  | A known duration, for a finished step that has no timestamps. |
| `endedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When it ended. Freezes the clock; omit while the work is open. |
| `label` | `string` |  | Override the word — e.g. "Thinking" for a running reasoning step. The glyph stays. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<RunStatusMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the word for each status. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `register` | `"inline" \| "label"` | `label` | `label`: the rótulo register, for headers. `inline`: sentence case, for rows and running text. |
| `startedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When the work started. With it, an elapsed clock follows the label. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="run-status">`.

#### `RunStatusGlyph` (component)

`className`, `ref` and the rest of the DOM props go to the glyph's `<span>`.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `status` (required) | `RunStatusValue` |  | Which mark to draw. Each status has its own shape, not only its own hue, so the glyph survives greyscale. Decorative: pair it with the word, as `RunStatus` does. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="run-status-glyph">`.

#### `RUN_STATUS_LABEL` (constant)

#### `isActiveStatus` (function)

Work is still open: the clock is ticking and the operator can cancel.

#### `isRetryableStatus` (function)

A failure the operator can do something about.

#### `isTerminalStatus` (function)

Work is over and will not change again without a retry.

#### `formatDuration` (function)

`0.4s`, `12.4s`, `4m 07s`, `1h 02m`. One decimal under a minute because that is where tenths change a decision; whole units above it because nobody cancels a 4-minute run over a tenth of a second. Seconds are zero-padded past the first minute so a ticking clock never changes width — the figure is set tabular, and a `4m 7s` → `4m 10s` jump would still shift the row.

#### `useElapsed` (hook)

Milliseconds since `startedAt`, ticking while the work is still open.

Types: `RunStatusMessages`, `RunStatusGlyphProps`, `RunStatusProps`, `RunStatusValue`

Used in: Agent hand-off

### Side-by-side diff

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-side-by-side-diff--overview

Two versions of one text in two panes, each change opposite its replacement, falling back to one column when narrow.

Phase: Experimental. Its API may change in a minor release, without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Comparing a prompt, a policy or a config with an agent’s revision of it, line against line.
- You hold both versions rather than a patch: pass before and after and the diff is worked out.
- A reviewer has to read across, comparing a changed line with the one it replaces.

When not to:
- One file among several in a proposed change, with hunks to fold. Use FileDiff.
- A change to a record’s fields rather than to lines of text. Use FieldChanges.
- A single version of code or a payload, with nothing to compare it to. Use CodeBlock.

- Do: Name the panes for what the versions are, such as Live and Proposed.
- Don’t: Leave both panes as Before and After when the reader needs to know which one is running.
- Do: Put it inside a ChangeReview when an agent proposes the change, so the comparison and the decision sit together.
- Don’t: Show a proposed change in a diff with no way to approve or reject it.

Content:
- Title it by what is compared, “System prompt, support triage agent”, not by the action.
- Keep pane labels to one or two words; they name the scroll regions.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves focus to the old pane, then the new one; a focused pane scrolls sideways with the arrow keys.

Mola handles:
- The diff is a section named by its title, and each pane a focusable region named by its label and the title, so a keyboard can scroll it and a screen reader can jump to it.
- Changed lines say “Removed:” or “Added:”, and carry a +/− glyph as well as the wash; line numbers and glyphs are hidden from screen readers and from a copy.
- The blank rows that keep the panes aligned are hidden from screen readers, so each pane reads as its version, in order.
- The panes scroll together and carry the scroll-edge cue while there is more to the side; below 48rem they become one column of wrapped lines.
- Code is pinned left to right, so a right-to-left page does not reorder a line of source.

You handle:
- Give it a `title` that says what is compared, and pane labels that say which version is which.
- Set `headingLevel` so the title fits the page outline.

Anatomy:
- Container (`[data-slot="side-by-side-diff"]`): A keylined section named by its title, and the container the two-pane query measures.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): What is being compared, as a heading.
- Count (`[data-part="count"]`): Lines added and removed: “+2 −1” to the eye, a sentence to a screen reader.
- Pane (`[data-part="pane"]`): One version. Its code scrolls sideways under the scroll-edge cue, in step with the other pane.
- Pane label (`[data-part="pane-label"]`): Which version this is; it names the pane’s scroll region.
- Hunk header (`[data-part="hunk-header"]`): The range and section, held at the start while the pane scrolls.
- Line (`[data-part="line"]`): One line of the version. A change is a wash, a glyph and a hidden word.
- Gutter (`[data-part="gutter"]`): Line number and +/− glyph, held in place while the code scrolls, never copied.
- Blank row (`[data-part="filler"]`): Keeps the panes aligned where one side has no line, in the empty texture, hidden from screen readers.

#### `SideBySideDiff` (component)

Two versions of one text, side by side: the old in one pane, the new in the other, each change opposite its replacement.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` (required) | `ReactNode` |  | What is being compared, as a heading: “System prompt, triage agent”. |
| `after` | `string` |  | The text after the change. |
| `afterLabel` | `string` |  | The new pane's name. Defaults to “After” (`messages.after`). |
| `before` | `string` |  | The text before the change. With `after`, the diff is worked out for you. |
| `beforeLabel` | `string` |  | The old pane's name, shown above it and naming its scroll region. Defaults to “Before” (`messages.before`). |
| `context` | `number` | `3` | Unchanged lines kept around each change when the diff is worked out from `before` and `after`. Defaults to 3. |
| `diff` | `string` |  | Or one unified diff, as `git diff` prints it. Wins over `before` and `after`. |
| `headingLevel` | `3 \| 4 \| 5` | `4` | Heading level for the title. Defaults to 4, under a `ChangeReview` heading. |
| `hunks` | `readonly (DiffHunk \| DiffHunkInput)[]` |  | Or the hunks, structured. Wins over `diff`. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<SideBySideDiffMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the pane names, the empty sentence and the words said before each changed line. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<section data-slot="side-by-side-diff">`.

Types: `SideBySideDiffMessages`, `SideBySideDiffProps`

### Sources

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-sources--overview

Inline citation markers and the numbered list of sources they point to, with a preview card on hover, focus or first tap.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- An answer makes claims drawn from documents, and readers need to check them where they are made.
- Listing what each source is, where it lives, the passage used and when it was read.
- Rendering [n] markers in streamed text, with citationRenderer built from the same array as the list.

When not to:
- Ordinary links in running text that are not evidence for a claim. Use Prose.
- Saying how strongly the sources support the answer. Use Confidence.
- A tool’s raw result, such as the rows a query returned. Use Tool call.

- Do: Build the renderer once from the sources array, so marker 2 always opens source 2.
- Don’t: Number markers and list items separately; one mismatch sends the reader to the wrong document.
- Do: Include the passage the answer used and when it was read.
- Don’t: List only a title and a link; checking one sentence then means opening every document.

Content:
- Use the document’s own title as the source title, not its URL or file name.
- Quote the passage exactly as retrieved; do not paraphrase it.
- Give the domain as people know it: “docs.cayuco.internal”.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves to a citation marker and opens its preview card, or to a source card in the list.
- Enter: Follows the focused marker to its source card, or opens the focused source in a new tab.
- Escape: Closes the preview card.

Mola handles:
- A marker is a link named “Source 2:” and the source’s title, not “2”.
- The preview card opens on keyboard focus as well as hover, and repeats the list card, so missing it loses nothing.
- Links that open a new tab say so to screen readers, and the arrow glyphs are hidden from them.

You handle:
- Pass `source` or `title` to each `Citation`, or the marker is named only by its number.
- Render the `SourceList` on the same page under a heading, so `#source-n` has somewhere to land.

#### `Citation` (component)

An inline citation marker: a small cut chip with the source's number.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `n` (required) | `number` |  | The source's number, matching its place in the `SourceList`. Shown as "[n]"; the link defaults to `#source-n`. |
| `cardSide` | `"bottom" \| "top"` | `top` | Which side of the marker the card opens on. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<SourcesMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the marker's name and the preview card's link and read time. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `render` | `ReactElement<unknown, string \| JSXElementConstructor<any>> \| ((props: DetailedHTMLProps<AnchorHTMLAttributes<HTMLAnchorElement>, HTMLAnchorElement>) => ReactElement<...>)` |  | The element the marker renders as: your router's `<Link />` when `href` points inside the app, or a function returning one. Base UI's render prop, so the marker's classes, name and handlers are merged onto it. |
| `source` | `Source` |  | The source this marker points at. With it, hovering or focusing the marker opens a preview card — title, domain, the passage used — so the claim can be checked without leaving the sentence. |
| `title` | `string` |  | The source's title, for the accessible name. "[2]" alone tells a screen reader nothing. Defaults to `source.title`. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<a data-slot="citation">`.

#### `SourceList` (component)

The sources an answer drew on, as an ordered list whose numbers match the inline citations. Each card says what it is (title), where it lives (domain), what was used (the snippet, in the reading voice — it is quoted prose) and when it was read.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sources` (required) | `readonly Source[]` |  | The sources the answer drew on, in citation order, so each card's number matches its inline marker. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<SourcesMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the read time. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `timeZone` | `string` |  | IANA zone the read times are shown in, such as `"UTC"`. Without it they are the reader's own and appear only after hydration, because the server cannot know the reader's zone. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<ol data-slot="source-list">`.

#### `citationRenderer` (function)

A `renderCitation` for `StreamingText`, built from the same array the `SourceList` renders, so marker [2] opens source 2 and links to its card.

Types: `SourcesMessages`, `CitationProps`, `Source`, `SourceListProps`

Used in: Agent console

Changes:
- 0.3.0 (2026-10-06), feature: add change approval, citation cards and run recovery [#13](https://github.com/demogar/molaui/issues/13)

### Streaming text

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-streaming-text--overview

A model’s answer arriving a token at a time, in the reading voice, without layout shift, and announced once when it ends.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Rendering an assistant’s answer as it streams, with a block caret at the end.
- Paragraphs, lists, code blocks, inline code and [n] citation markers from a model.
- Text the reader may skim, such as reasoning, with tone set to muted.

When not to:
- An answer in Markdown with headings, tables, numbered lists or links. Use Markdown.
- Long-form markup that does not stream, such as rendered documentation. Use Prose.
- Text the system says in its own voice, such as labels and hints. Use Text.
- Saying that work is under way before any text exists, outside a message. Use Run status.

- Do: Append to text as tokens arrive and pass status, so completion is announced once.
- Don’t: Wrap the text in a live region; a screen reader would read every token aloud.
- Do: Pass a stable renderCitation, built once at module scope or with useMemo.
- Don’t: Create the renderer inline on every render; finished blocks then render again on every token.

Content:
- Override doneAnnouncement only with a short, complete sentence: “Summary complete.”

Mola handles:
- The text is `aria-busy` while it streams, so it is not read token by token.
- A polite status says “Response complete.” or “Response stopped with an error.” once, when a running stream ends, and not on mount; inside an `AgentRun` the run announces the outcome instead.
- The caret is hidden from screen readers, and under reduced motion it stops blinking.

You handle:
- Set `doneAnnouncement` when “Response complete.” is not the right words for what finished.
- Name the citations you render through `renderCitation`; `Citation` does this from the source’s title.

#### `Caret` (component)

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<span data-slot="caret">`.

#### `StreamingText` (component)

Text that arrives a token at a time.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `text` (required) | `string` |  | Everything received so far. Grows; never rewritten from the top. |
| `doneAnnouncement` | `string` |  | Spoken once when a stream that was running completes. |
| `errorAnnouncement` | `string` |  | Spoken once when a stream that was running fails. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<StreamingTextMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default done and error announcements. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `renderCitation` | `((n: number) => ReactNode)` |  | How an inline `[n]` marker renders. Without it the marker stays as text. Pass a stable function (module scope or `useCallback`): finished blocks skip re-rendering only while it stays the same. |
| `status` | `StreamStatus` | `done` | Where the stream is. `streaming` shows the caret and sets `aria-busy`; the move from `streaming` to `done` or `error` is announced once. Defaults to `done`. |
| `tone` (deprecated) | `"default" \| "muted"` |  | Deprecated: Since 0.7.0; removed in 1.0.0. Use `variant`, which takes the same values: a muted ink is a presentation, and `tone` is a status. |
| `variant` | `"default" \| "muted"` |  | `muted` for text the reader may skim — reasoning, not the answer. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="streaming-text">`.

#### `parseBlocks` (function)

#### `parseInline` (function)

Types: `StreamingTextMessages`, `CaretProps`, `StreamStatus`, `StreamingTextProps`, `Block`, `InlineRun`

Used in: Agent console

### Token usage

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-token-usage--overview

What a run consumed: input, cached and output tokens, cost, and how full the context window is.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- A run summary that reports tokens and cost, or a run header, with the inline variant.
- Warning before the context window fills: the meter turns gold at 80% and rojo at 95%, and says so.

When not to:
- A workspace budget or a monthly quota. Use Meter.
- A headline figure on a dashboard, such as tokens this month. Use Stat.

- Do: Pass contextWindow whenever it is known, so the meter shows how close the run is to the limit.
- Don’t: Report spend alone; a full context fails quietly and looks like the model forgetting.

Content:
- Pass raw numbers; counts are shown compact, “12.4k”, and cost in US dollars to four decimals, “$0.0731”.

Mola handles:
- The context bar is a `role="meter"` named “Context window used”, with a value text such as “84% of the context window, near the limit”.
- Near and at the limit are said in words as well as in gold and red.
- The full variant is a description list, so each figure is heard with its term.

You handle:
- Say whose usage it is, with a heading or the run it sits in; the component does not.

#### `TokenUsage` (component)

What a run consumed.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `input` (required) | `number` |  | Tokens sent to the model, cached ones included. |
| `output` (required) | `number` |  | Tokens the model produced. With `input`, the total measured against `contextWindow`. |
| `cached` | `number` |  | Input tokens served from cache — cheaper, and worth knowing about. |
| `contextWindow` | `number` |  | The model's context window. With it, a meter shows how full the context is. |
| `costUsd` | `number` |  | Cost in USD, if the platform knows it. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<TokenUsageMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the terms, the warnings and the meter's name and reading. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `variant` | `"full" \| "inline"` | `full` | `full`: a definition list and meter. `inline`: one line, for a run header. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="token-usage">`.

#### `contextLevel` (function)

Types: `TokenUsageMessages`, `TokenUsageProps`

Used in: Agent console

### Tool call

https://demogar.github.io/molaui/?path=/docs/ai-tool-call--overview

One tool invocation: the tool’s literal name, its arguments, the result or error, and an approval when a person must decide.

Phase: Stable. Semantic versioning applies: nothing breaks without a deprecation first.

When to use:
- Showing what the model asked a system to do, with what, and what came back.
- Holding a call that writes, spends or sends until a person approves or denies it.
- Calls the model made at the same time, grouped with ToolCallGroup.

When not to:
- A larger change to files or records that needs a diff and a reason. Use Change review.
- The whole run the calls belong to. Use Agent run.
- A payload shown on its own, outside a call. Use CodeBlock.

- Do: Say in the approval reason what the call changes and whether the agent can undo it.
- Don’t: Ask for approval with no reason, or make Approve the primary button and Deny a link.
- Do: Pass args and result as objects, so they render as formatted JSON.
- Don’t: Rewrite payloads as prose; operators inspect them and paste them into tickets.

Content:
- The title says what the call is for, “Rollout results”; the name stays exactly as the model called it.
- Name approval buttons by the action: “Approve rollout” and “Deny”, not “Yes” and “No”.
- Keep the approval reason to a sentence or two: what changes, for whom, and whether it can be undone.

Keyboard:
- Tab: Moves to the call’s header, then to Approve, Deny, Retry call and the payload’s buttons when open; the header is a stop even while the call is held open.
- Enter or Space: Opens or closes the call from its header, or activates the focused button.
- ArrowUp or ArrowDown or ArrowLeft or ArrowRight: Scrolls a focused arguments or result block.

Mola handles:
- The header is a button with `aria-expanded`, and a failed call or one waiting for approval opens itself; held open, the header stays focusable and is `aria-disabled`, so its name and status are still a stop.
- A failure is an alert with the error verbatim, except inside an `AgentRun` that has already raised it.
- Status is a glyph, a word and a colour, and Approve and Deny are buttons of the same weight.
- `ToolCallGroup` is a group named “N tools in parallel”, or by its `label`.

You handle:
- State in `approval.reason` why a person must approve, in words.
- Pass a `title` in words when the tool’s name alone does not say what the call is for.

Anatomy:
- Container (`[data-slot="tool-call"]`): A keylined card. While it waits for a person it wears the gold band, revealed by the cut.
- Header (`[data-part="header"]`): The disclosure trigger. A waiting or failed call is held open, so its trigger is inert.
- Tool name (`[data-part="name"]`): The identifier exactly as the model called it, as a machine literal.
- Title (`[data-part="title"]`): What the call is for, in words.
- Status (`[data-part="status"]`): A Run status: glyph, word and colour, and the elapsed time when known.
- Body (`[data-part="body"]`): The approval or the error first, then the arguments and the result.
- Approval (`[data-part="approval"]`): Why a person has to decide, and two buttons of equal weight.
- Progress (`[data-part="progress"]`): Working relleno along the top edge while the call runs.
- Error (`[data-part="error"]`): What failed, the error verbatim, and Retry. A failed call is held open.

#### `ToolCall` (component)

One tool invocation: what the model asked a system to do, with what, and what came back.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` (required) | `string` |  | The tool's identifier, exactly as the model called it. |
| `status` (required) | `RunStatusValue` |  | The call's state. `waiting` and `failed` keep the details open, because the decision or the error is the content. |
| `approval` | `ToolCallApproval` |  | Present when `status` is `waiting`: the call is held for a person. |
| `args` | `unknown` |  | The arguments the model sent. Objects are shown as formatted JSON. |
| `defaultOpen` | `boolean` | `false` | Start expanded. Waiting and failed calls are always expanded. |
| `durationMs` | `number` |  | A known duration, for a finished call that has no timestamps. |
| `endedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When the call ended. Freezes the clock; omit while the call is open. |
| `error` | `string` |  | The error, verbatim. |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ToolCallMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the payload labels, the error and approval sentences and their buttons. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |
| `onOpenChange` | `((open: boolean) => void)` |  | Called when the reader opens or closes the details. |
| `onRetry` | `(() => void)` |  | Calls the tool again. Shows Retry beside the error once the call has failed; omit when the run, not the call, owns retrying. |
| `open` | `boolean` |  | Whether the details are expanded, when the page owns that. Waiting and failed calls are always expanded. |
| `result` | `unknown` |  | What came back. Objects as JSON; strings as text. |
| `startedAt` | `number \| Date` |  | When the call started. With it, the header shows a clock that counts while the call is active. |
| `title` | `string` |  | What the call is for, in words — "Query rollout results". Optional; the name is always shown. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tool-call">`.

#### `ToolCallGroup` (component)

Calls the model made at the same time. Drawn as a bracket on the left — one ink rule spanning the group — so "these ran together" is a shape, not a sentence the operator has to find.

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `count` (required) | `number` |  | How many calls ran together. Used for the default label, "N tools in parallel". |
| `label` | `string` |  | Defaults to "N tools in parallel". |
| `messages` | `MessagesOverride<ToolCallGroupMessages>` |  | Words for translation: the default label. Keys left out come from `MessagesProvider`, then English. |

`className`, `ref`, `id` and the other DOM props go to `<div data-slot="tool-call-group">`.

Types: `ToolCallGroupMessages`, `ToolCallMessages`, `ToolCallApproval`, `ToolCallGroupProps`, `ToolCallProps`

Used in: Agent console, Agent hand-off
